<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that for any digraph \(D\), one of the following~holds:
	\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(I\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
		\item \(D\) contains a cylindrical wall of order $k$ as an immersion, or
		\item the directed tree-cutwidth of $D$ is at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{enumerate}
	Moreover, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm (with parameter $k$) to find either an immersion model of a cylindrical wall of order $k$ in $D$, or a directed tree-cut decomposition of $D$ of width at most~\(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that for any positive integer \(k\),
	every digraph containing a cylindrical wall of order \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\) as an immersion has directed tree-cutwidth\xspace at least \(k\).
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{lemma:potato}
	There exists a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi \colon \mathds{N} \to \mathds{N}\) such that for every positive integer~$k$, every digraph \(D\) has a $k$-flow-connected\xspace tree-partition of porosity\xspace at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	
	Moreover, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm that finds, for any digraph $D$ and parameter $k$, a $k$-flow-connected\xspace tree-partition of porosity at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:menger}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph, \(s,t\) two vertices in \(D\) and \(k \in \mathds{N}\).
	Then either \(D\) contains an \(s\)-\(t\)-flow of order \(k\) or a set \(F\) of less than \(k\) edges such that \(D-F\) does not contain an \(s\)-\(t\)-path.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:setFlow}
	Let \(Z\) be a \(d\)-flow-connected set in a digraph \(D\).
	Then for every \(z \in Z\) and \(X \subseteq Z\) there is a \(z\)-\(X\)-flow of order \(d\).
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:cycleDeg-hitting}
	Let \(v\) be a vertex of a digraph \(D\).
	Then the cycle-degree of \(v\) is equal to the minimum size of a set of edges \(F\) such that \(F\) hits every cycle containing an edge adjacent to \(v\).
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:BipRamsey}
	There is a function \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}\colon \mathds{N}\times\mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that the following holds:
	For every bipartite graph \(G\) with both sides, say \(A\) and \(B\), of size at least \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(a,b)\), there exist subsets $A' \subseteq A$ and $B' \subseteq B$ of sizes $a$, and $b$ respectively, that are either complete or anti-complete to each other.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:ColourRamsey}
	For every \(k \in \mathds{N}\) there exists a function \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{col}}\colon \mathds{N}^k \to \mathds{N}\) such that the following holds:
	For every complete graph \(G\) on at least  \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{col}}(x_1,\dots,x_k)\) vertices, whose edges are coloured with \(k\) colours, say \(c_1,\dots,c_k\), there exists \(i \in [k]\) and a set \(K\) of \(x_i\) vertices of \(G\) such that all the edges with both endpoints in \(K\) have colour \(c_i\).
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:gridImm_from_onion_star}
	Let \(H\) be a digraph with \(t\) vertices in which every vertex has total degree at most \(3\) and both in-degree and out-degree at most \(2\).
	Then every digraph containing the onion-star of order \(t\) as an immersion also contains \(H\) as an immersion.
	In particular, if a digraph \(D\) contains an onion-star of order $2k^2$ as an immersion, then \(D\) also contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as an immersion.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:tripod-EP}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that for every migration digraph \(D\) and \(k\in \mathds{N}\)~either
	\begin{itemize}
		\item there is a family of \(k\) edge-disjoint \(S\)-\(T\)-in-tripods (resp.~out-tripods) in \(D\), or
		\item there is a set $X$ consisting of at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\) edges such that \(D-X\) does not contain any \(S\)-\(T\)-in-tripod (resp.~out-tripod).
	\end{itemize}
	Moreover, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm to find, for any digraph \(D\) and parameter $k$, either of these objects in $D$.
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:treept}
	Given a digraph $D$, a triple $(T,\beta,\gamma)$ is a {\em{tree-partition}} of $D$ if $T$ is a tree and we have the following:
	\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(P\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
		\item \(\beta \colon 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi \to 2^{
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi}\) maps every node $x$ of \(T\) to a subset of vertices \(\beta(x)\subseteq 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\), called the \emph{bag} of $x$, so that $\{\beta(x)\colon x\in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\}$ is a near-partition of \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\).\label{item:tp:bags}
		\item \(\gamma \colon 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi \to 2^{A(D)}\) maps every edge~$e=xy$ of \(T\) to a set of edges \(\gamma(e)\subseteq A(D)\), called the \emph{edge-guard} of~$e$, so that \(\gamma(e)\) hits all the cycles in $D$ that cross the cut induced by $e$; i.e.,  $(\beta({T}^{x}_{e}),\beta({T}^{y}_{e}))$.\label{item:tp:edgeguards}
	\end{enumerate}
	The \emph{porosity} of $(T,\beta,\gamma)$ is the maximum size of an edge-guard, that is, $\max_{e\in E(T)} |\gamma(e)|$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:rooting_potdecomps}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph and let \((T, \beta, \gamma)\) be a rooted tree-partition of \(D\).
	Then, there exists a coherent tree-partition \((T', \beta', \gamma')\) of \(D\) such that:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item for each node \(x' \in V(T')\) there exists a node \(x \in V(T)\) such that \(\beta'(x') \subseteq \beta(x)\); and
		\item for each edge \(e' \in E(T')\) there exists an edge \(e \in E(T)\) such that \(\gamma'(e') = \gamma(e)\);
		                                                                                                  
		                                                                           
		                                                
	\end{itemize}
	Moreover, $(T',\beta',\gamma')$ can be found in time polynomial in the size of $D$ and $T$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
For every node $x \in V(T)$ denote by $h(x)$ the maximum number of nodes on a path in $T$ leading from $x$ to a leaf.
	                                                      
	Let $r$ be the root of $T$, and \(\mathcal{T}_0 \coloneqq (T, \beta, \gamma)\).
	For every $i \in [h(r)-1]$ let \(T^{\text{rest}}_i\) be the subtree of $T$ induced by all vertices for which $h(x) >i$.
	We will define a sequence \(\mathcal{T}_1,\mathcal{T}_2,\mathcal{T}_3,\ldots,\mathcal{T}_{h(r)-1}\) of rooted tree-partitions of~\(D\), where \(\mathcal{T}_i = (T_i, \beta_i, \gamma_i)\),
	satisfying the following conditions for each $i$:
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item $V(T^{\text{rest}}_i) \subseteq V(T_{i})$, in particular $r \in V(T_i)$; moreover, $r$ is a root of $T_i$; 
		\item for each node \(x' \in V(T_i)\) there exists a node \(x \in V(T)\) such that \(\beta_i(x') \subseteq \beta(x)\);
		\item for each edge \(e' \in E(T_i)\) there exists an edge \(e \in E(T)\) such that \(\gamma_i(e') = \gamma(e)\); and
		\item for each node \(x \in V(T_i) - V(T^{\text{rest}}_i)\), the set
		\(\bigcup \Set{\beta_i(y) \colon y \text{ is a descendant of } x\text{ in }T_i}\)
		induces a strongly connected subgraph of \(D\);
	\end{enumerate}
	Suppose we have already defined \(\mathcal{T}_i = (T_i, \beta_i, \gamma_i)\).
	Let \(X\subseteq V(T^{\text{rest}}_i)\) be the set of all nodes $x$ for which \(h(x)=i + 1\).
	If \(X = \Set{r}\) the construction terminates with \((T',\beta',\gamma')\coloneqq ( T_i, \beta_i, \gamma_i)\).
	Otherwise, every node in \(X\) has a parent.
	For \(x \in X\), let \(p(x)\) be the parent of \(x\) in \(T_i\).
	As \(h(p(x)) > h(x)\) by definition of $h$, the node \(p(x)\) does not belong to \(X\).
	Let \(S_x \coloneqq \bigcup \Set{\beta_i(y) \colon y \text{ is a descendant of } x \text{ in }T_i}\).
	Since the nodes of \(X\) are pairwise incomparable in the ancestor-descendant relation of $T_i$,
	the sets \(S_x\) for $x\in X$ are pairwise disjoint.
	
	We now proceed to define \(T_{i + 1}\).
	For each $x\in X$, let \(C^x_1, C^x_2, \dots, C^x_{\ell(x)}\) be the strongly connected components of \(D[S_x]\).
	                                                                                       
	                                                  
	                                                                           
	Note that the tree $T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1}$ is induced by the vertices $V(T^{\text{rest}}_i) - X$, and is not empty since it includes at least the root \(r\), as well as all the parents of the nodes from \(X\).
	The tree \(T_{i + 1}\) will have \(r\) as its root and as its nodes the set
	\(V(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1}) \cup \bigcup _ {x \in X} V({T}^{\downarrow}_{x}) \times [\ell(x)]\).
	The edges of \(T_{i + 1}\) are defined as follows:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item \(V(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1})\) induces a tree isomorphic to \(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1}\);
		\item \(V({T}^{\downarrow}_{x}) \times \Set{j}\), for each $x\in X$ and \(j \in [\ell(x)]\), induces a subtree
		isomorphic to \({T}^{\downarrow}_{x}\); and
		\item the node \((x, j)\), for each $x\in X$ and \(j \in [\ell(x)]\), has \(p(x) \in V(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1})\) as its parent.
	\end{itemize}
	This gives us the tree \(T_{i + 1}\).
	Next, we define the bag function \(\beta_{i + 1}\) as follows (see \cref{fig:potdecomp_rooting_step}):
	\begin{equation*}
		\beta_{i + 1}(z) = \begin{cases}
			\beta_i(z), & \text{ if } z \in V(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1})\\
			\beta_i(z') \cap V(C^x_j), & \text{ if } z = (z', j) \text{ where } z' \in V({T}^{\downarrow}_{x}), x \in X, \text{ and } j \in [\ell(x)]\text{.}
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	Finally, we complete the construction with the definition of the edge-guard function \(\gamma_{i + 1}\):
	\begin{equation*}
		\gamma_{i + 1}(a,b) = \begin{cases}
			\gamma_i(a, b), & \text{ if } a,b \in V(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1}),\\
			\gamma_i(p(x),x), & \text{ if } a = p(x), b=(x, j) \text{ where } x \in X, j \in [\ell(x)],\\
			\gamma_i(a', b'), & \text{ if } a = (a', j), b = (b', j) \text{ where } a', b' \in V({T}^{\downarrow}_{x}), x \in X, j \in [\ell(x)]\text{.}
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	\begin{figure}
		\centering
		                                                         
		\begin{tikzpicture}
			\path[use as bounding box] (-5,-5) rectangle (5,2);
			\tikzstyle{child} = [draw,thick,isosceles triangle,rotate=90,scale=0.8]
			\tikzstyle{help} = [draw,thick,rectangle]
			\def2{2}
			\node (left) at (-4,0) {
				\begin{tikzpicture}
					\path[use as bounding box] (-2,-2) rectangle (2,2);
					\node[draw,thick,circle] (vertex) at (0,0) {};
					\node (parent) at ($(vertex)+(110:1.2)$) {};
					\node (parent-p) at ($(parent)+(110:0.3)$) {};
					\node[child] (child-1) at ($(vertex)+(-2.2,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_1\)}};
					\node[child] (child-2) at ($(vertex)+(0,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_2\)}};
					\node[child] (child-3) at ($(vertex)+(2.2,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_3\)}};
					
					\node (child-1-text) at ($(child-1)$) {\(T_1\)};
					\node (child-2-text) at ($(child-2)$) {\(T_2\)};
					\node (child-3-text) at ($(child-3)$) {\(T_3\)};
					
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-1.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-2.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-3.east);
					
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (parent);
					\draw[edge,dotted] (parent-p.center) to (parent.center);
					
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-1)+(270:1.4)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)+(180:1)$) {};
							                                                                                             
							                                                                                              
							\draw[directededge,PastelOrange] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);                                                                              
							
							
							                                                            
							\node (cyc-2) at ($(centre)+(60:1)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,LavenderMagenta] ($(cyc-2)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
							\node (cyc-3) at ($(centre)+(-60:1)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,CornflowerBlue] ($(cyc-3)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
							
							\draw[directededge,shorten >=4] (cyc-1) to (cyc-2);
							\draw[directededge,shorten <=5,shorten >=5] (cyc-2) to (cyc-3);
							\draw[directededge,shorten <=3,shorten >=4] (cyc-1) to (cyc-3);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-2)+(270:1.4)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)+(180:1)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,PastelOrange] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
							
							\node (cyc-2) at ($(centre)+(0:1)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,LavenderMagenta] ($(cyc-2)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
							
							\draw[directededge,shorten >=5,shorten <=1] (cyc-1) to (cyc-2);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-3)+(270:1.4)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,CornflowerBlue] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
			\end{tikzpicture}};
			
			\node (right) at (4,0) {\begin{tikzpicture}
					\path[use as bounding box] (-2,-2) rectangle (2,2);
					\node[draw,thick,circle] (vertex) at (0,0) {};
					\node (parent) at ($(vertex)+(110:1.2)$) {};
					\node (parent-p) at ($(parent)+(110:0.3)$) {};
					\node[child] (child-1) at ($(vertex)+(-3,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_1\)}};
					\node[child] (child-11) at ($(vertex)+(-1.8,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_1\)}};
					\node[child] (child-111) at ($(vertex)+(-0.6,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_1\)}};
					\node[child] (child-2) at ($(vertex)+(0.6,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_2\)}};
					\node[child] (child-22) at ($(vertex)+(1.8,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_2\)}};
					\node[child] (child-3) at ($(vertex)+(3,-2)$) {\phantom{\(T_3\)}};
					
					\node (child-1-text) at ($(child-1)$) {\(T_1\)};
					\node (child-11-text) at ($(child-11)$) {\(T_1\)};
					\node (child-111-text) at ($(child-111)$) {\(T_1\)};
					\node (child-2-text) at ($(child-2)$) {\(T_2\)};
					\node (child-22-text) at ($(child-22)$) {\(T_2\)};
					\node (child-3-text) at ($(child-3)$) {\(T_3\)};
					
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-1.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-11.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-111.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-2.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-22.east);
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (child-3.east);
					
					\draw[edge] (vertex) to (parent);
					\draw[edge,dotted] (parent-p.center) to (parent.center);
					
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-1)+(270:1)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,PastelOrange] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-11)+(270:1)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,LavenderMagenta] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-111)+(270:1)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,CornflowerBlue] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-2)+(270:1)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,PastelOrange] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-22)+(270:1)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,LavenderMagenta] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
					
					\node[blue!60!white,draw,rectangle,rounded corners] at ($(child-3)+(270:1)$) {
						\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.7]
							\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
							
							\node[scale=2,thick] (cyc-1) at ($(centre)$) {};
							\draw[directededge,CornflowerBlue] ($(cyc-1)+(0:0.4)$) arc (0:355:0.4);
					\end{tikzpicture}};
			\end{tikzpicture}};
			
			\node[scale=2] at ($(left)!0.45!(right)$) {\(\Rightarrow\)};
		\end{tikzpicture}
		
		\caption{
			Illustration depicting local modification of the tree-partition decomposition\xspace \(\mathcal{T}_i\).
			Blue blocks below each subtree depict the partition of the subgraph decomposed by each subtree into strongly connected components.
		}
		\label{fig:potdecomp_rooting_step}
	\end{figure}
	
	We now verify the postulated properties of \(\mathcal{T}_{i+1}\coloneqq (T_{i+1},\beta_{i+1},\gamma_{i+1})\).
	That properties 1--3 hold immediately from the construction and from the assumption that they hold for \(\mathcal{T}_i\).
	Property~4 holds by construction for the nodes
	of height \(i + 1\), while for the nodes of height \(i\) or less, it follows by the assumption about \(\mathcal{T}_i\)
	and the observation that the strongly connected components we split by at height \(i + 1\) cannot ever split
	a strongly connected component induced by the bags of a subtree deeper down.
	
	Finally, we need to verify that $\mathcal{T}_{i+1}$ is indeed a rooted tree-partition of $D$.
	For~\cref{item:tp:bags}, this follows trivially from the construction and the assumption that
	\((T, \beta, \gamma)\) is a rooted tree-partition of~$D$.
	For~\cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, observe that every edge \(e\) of \(T_{i + 1}\) induces either a cut
	already present in \(T_i\) (with the same guard set), or a cut of the form
	\((A \cap C^x_j, B \cup (A - C^x_j))\) where \((A,B)\) is the cut induced in $\mathcal{T}_i$ by an edge \(e'\) incident to \(V(T_x)\), for some $x\in X$.
	But noting
	that \(C^x_j\) is a strongly connected component of a superset of \(A\), every cycle that contains
	a vertex in \(A \cap C^x_j\) and a vertex outside of \(A \cap C^x_j\) must necessarily contain
	a vertex in \(A\) and a vertex outside of \(A\).
	Hence, the cycle must be hit by the guard set \(\gamma_i(e') = \gamma_{i + 1}(e)\).
	This concludes the verification of the properties of $\mathcal{T}_{i+1}$.
	
	As a last step, to ensure that we do not introduce too many empty nodes in $T_{i+1}$, if $x \in V(T_{i+1}) -  V(T^{\text{rest}}_{i+1})$ is such that $\beta_{i+1}(x)=\emptyset$, we exhaustively do the following:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(A\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
	\item if $x$ is a leaf of $T_{i+1}$, we delete $x$;
	\item if $x$ has precisely two neighbours $y$ and $z$, we delete $x$, add edge $yz$ and set $\gamma_{i+1}(yz)=\gamma_{i+1}(xy)$;
\end{enumerate}	
	Note that these modifications preserve properties 1--3 and the fact that $\mathcal{T}_{i+1}$ is a tree-partition rooted in~$r$.
	It is straightforward to verify that property 4 is also preserved, as since $x \neq r$, one of the neighbours of $x$ must be a parent of $x$.
	
    The construction finishes after at most $h(r)$ rounds, yielding a coherent tree-partition $\mathcal{T}'$.
 	Moreover, constructing $\mathcal{T}'$ takes time polynomial in $|E(D)|$ and $|V(T)|$; in particular, by (A1)-(A2), each of the nodes of $T'$ corresponding to empty bag is of degree at least 3, thus the size of $T'$ is polynomial in $|V(D)|$ (and thus in $|E(D)|$).
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{def:linked}
	We say that a tree-partition $(T,\beta,\gamma)$ of a digraph $D$ is \emph{$k$-flow-connected\xspace} if for every node $x$ of $T$, the set $\beta(x)$ is $k$-flow-connected in $D$.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{def:dtcw}
	Given a digraph \(D\), a quadruple \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) is a \emph{directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace} of \(D\) if
	\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(D\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
		\item \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma )}{( T,\beta,\gamma )}{( T,\beta,\gamma )}\) is a tree-partition of $D$, and \label{item:dtcw:tp}
		\item \(\theta \colon 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi \to 2^{A(D)}\) maps every node $x$ of \(T\) to a set of edges in \(\theta(x)\subseteq A(D)\), called the \emph{onion-guard} of \(x\), so that $\theta(x)$ hits all the out-onions of $x$ or all the in-onions of \(x\).
		\label{item:dtcw:onionguards}
	\end{enumerate}
	
	The \emph{width} of \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) is defined as
	\begin{equation*}
		\max\left\{\max_{v \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi} \Abs{\beta(v)}, \max_{e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi} \Abs{\gamma(e)}, \max_{v \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi} \Abs{\theta(v)}\right\},
	\end{equation*}
	while the \emph{directed tree-cutwidth\xspace} of \(D\) is defined as the minimum width over all possible directed tree-cut-decompositions\xspace of \(D\).
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:grid_imm_in_torsos}
	Let \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma )}{( T,\beta,\gamma )}{( T,\beta,\gamma )}\) be a rooted tree-partition of a digraph \(D\), and let \(x \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\).
Suppose that for every child $y$ of $x$ the set $\bigcup \Set{\beta(z) \ \colon z \text{ is a descendant of } y}\) induces a strongly connected subgraph of \(D\).
	If \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{2k}\) as an immersion, then \(D\) contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as an immersion.
	
	Moreover, having an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{2k}\) in \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\), we can find an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) in~$D$ in time polynomial in size of $D$.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $\mu'$ be an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{2k}\) in \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\).
	Let $C'_1=C_1$, and for every $i \in \{2,\ldots,k\}$ we define the simple cycle $C'_i$ in \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{2k}\) as
	\[p^+_{1,2i-2} \xrightarrow{P_1} p^+_{1,2i-1}\xrightarrow{C_{2i-1}}q^+_{1,2i-1}\xrightarrow{Q_1}q^+_{1,2i-2}\xrightarrow{C_{2i-2}} p^+_{2,2i-2} \xrightarrow{P_2} p^+_{2,2i-1} \ldots q^+_{2k,2i-2} \xrightarrow{C_{2i-2}} p^+_{1,{2i-2}}.\]
	                                                                                                                                                                     
	
	Consider a subgraph $W$ of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{2k}\) obtained by taking the union of all edge and vertex sets of the elements of $\bigcup\{P_{2i-1},Q_{2i-1},C'_{i} \ \colon \ i \in [k]\}.$ 
	Define 
	\begin{align*}
	&V^-:=\{p^-_{2i-1,2j-1},q^-_{2i-1,2j} \ \colon \ i \in [k],j \in [k-1]\} \\ 
	&V^+:=\{p^+_{2i-1,2j},q^+_{2i-1,2j-1} \ \colon \ i \in [k],j \in [k-1]\}.
	\end{align*}
	Clearly, $W$ contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as an immersion, which in turn gives rise to an immersion model $\mu$ of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) in $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$ where the image of the vertex set of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) is $\mu'\left(V^+ \cup V^-\right).$ We treat $V^+ \cup V^-$ also as the set of vertices of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\).
	
	Note that for every $v \in V^+$, no out-neighbour $w$ of $v$ in \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{2k}\) belongs to $V^-$, as for every $(i,j) \in [k] \times [k-1]$ the out-neighbour of $p^+_{2i-1,2j}$ is $p^-_{2i-1,2j}$, and the out-neighbour of $q^+_{2i-1,2j-1}$ is $p^-_{2i,2j-1}.$
	Thus, if $w$ is an out-neighbour of $v$ in \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\), the trail $\mu(v,w)$ is obtained by concatenating more than one trail given by $\mu'$, implying that $\mu(v,w)$ has an internal vertex.
  

	If $x$ is a leaf of $T$, $\mu$ is a model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) in $D$.
	Thus, we can assume that $y_1,\ldots,y_\ell$ are the children of $x$.
	Fix $i \in [\ell]$.
	Since the out-degree of the auxiliary vertex $a_i$ and the in-degree of the auxiliary vertex $b_i$ are equal to 1, if there exists a vertex $v$ in $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$ such that $\mu(v)=a_i$ (resp.~$\mu(v)=b_i$), then $v \in V^+$ (resp.~$v \in V^-$).
	Thus, there is no edge $(v,w)$ of $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$ such that $\mu(v)=a_i$ and $\mu(w)=b_i$, as in that case $\mu(v,w)=(a_ib_i)$ would not have an internal vertex.
	Having established that property, we can modify $\mu$ to be an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) in $D$ in a rather straightforward way.
	
	We assume first that there is an edge $(v,w)$ in $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$ such that $\mu(v,w)$ uses the edge $a_ib_i$ but $\mu(v)\neq a_i$ and $\mu(w)\neq b_i$.
	Let $e$ and $f$ be the edges of \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) that, respectively
	                                                                                
	 directly precede and succeed $a_ib_i$ in $\mu(v,w)$.
	Since $\bigcup\{\beta(z) \ \colon \ z \textrm{ is a descendant of } y_i\}$ induces a strongly connected subgraph $D'$ of $D$, $D'$ contains a $
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e)\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e) )}}
                     
	\fi$-$
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f)\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}
                     
	\fi$ path $(r_0,\ldots,r_m)$.
	We replace the subsequence $(e,a_ib_i,f)$ of $\mu(x,y)$ by $(\mathsf{origin}_{x}(e),r_0,\ldots,r_m,\mathsf{origin}_{x}(f))$.
	
	Thus, we consider the case in which $\mu(v,w)$ uses the edge $a_ib_i$ and $\mu(v)= a_i$ (the case $\mu(w)= b_i$ is symmetric).
	As we observed, in this case $v \in V^+$ and $\mu(w) \neq b_i$.
	Let $u_1,u_2 \in V^-$ be such that $(u_1,v),(u_2,v)$ are the edges of $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$.	Let $e_1,e_2 \in E(\mathsf{tor}(D,x))$ be, respectively, last elements of $\mu(u_1,v)$ and $\mu(u_2,v)$, and let $f$ be the second (following $a_ib_i$) element of $\mu(v,w)$.
	Again, if $D'$ is a strongly connected subgraph $D$ induced by the sum of bags in the subtree rooted in $y_i$, $D'$ must contain an $
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1)\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1) )}}
                     
	\fi$-$
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f)\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(f) )}}
                     
	\fi$ path $R$, and a $
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2)\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}
                     
	\fi$-$r$ path $S$ for some vertex $r$ that lays on $R$ (it may happen that $r=
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2)\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2) )}}
                     
	\fi$ and $S$ is trivial).
	We set $\mu(v)$ to be $r$, replace $e_1$ and $e_2$ in the trails by, respectively, $\mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_1)$ followed by a prefix of $R$ that ends in $r$, and by $\mathsf{origin}_{x}(e_2)$ followed by $S$.
	Similarly, we replace $(a_ib_i,f)$ in $\mu(v,w)$ by the suffix of $R$ that starts in $r$, followed by $\mathsf{origin}_{x}(f)$.
	
	Since for different $i,i' \in [\ell]$ the bags of descendants of $i$ and of $i'$ are disjoint, and $\mathsf{origin}_{x}$ is a bijection, we never use the same edge of $D$ twice in the replacing procedure.
	Thus, after replacing in $\mu$ every vertex that is initially mapped to an auxiliary vertex, and every edge of $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$ that does not belong to $D$, we get an immersion model $\mu$ of $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$ in $D$.
	
	Last, we note that all these modifications can be performed in time polynomial in the size of $D$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{def:dtw}
	A \emph{directed tree-decomposition} of a digraph \(D\) is a triple \(\mathchoice{\left( T, \beta, \gamma \right)}{( T, \beta, \gamma )}{( T, \beta, \gamma )}{( T, \beta, \gamma )}\), where \(T\) is a rooted tree, and \(\beta\colon V(T) \to 2^{V(D)}\) and \(\gamma\colon E(T) \to 2^{V(D)}\) are functions mapping every node $x$ of \(T\) to its \emph{bag} $\beta(x)\subseteq 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi$, and every edge $e$ of \(T\) to its \emph{guard} \(\gamma(e)\subseteq D\), so that the following conditions are satisfied.
	\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(T\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
		\item \label{item:directed-tree-width:beta}
		\(\Set{\beta(x) \colon x \in V(T)}\) is a near-partition of \(V(D)\); and
		\item \label{item:directed-tree-width:guard}
		for every \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\), there is no cycle in \(D - \gamma(e)\) visiting both \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{e})\) and~\(\beta({T}^{\uparrow}_{e})\).
	\end{enumerate}
	For \(x \in V(T)\) we define \(\Gamma(x) \coloneqq \beta(x) \cup \bigcup \set{\gamma(e) : e \sim x}\).
	The \emph{width} of \(\mathchoice{\left( T, \beta, \gamma \right)}{( T, \beta, \gamma )}{( T, \beta, \gamma )}{( T, \beta, \gamma )}\) is the least integer \(w\) such that \(\Abs{\Gamma(t)} \leqslant w + 1\) for all \(t \in V(T)\).
	The \emph{directed treewidth\xspace} of \(D\) is the smallest width of any directed tree-decomposition of \(D\).
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{thm:gridtheorem}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that for every digraph $D$, one of the following conditions holds:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item \(D\) contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as a topological minor; or
		\item \(D\) admits a directed tree-decomposition of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- theorem-9: body_md -->
\label{thm:grid-algorithmic}
There is a fixed-parameter algorithm to find, for any digraph \(D\) and parameter~$k$, either
\begin{itemize}
\item a topological minor model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) in $D$; or
\item a directed tree-cut decomposition of $D$ of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-9 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{def:dbw}
	A \emph{directed branch-decomposition} of a digraph \(D\) is a tuple \((T,\lambda)\) where \(T\) is a subcubic tree and \(\lambda\) is a bijection between the vertices of \(D\) and the leaves of \(T\).
	
	Now define \(\gamma\colon E(T)\to 2^{V(D)}\) mapping every edge \(e=xy\) of \(T\) to a minimum vertex set \(\gamma(e) \subseteq 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\), which we call the \emph{vertex-guard}, so that the following condition is satisfied: \(D-\gamma(e)\) does not contain any cycle containing both a vertex of \(\lambda({T}^{x}_{e})\) and a vertex of \(\lambda({T}^{y}_{e})\).
	
	The \emph{width} of \((T,\lambda)\) is the maximum size of \(\gamma(e)\) over all edges \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	
	The \emph{directed branchwidth} of \(D\), denoted \(\ifx\relax D\relax
	\operatorname{dbw}
	\else
	
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dbw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dbw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dbw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dbw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dbw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi\), is the minimum width over all valid directed branch-decompositions of \(D\).
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- theorem-10: body_md -->
\label{thm:cyw_dtw}
	Let \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq 9k + 1\) and \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq k+1\). Then for every digraph \(D\), we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\ifx\relax D\relax
	\operatorname{dbw}
	\else
	
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dbw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dbw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dbw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dbw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dbw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi \right)}{( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi )}{( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi )}{( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi \right)}{( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi )}{( \ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtw}
	\else

	\end{equation*}
                                           
	Moreover, given a directed tree-decomposition of $D$ of width $k$, a directed branch-decomposition of $D$ of width at most $
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}

	\fi$ can be computed in polynomial time.
<!-- end theorem-10 -->
<!-- theorem-11: body_md -->
\label{thm:immersion-monotone}
Suppose \(D\) and \(D'\) are digraphs such that \(D\) contains \(D'\) as an immersion.
Then, the directed tree-cutwidth\xspace of \(D'\) is at most the directed tree-cutwidth\xspace of \(D\).
<!-- end theorem-11 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let \((T, \beta, \gamma, \theta)\) be a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of \(D\) of optimum width, and
	let \(\mu\) be an immersion model of \(D'\) in \(D\).
	Let us define a new bag function \(\beta' \colon V(T) \rightarrow 2^{V(D')}\) by setting \[\beta'(x) \coloneqq \mu^{-1}(\beta(x)),\qquad\text{for every node }x\text{ of }T.\]
	                                                    
	Further, for a set of edges \(F \subseteq E(D)\) we define
	\[\zeta(F) \coloneqq \set{e \in E(D') \mid \mu(e) \cap F \neq \emptyset}.\]
	Observe that for any \(F \subseteq E(D)\) we have \(\Abs{\zeta(F)} \leqslant \Abs{F}\), as the trails used by \(\mu\) are
	pairwise edge-disjoint by definition.
	So, we finally define \(\gamma' \colon E(T) \rightarrow 2^{E(D')}\),
	and \(\theta' \colon V(T) \rightarrow 2^{E(D')}\) by setting, for each $e\in E(T)$ and $x\in V(T)$
	\begin{equation*}
		\gamma'(e) \coloneqq \zeta(\gamma(e))\qquad\text{and}\qquad
		\theta'(x) \coloneqq \zeta(\theta(x))\text{.}
	\end{equation*}
	We now argue that \((T, \beta', \gamma', \theta')\) is a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of \(D'\). First, to argue \cref{item:dtcw:tp} we have to check that $(T, \beta', \gamma')$ is a tree-partition of $D'$.
	Clearly, \(\beta'\) is a near-partition of \(V(D')\), so \cref{item:tp:bags} holds.
	To argue \cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, consider any edge \(e = xy\) of \(T\).
	Let \(A \coloneqq \beta({T}^{x}_{e})\), \(B \coloneqq \beta({T}^{y}_{e})\),
	\(A' \coloneqq \beta'({T}^{x}_{e})\), and \(B' \coloneqq \beta'({T}^{y}_{e})\). Further,
	let \(C\) be a cycle in \(D'\) crossing the cut \((A',B')\),
	and let \(F \coloneqq \gamma(e)\) and \(F' \coloneqq \gamma'(e)\).
	Observe that \(\mu(E(C))\) is the edge set of a cycle (not necessarily simple) in \(D\)
	that crosses the cut $(A,B)$.
	Therefore, $C$ must contain an edge \(f \in F\).
	This edge must be contained in some \(\mu(f')\) where \(f' \in E(C)\),
	and therefore we have $f'\in F'\cap E(C)$; so $C$ is hit by $F'$. This establishes \cref{item:tp:edgeguards} and thereby also \cref{item:dtcw:tp}.
	The proof of \cref{item:dtcw:onionguards} follows similar lines as that of \cref{item:tp:edgeguards}; we leave it to the reader.
	                                                                           
	                                                           
	
	From the construction it follows immediately that the width of \((T, \beta', \gamma', \theta')\)
	is at most that of \((T, \beta, \gamma, \theta)\). As the width of \((T, \beta, \gamma, \theta)\) is equal to the directed tree-cutwidth of $D$, this proves that the directed tree-cutwidth of $D'$ is bounded by the directed tree-cutwidth of $D$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lemma:wall-dtcw}
	There exists a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi \colon \mathds{N} \rightarrow \mathds{N}\),
	such that for any positive integer \(k\), the directed tree-cutwidth\xspace
	of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi}\) is at least \(k\).
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:another-onion-in-the-wall}
	Let $k \geqslant 2$ be an integer, and consider the cylindrical wall $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$.
	We define $B_k$ to be the bipartite graph, with bipartition classes $P^- \cup Q^-$ and $P^+\cup Q^+$, so that vertices $u \in P^- \cup Q^-$ and $v \in P^+\cup Q^+$ are adjacent if and only if there exists an onion immersion model in $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}$ that maps the root to $u$ and the tip to $v$.
	Let $i \in [k], j\in [k-1]$.
	Then the neighbourhood of $q^+_{i,j}$ in $B_k$ contains
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $p^-_{a,b}$ for all $a \in [k] - \{i\}$ and $b \in \{1,\ldots,j\}$, and
		\item $q^-_{a,b}$ for all $a \in [k] - \{i\}$ and $b \in \{j+1,\ldots,k\}$.
	\end{itemize}
	                
	                                                                                        
	              
Similarly, for $i \in [k]$ and $j \in \{2,\ldots,k\}$, the neighbourhood of $p^+_{i,j}$ in $B_k$ contains
\begin{itemize}
	\item $p^-_{a,b}$ for all $a \in [k] - \{i\}$ and $b \in \{1,\ldots,j-1\}$, and
	\item $q^-_{a,b}$ for all $a \in [k] - \{i-1\}$ and $b \in \{j,\ldots,k\}$,
\end{itemize}
where we follow the convention that if $i=1$, then $i-1=k$.
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
The four different cases of the construction are depicted in \cref{fig:another-onion-in-the-wall}.
	First, we analyse the neighbourhood of $q^+_{i,j}$ in $B_k$, where $j\leqslant k-1$. 
	                                   
	To build an onion immersion model with tip $q^+_{i,j}$ and root $p^-_{a,b}$ with $a\neq i$ and $b\leqslant j$, we define paths                                                                       
	                                                  
	               
	
	\begin{tabular}{ccccccccc}
		$W_1=$&$q^+_{i,j}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_i}$&$q^+_{i,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$p^-_{a,b},$&& & \\
		$W_2=$&$p^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{P_a}$&$p^+_{a,j+1}$&$\xrightarrow{C_{j+1}}$&$q^-_{i,j+1}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_i}$& $q^+_{i,j},$&\textrm{and} \\
		$W_3=$&$p^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$p^-_{i,b}$&$\xrightarrow{P_i}$&$p^-_{i,j}$&$\xrightarrow{C_j}$&$q^+_{i,j}$.& 
	\end{tabular}
	
	                                                 
	\begin{figure}
		\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=0.6, label distance=-2mm]
			\def1.5{1.5}
			\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
			\node[vertex] (p-1-1-m) at ($(0,0)$) {};
			\node[vertex,label=315:{\(p^{-}_{2,1}\)}] (p-2-1-m) at ($(p-1-1-m)+(270:{2*1.5})$) {};

			\draw[directededge] (q-2-2-m) to (p-3-2-p);

			\end{tikzpicture}
		\caption{The construction of onion immersion models corresponding to four different cases considered in the proof (top left, top right, bottom left, bottom right).}
		\label{fig:another-onion-in-the-wall}
	\end{figure}

	As $b \leqslant j \leqslant k-1$, it is straightforward to verify that $W_1$, $W_2$ and $W_3$ are well-defined paths.
	Moreover, a direct inspection shows that they are pairwise internally vertex-disjoint, and thus also edge-disjoint. 
	Thus, $W_1,W_2$, and $W_3$ form an onion immersion model with root $p^-_{a,b}$ and tip~$q^+_{i,j}$.
	
	Next, we argue that for each $a\neq i$ and $b>j$, we may find an onion immersion model with root $q^-_{a,b}$ and tip $q^+_{i,j}$.
	
	\begin{tabular}{ccccccccc}
		$W_1=$&$q^+_{i,j}$&$\xrightarrow{C_j}$&$p^-_{i+1,j}$&$\xrightarrow{P_{i+1}}$&$p^+_{i+1,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$ q^-_{a,b}$, \\
		$W_2=$&$q^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$q^-_{i,b}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_i}$& $q^+_{i,j},$&\textrm{and}& \\
		$W_3=$&$q^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_a}$&$q^+_{a,j}$&$\xrightarrow{C_j}$&$q^+_{i,j}$.&&
	\end{tabular}
	
	Again, as $b>j$, these paths are well-defined and a direct inspection shows that they are internally vertex-disjoint. So they constitute an onion immersion model with root root $q^-_{a,b}$ and tip $q^+_{i,j}$.
	                                                                                                                                               
	                                                                                              
	
	               
	                                                                               
	                                                              
	             
	                                                
	
	Next, consider a vertex $p^+_{i,j}$ where $j>1$. To argue that there is an onion immersion model with tip $p^+_{i,j}$ and root $p^-_{a,b}$, for any $a\neq i$ and $b<j$,  we consider the following paths:
	
	\begin{tabular}{ccccccccc}
		$W_1=$&$p^+_{i,j}$&$\xrightarrow{C_j}$&$q^-_{i,j}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_i}$&$q^+_{i,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$ p^-_{a,b}$, \\
		$W_2=$&$p^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{P_a}$&$p^+_{a,j}$&$\xrightarrow{C_j}$&$p^+_{i,j}$,&\textrm{and}& \\
		$W_3=$&$p^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$p^-_{i,b}$&$\xrightarrow{P_i}$& $p^+_{i,j}$.&& 
	\end{tabular}

	And to argue that there is an onion immersion model with tip $p_{i,j}^+$ and root $q^-_{a,b}$, for any $a\neq i-1$ and $b\geqslant j$, we consider the following paths:
	
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        
	                                                                                                                    
	
	\begin{tabular}{ccccccccc}
		$W_1=$&$p^+_{i,j}$&$\xrightarrow{P_i}$&$p^+_{i,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$q^-_{a,b},$&& & \\
		$W_2=$&$q^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_a}$&$q^+_{a,j-1}$&$\xrightarrow{C_{j-1}}$&$p^-_{i,j-1}$&$\xrightarrow{P_i}$& $p^+_{i,j},$&\textrm{and} \\
		$W_3=$&$q^-_{a,b}$&$\xrightarrow{C_b}$&$q^-_{i-1,b}$&$\xrightarrow{Q_{i-1}}$&$q^-_{i-1,j}$&$\xrightarrow{C_j}$&$p^+_{i,j}$.& 
	\end{tabular}
	
	And again, a direct inspection shows that
	in all these cases, the paths are well-defined and internally vertex-disjoint, so they give us the desired onion immersion models.
