<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
We set \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi=k^2+1\). The case $k=1$ is straightforward to verify, so from now on we consider only $k \geqslant 2$.
	For the sake of contradiction, let $(T, \beta, \gamma, \theta)$ be a directed tree-cut decomposition of $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k^2+1}$ of width at most $k-1$. 
	Note that $\left|V\left(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k^2+1}\right)\right| = 4(k^2+1)k^2$.
	
	First, we observe that there must exist a node $x^\star \in V(T)$ such that for every neighbour $x$ of $x^\star$, the set $\beta({T}^{x}_{xx^\star})$ contains at most $2(k^2+1)k^2$ vertices of $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k^2+1}$.
	                                                                                                                                
	Indeed, to find such a node $x^\star$ it suffices to orient every edge $e=xy$ of $T$ towards $x$ or $y$ depending on which of the sets $\beta(T^x_{xy}),\beta(T^y_{xy})$ is larger (with ties broken arbitrarily), and select $x^\star$ to be a sink in this orientation (which exists since $T$ is a tree).

	As $|\theta(x^\star)| \leqslant k-1$, there exist sets $\mathcal{C}' \subseteq \{C_1,\ldots,C_{k^2+1}\}$, and $\mathcal{P}' \subseteq \{(P_1,Q_1),\ldots,(P_{k^2+1},Q_{k^2+1})\}$, each of size $\ell\coloneqq k^2-k+2$, such that every cycle in $\mathcal{C}'$, and every path in every pair in $\mathcal{P}'$ is disjoint with $\theta(x^\star)$. 
	Consider the digraph $D$ obtained by taking the union of all the edge and vertex sets of all the cycles from $\mathcal{C}'$ and paths from the pairs in $\mathcal{P}'$, and dissolving all vertices whose indegrees and outdegrees in the resulting subdigraph are at most one. 
	(Dissolving such a vertex means either removing it, if it has in-degree $0$ or out-degree~$0$, or replacing it, together with the incident edges, by a single edge from the unique in-neighbour to the unique out-neighbour.)
	                                                                                                             
	Clearly, $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k^2+1}$ contains $D$ as an immersion and $E(D) \cap \theta(x^\star) = \emptyset$.
	So in particular, there is no onion immersion model in $D$ whose root lies in a bag placed in one of the components of $T-x^\star$ and tip in the other.
	
	We observe that $D$ is isomorphic to the cylindrical wall $\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{\ell}$.
	In what follows, the sets $Q^+$, $Q^-$, $P^+$, $P^-$ are with respect to $D$, with this isomorphism in mind.
	Note that each of these sets is of size $\ell(\ell-1)$.
	Define the graph $B_\ell$ as in~\cref{lem:another-onion-in-the-wall}.
	Observe that if two vertices $u,v \in V(D)$ are adjacent in the graph $B_\ell$, there must exist a neighbour $x$ of $x^\star$ such that $u,v \in \beta(T^x_{xx^\star}) \cup \beta(x^\star)$.
	
	By \cref{lem:another-onion-in-the-wall}, any two vertices $u,v \in P^+\cup Q^+$ (resp.~$u,v \in P^- \cup Q^-$) have at least $2(\ell-2) > k-1$ common neighbours in $B_\ell$, as they must have at least $\ell-2$ common neighbours on the first cycle and at least $\ell-2$ on the last one. 
	Since $|P^+\cup Q^+| = 2\ell(\ell-1)>k-1$, there exist $u \in P^+\cup Q^+$ and a neighbour $x$ of $x^\star$ such that $u \in \beta(T^x_{xx^\star})$.
	Denote by $N(u) \subseteq P^-\cup Q^-$ the neighbourhood of $u$ in~$B_\ell$, and note that $|N(u)|\geqslant \ell(\ell-1)$. Clearly, $N(u) \subseteq \beta(T^x_{xx^\star}) \cup \beta(x^\star)$, and since $|N(u)| > k-1$, there must be $w \in N(u)$ that belongs to $\beta(T^x_{xx^\star})$ as well.
	
	Since every $u' \in P^+\cup Q^+$ has more than $k-1$ common neighbours with $u$ in $B_\ell$, all elements of $P^+\cup Q^+$ must belong to $\beta(T^x_{xx^\star}) \cup \beta(x^\star)$.
	Similarly, since every $w' \in P^-\cup Q^-$ has more than $k-1$ common neighbours with $w$ in $B_\ell$, all elements of $P^-\cup Q^-$ must belong to $\beta(T^x_{xx^\star}) \cup \beta(x^\star)$, and therefore $V(D) \subseteq \beta(T^x_{xx^\star}) \cup \beta(x^\star)$. As \[|V(D) - \beta(x^\star)|\geqslant 4\ell(\ell-1)-(k-1)>2(k^2+1)k^2 \geqslant |\beta(T^x_{xx^\star})|,\] we get a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
Set \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:wall-dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	Let \(D\) be a digraph containing an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi}\).
	By \cref{thm:immersion-monotone}, the directed tree-cutwidth\xspace of \(D\) is at least that of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi}\).
	Now \cref{lemma:wall-dtcw} implies that directed tree-cutwidth\xspace of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:imm_implies_large_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi}\)
	is at least \(k\).
	This ends the proof.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- theorem-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:onion_harvesting}
	There exists a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi \colon \mathds{N} \times \mathds{N} \to \mathds{N}\) such that the following holds.
	Let \(D\) be a digraph, \(x\) be a vertex of \(D\), \(k\) and \(c\) be a positive integers,
	and let \(\mathcal{P}\) and~\(\mathcal{Q}\) be two families of trails in \(D\), each of size at least \(
	\ifx\relax k,c\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,c \right)}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,c \right)}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,c \right)}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,c \right)}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}{( k,c )}}
                     
	\fi\), satisfying the following:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item \(
	\ifx\relax P\relax
		\mathsf{start}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{start}\!\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax Q\relax
		\mathsf{end}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{end}\!\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}
                     
	\fi = x\) for all \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) and \(Q\in \mathcal{Q}\),
		\item \(
	\ifx\relax P\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}
                     
	\fi \cap 
	\ifx\relax Q\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}
                     
	\fi \neq \emptyset\) for all \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) and \(Q \in \mathcal{Q}\), 
		\item $x$ only occurs once and each other vertex only occurs at most \(c\) times within every single trail \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) or \(Q \in \mathcal{Q}\), and
		\item the trails of \(\mathcal{P}\) are pairwise edge-disjoint and the trails of \(\mathcal{Q}\) are pairwise edge-disjoint.
	\end{itemize}
	Then \(D\) contains an onion-star immersion model of order \(k\) with centre \(x\).
	Moreover, there exists a fixed-parameter algorithm (with parameter $k$) to find such a model in~$D$.
<!-- end theorem-12 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:two_onion_stars}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi \colon \mathds{N} \to \mathds{N}\) such that the following holds.
	Let \(D\) be a digraph, \(k\) be a positive integer, and \(x \in 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) be such that there
  is an out-onion-star model immersion of order \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\)
  centred at \(x\) as well as an in-onion-star model immersion of order \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\) centred at \(x\).
	
	Then \(D\) contains an onion-star immersion model of order \(t\) centred at \(x\).
	Moreover, there exists a fixed-parameter algorithm (with parameter $k$) to find such a model in~$D$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We set $
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq 2 \cdot \mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(3((
	\ifx\relax k,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}
                     
	\fi-1)2^{8 
	\ifx\relax k,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}
                     
	\fi}+1)).$
	
	Let $\mathcal{O}'_i$ and $\mathcal{O}'_o$ be the respective families of onion immersion models that make up the in-onion-star and the out-onion-star immersion models of order \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\) in $D$ that are centred at $x$.
	In what follows, we treat the elements of $\mathcal{O}'_i$ and of $\mathcal{O}'_o$ as subgraphs of $D$.
	Each of these subgraph is the union of three edge-disjoint trails which we can assume without loss of generality to be paths.
	
	By definition of an in-onion-star immersion, the elements of $\mathcal{O}'_i$ are pairwise edge-disjoint, and the roots of the elements of $\mathcal{O}'_i$ are pairwise distinct. 
	Analogously, the elements of $\mathcal{O}'_o$ are pairwise edge-disjoint, and the tips of the elements of $\mathcal{O}'_o$ are pairwise distinct.
	We arbitrarily choose a subfamily $\mathcal{O}_i$ of \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi/2=\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(3((
	\ifx\relax k,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}
                     
	\fi-1)2^{8 
	\ifx\relax k,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}
                     
	\fi}+1))\) elements of $\mathcal{O}'_i$, and then choose a subset $\mathcal{O}_o \subseteq \mathcal{O}'_o$ of the same size so that the set of all root vertices of the elements in $\mathcal{O}_i$ is disjoint with the set of all tip vertices of the elements in $\mathcal{O}_o$. 
	
	Next, we construct a bipartite graph $G_1$ with bipartition classes $\mathcal{O}_i$ and $\mathcal{O}_o$ so that the elements of $\mathcal{O}_i$ and $\mathcal{O}_o$ are adjacent in $G_1$ if they are not edge-disjoint.
	By \cref{thm:BipRamsey}, there exist $\textsf{In} \subseteq \mathcal{O}_i$ and $\textsf{Out} \subseteq \mathcal{O}_o$, each of size $3((

		\mathsf{start}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{start}\!\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}
                     
	\fi = x\) for all \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) and \(Q\in \mathcal{Q}\),
		\item \(
	\ifx\relax P\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P \right)}{( P )}{( P )}{( P )}}
                     
	\fi \cap 
	\ifx\relax Q\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}
                     
	\fi \neq \emptyset\) for all \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) and \(Q \in \mathcal{Q}\),
		\item the trails of \(\mathcal{P}\) are pairwise edge-disjoint and the trails of \(\mathcal{Q}\) are pairwise edge-disjoint, and
		\item each trail of \(\mathcal{P}\cup \mathcal{Q}\) is the union of two paths, at most one of which visits $x$.
	\end{itemize}
	Therefore, we may use \cref{lem:onion_harvesting} with $c=2$ to conclude that $D$ contains an onion-star immersion model of order $k$ with centre $x$.
	Finally, thanks to the algorithmic statement of \cref{lem:onion_harvesting}, the reasoning given above can be turned into a fixed-parameter algorithm in a straightforward manner.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lemma:porosity-boolean}
  Let \(D\) be a digraph, \((A,B), (A',B')\) be cuts in \(D\), and $p,p'$ be the porosity of \((A,B)\) and \(A',B'\), respectively.
  Then \((A \cup A', B \cap B'), (A \cap A', B \cup B'), (A \cap B', B \cup A'), (A' \cap B, B' \cup A)\)
  are all cuts in \(D\) of porosity at most \(p+p'\).
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Since the porosity of \((A,B)\) is the same as that of \((B,A)\),
  and the roles of \((A,B)\) and \((A',B')\) are symmetric,
  it suffices to argue about \((A \cap A', B \cup B')\).

  Let \(F\) be the set of \(p\) edges hitting all cycles visiting  both \(A\) and \(B\), and let \(F'\) be the set of \(p'\) edges hitting all cycles visiting both \(A'\) and \(B'\).
  We argue next that \(F \cup F'\) hits all cycles containing a vertex of both \(A \cap A'\) and \(B \cup B'\).
  Let \(C\) be such a cycle.
  It contains a vertex \(a \in A \cap A'\), and a vertex \(b \in B \cup B'\).
  If \(b \in B\), then the cycle contains a vertex of both \(A\) and \(B\) and thus is hit by \(F\).
  If \(b \in B'\) then \(C\) contains a vertex of \(A'\) and a vertex of \(B'\) and thus is hit by \(F'\).
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lemma:porosity-onecl}
  Let \(D\) be a digraph and let \((A,B)\) be a cut in \(D\).
  Let \(X \subseteq A\) and \(A' \coloneqq \ensuremath{\text{closure}_{D[A]}\Paren*{X}}\).
  Then the porosity of the cut \((A', V(D) - A')\)
  is at most the porosity of the cut \((A,B)\).
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Let \(F\) be any set of edges hitting all cycles visiting both \(A\) and \(B\).
  We now argue that \(F\) is also a hitting set for cycles visiting both \(A'\) and \(V(D) - A'\).

  Let \(C\) be a cycle visiting a vertex \(a \in A' \subseteq A\) and \(b \in V(D) - A'\).
  This cycle clearly contains a vertex of \(A\).
  Observe, however, that \(C\) cannot be entirely contained in $D[A]$, since then \(b\) would also be a member of the closure of \(X\) within \(D[A]\), that is, of $A'$.
                                      
                                                
                                         
                                              
                                                   
                                                    
                                                       
                                                      
                                          
                                              
                                               
                                                   
  So \(C\) must visit \(B\), and thus, is a cycle crossing the cut $(A,B)$, that therefore is hit by \(F\).
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lemma:potato_uncrossing}
  Let \(D\) be a digraph and \(k\) be a non-negative integer.
  Let \(X\) be a subset of vertices of \(D\) which is \(k\)-flow-connected.
  Suppose \((A, B)\) and \((A', B')\) are cuts in \(D\) that both split \(X\), and moreover \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\) and \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
  Then one of the following outcomes holds:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(C\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
    \item \(A \cap X \subseteq B' \cap X\) or \(B' \cap X \subseteq A \cap X\), or \label{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uninteresting}
    \item \((A \cap A', B \cup B')\) and \((A \cup A', B \cap B')\) are both cuts of order \(k\) that split \(X\). \label{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uncrossing}
  \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
If~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uninteresting} does not hold, then \(A \cap A' \cap X = (A \cap X) - (B' \cap X) \neq \emptyset\) and \(B \cap B' \cap X = (B' \cap X) - (A \cap X) \neq \emptyset\).
  Thus \((A \cap A', B \cup B')\) and \((A \cup A', B \cap B')\) are both cuts in \(D\) that split \(X\).
  Since $X$ is $k$-flow-connected, both these cuts must have order at least \(k\).
  However, by submodularity we have \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cap A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}
                     
	\fi} + \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cup A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}
                     
	\fi} \leqslant \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} + \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = 2k\), so in fact they must both have order exactly \(k\).
  This shows~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uncrossing}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
Let $D$ be a digraph and $X,Y\subseteq V(D)$ be disjoint subsets of vertices. We say that an \(X\)-\(Y\)-cut $(A,B)$ is \emph{important} if the following conditions hold:
 \begin{itemize}
 	\item every vertex of $A$ is reachable from $X$ by a path whose all vertices belong to $A$; and
 	\item there is no $X$-$Y$-cut $(A',B')$ such that \(A \subsetneq A'\), every vertex of $A'$ is reachable from $X$ by a path whose all vertices belong to $A'$, and \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} \leqslant \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi}\).
 \end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- theorem-13: body_md -->
\label{thm:important_cuts}
  Let \(D\) be a digraph, \(k\) be a non-negative integer, and \(X, Y\) be disjoint subsets of \(V(D)\).
  Then,  in \(D\) there are at most \(4^k\) distinct important \(X\)-\(Y\)-cuts $(A,B)$ with \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi}\leqslant k\).
<!-- end theorem-13 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{coro:key_cuts}
  Let \(D\) be a digraph, \(k\) be a non-negative integer, and \(X, Y\) be disjoint subsets of \(V(D)\).
  Then, in \(D\) there are at most \(2\cdot4^k\) distinct $k$-key \(X\)-\(Y\)-cuts.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
Given a digraph \(D\) and a set of vertices \(S \subseteq 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) a \emph{partial tree-partition of \(S\) in \(D\)} is a tuple \(\mathcal{T} =(T,\beta,\eta)\) such that
	\begin{itemize}
		\item \(T\) is a rooted tree,
		\item \(\beta:
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi \to 2^S\) is a near-partition of \(S\),
		\item \(\eta:
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi \to \mathcal{F}\) is a bijection, where \(\mathcal{F}\) is some family of cuts in \(D\),
    \item \(\mathcal{F}\) is a family of cuts in \(D\) that is laminar on \(S\),
    \item if \(T\) has an edge, then \(T\) is the underlying tree of \(\mathsf{LaminarTree}(\mathcal{F}|_S, s)\), for some vertex \(s\) of~\(S\)
		\item for every edge \(e \in E(T)\) we have \(\beta({(T)}^{\downarrow}_{e}) \subseteq A\),
		and \(\beta({(T)}^{\uparrow}_{e}) \subseteq B\), where  \((A,B) = \eta(e)\).
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:partial_potatoes}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph and \(S \subseteq 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) a \(k\)-flow-connected set.
	Then, there exists a partial tree-partition \((T, \beta, \eta)\) of \(S\) in \(D\),
  where each \(\beta(x)\), for \(x \in V(T)\) is a \((k + 1)\)-flow-connected set.
	Furthermore, all cuts \(\eta(e)\), for \(e \in E(T)\), have order \(k\).
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
If \(S = \emptyset\), then \(S\) is already \((k + 1)\)-flow-connected,
	and we simply set
	\begin{itemize}
		\item \(T\) to be a single-node tree,
		\item \(\beta\) to map the single node to the empty set,
		\item \(\eta\) to be the empty mapping, and
	\end{itemize} 
	Let us assume henceforth that \(S\) is not empty, and fix any \(s \in S\).
	                                                                              
	Note that since \(S\) is $k$-flow-connected, every cut of $D$ that splits $S$ has order at least~$k$.
	
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      
	Let \(\mathcal{F}\) be an inclusion-wise maximal family of cuts of $D$ of order \(k\) that is laminar on \(S\).
	By reverting every cut in \(\mathcal{F}\) if necessary we may assume that for each $(A,B)\in \mathcal{F}$, we have $s\notin A$ (and thus $s\in B$).
	Let \(\sim_\mathcal{F}\) be the following equivalence relation on \(S\):
	\(u \sim_\mathcal{F} v\) if and only if no cut in \(\mathcal{F}\) splits \(\Set{u,v}\).
	                                                                                    
	Let \(\mathcal{X}\) be the set of equivalence classes under~\(\sim_\mathcal{F}\).
	
	\begin{claim}
		\label{potlemma:claim:connectivity}
		Every element \(X\) of \(\mathcal{X}\) is \((k + 1)\)-flow-connected in $D$.
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		Suppose this not the case and we have \(u,v \in X\) and a cut
		\((A, B)\) of order \(k\) in \(D\) such that \(u \in A\) and  \(v \in B\).
		Without loss of generality, assume that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		Since no cut in \(\mathcal{F}\) splits \(\Set{u,v}\) while \((A, B)\) does,
		this in particular means that \((A, B) \not\in \mathcal{F}\) and \((B,A)\notin \mathcal{F}\).
		Let us exploit this
		fact to exhibit a larger family of \(k\)-cuts that is laminar on \(S\).
		
		We construct a sequence of cuts \(F_0, F_1, \dots\).
		Let us start with \(F_0 = (A, B)\).
		We ensure that our sequence maintains the following invariants on each cut \(F_i = (A_i, B_i)\):
		\begin{itemize}
			\item \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A_i\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}
                     
	\fi}= k\);
			\item \(u \in A_i\) and \(v \in B_i\); and
			\item when \(i > 0\), if a cut \((A',B') \in \mathcal{F}\) does not cross
			with \(F_{i - 1}\) on \(S\), then it also does not cross with \(F_i\) on \(S\).
		\end{itemize}
		Suppose we have already defined \(F_i = (A_i, B_i)\).
		If the family \(\mathcal{F} \cup \Set{F_i}\) is laminar on \(S\), then the process terminates and we have arrived at a contradiction with the maximality of $\mathcal{F}$; note here that $F_i$ splits $\{u,v\}$ while no other cut of $\mathcal{F}$ does, so the cuts of $\mathcal{F} \cup \Set{F_i}$ have pairwise different intersections with~$S$.
		So, suppose that \((A_i, B_i)\) crosses some \((A', B') \in \mathcal{F}\) on $S$. Without loss of generality assume that
		\(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		Apply~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing} to \((A_i , B_i)\),
		\((A', B')\) and the set \(S\).
		In outcome~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uninteresting} we get either:
		\(A_i \cap S \subseteq B' \cap S\), or \(B' \cap S \subseteq A_i \cap S\).
		Either way, we get a contradiction that the two cuts were crossing on \(S\).
		Thus we must get outcome~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uncrossing}.
		If \(u \in A'\) then let us set \(F_{i + 1} =(A_{i+1,B_{i+1}})\coloneqq (A_i \cap A', B_i \cup B')\),
		and otherwise let us set \(F_{i + 1} =(A_{i+1,B_{i+1}})\coloneqq (A_i \cup A', B_i \cap B')\).
		By~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing}, \(F_{i + 1}\) is a cut of order k.
		By construction and by the fact that $(A',B')\in \mathcal{F}$ does not split $\{u,v\}$, we have \(u \in A_{i+1}\) and \(v \in B_{i+1}\). 
		Thus first two invariants hold. We also note that, clearly, \(C_{i + 1}\)
		does not cross \((A', B')\).
		We must argue the third invariant.
		
		Let \((A'', B'')\in \mathcal{F}\) be a cut that does not cross \(F_i\) on \(S\).
		Without loss of generality, let \(A''\) be the side that does not contain \(u\) and \(v\).
		Assume first that \(A'\) is the side of \((A', B')\) not containing \(u\) and \(v\).
		Then, by construction, \(A_{i + 1} = A_{i} \cup A'\).
		If \(A_{i}\cap S\) contains \(A''\cap S \) then so does \(A_{i + 1}\cap S\), and $(A'',B'')$ and $F_{i+1}$ do not cross on $S$.
		So \(A_i\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\) are disjoint. 
		Now, there are three cases to consider: 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item If \(A'\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\) are disjoint as well, then so are \(A_{i + 1}\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\), and again $(A'',B'')$ and $F_{i+1}$ do not cross on $S$.
			\item If \(A'\cap S \subseteq A''\cap S\), then \(A_i\cap S\) and \(A'\cap S\) are disjoint (due to \(A_i\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\) being disjoint), so $F_i$ and $(A',B')$ were not crossing on $S$ to begin with; a contradiction.
			\item Finally, if \(A''\cap S \subseteq A'\cap S\), then \(A''\cap S \subseteq A_{i + 1}\cap S\) and again, $(A'',B'')$ and $F_{i+1}$ do not cross on~$S$.
		\end{itemize}    
		The case in which \(B'\) is the side of \((A', B')\) not containing \(u\) and \(v\) follows by a symmetric argument.
		
		Since in each step the cut \(F_{i + 1}\) crosses on $S$ with at least one cut less than \(F_{i}\) does, the process must terminate in a finite number of steps giving
		the desired contradiction.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	As $\mathcal{F}$ is laminar on $S$, we have that \(\mathcal{F}|_S\) is laminar, hence we may define
	\begin{equation*}
		(T,\lambda,\eta') \coloneqq \mathsf{LaminarTree}(\mathcal{F}|_S,s).
	\end{equation*}
	Let $r$ be the root of $T$.                                                                                                                                             
	                                             
	                                                   
	We endow $T$ with a bag function \(\beta \colon V(T)\to 2^{S}\) defined as follows:
	\begin{equation*}
		\beta(t) \coloneqq \lambda(t) - \bigcup_{c\colon \text{ child of } t\text{ in }T} \lambda(c),\qquad\text{for every }t\in V(T)\text{.}
	\end{equation*}
	\begin{claim}\label{potlemma:near-partition}
		For every node \(t \in V(T)\), the set \(\beta(t)\) is either empty or satisfies
		\(\beta(t) \in \mathcal{X}\).
		Moreover, for every element \(X \in \mathcal{X}\) there is an unique node
		\(t_X \in V(T)\) such that \(\beta(t_X) = X\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		First, we verify that \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\) is a near-partition of \(S\).
		Consider any \(u \in S\) and suppose \(u \in \beta(t)\)
		and \(u \in \beta(t')\) for some distinct $t,t'\in V(T)$.
		It follows that \(u \in \lambda(t)\) and \(u \in \lambda(t')\),
		so either \(t\) is a descendant of \(t'\)
		or \(t'\) is a descendant of \(t\).
		Without loss of generality, assume the former.
		Let \(t''\) be the child of \(t'\) that is an ancestor of $t$.
		It follows that \(u \in \lambda(t'')\subseteq \lambda(t')\), and
		thus, by construction, \(u \not \in \beta(t)\); a contradiction.
		So \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\)	is a near-partition of \(S\).
		
		Note that for any node \(t \in V(T)\) we have
		\[\bigcup_{t'\colon \text{ a descendant of } t\text{ in }T} \beta(t') = \lambda(t)\text{.}\]
		Hence, if \(e=(t,c) \in E(T)\) (so \(t\) is the parent of \(c\)) and
		\((A,B) = \eta((t,c)) \in \mathcal{F}|_S\) with \(s \not \in A\), then we have
		\(A = \lambda(z)\) and consequently
		\(A = \beta({(T)}^{\downarrow}_{e})\), \(B = \beta({(T)}^{\uparrow}_{e})\).
		
		Observe now, that if $u \sim_\mathcal{F} v$, then there must exist $t\in V(T)$ with $u,v\in \beta(t)$.
		Indeed, otherwise there would be an edge $e\in E(T)$ such that $u$ and $v$ belong to the opposite sides of the cut $(\beta({(T)}^{\downarrow}_{e}),\beta({(T)}^{\uparrow}_{e}))=\eta'(e)$, implying that $\{u,v\}$ would be split by $\eta'(e)\in \mathcal{F}|_S$;
		a contradiction to $u\sim_\mathcal{F} v$.
		Consequently, every element of $\mathcal{X}$ is entirely contained in a single element of the near-partition \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\).
		
		It now remains to show that if $u,v\in S$ are such that \(u \nsim_\mathcal{F} v\), then $u,v$ belong to different parts of the near-partition \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\).
		Since $u\nsim_\mathcal{F} v$, there is a cut \((A,B) \in \mathcal{F}|_S\) such that $u$ and $v$ belong to the opposite sides of $(A,B)$.
		Since $\eta$ is a bijection, there is an edge \(e \in E(T)\) such that \(\eta'(e) = (A,B)\).
		It follows that the nodes whose bags contain $u$ and $v$ must belong to different components of \(T - e\), so these nodes in particular must be different.
		This ends the proof.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	Choosing \(\eta\) as the unique extension in \(\mathcal{F}\) of the cuts in \(\eta'\) to cuts in \(D\) finishes this proof
  (note that this is possible as \(\Abs*{\mathcal{F}} = \Abs*{\mathcal{F}|_S}\))
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- claim-1: body_md -->
\label{potlemma:claim:connectivity}
		Every element \(X\) of \(\mathcal{X}\) is \((k + 1)\)-flow-connected in $D$.
<!-- end claim-1 -->
<!-- claimproof-1: body_md -->
Suppose this not the case and we have \(u,v \in X\) and a cut
		\((A, B)\) of order \(k\) in \(D\) such that \(u \in A\) and  \(v \in B\).
		Without loss of generality, assume that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		Since no cut in \(\mathcal{F}\) splits \(\Set{u,v}\) while \((A, B)\) does,
		this in particular means that \((A, B) \not\in \mathcal{F}\) and \((B,A)\notin \mathcal{F}\).
		Let us exploit this
		fact to exhibit a larger family of \(k\)-cuts that is laminar on \(S\).
		
		We construct a sequence of cuts \(F_0, F_1, \dots\).
		Let us start with \(F_0 = (A, B)\).
		We ensure that our sequence maintains the following invariants on each cut \(F_i = (A_i, B_i)\):
		\begin{itemize}
			\item \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A_i\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_i \right)}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}{( A_i )}}
                     
	\fi}= k\);
			\item \(u \in A_i\) and \(v \in B_i\); and
			\item when \(i > 0\), if a cut \((A',B') \in \mathcal{F}\) does not cross
			with \(F_{i - 1}\) on \(S\), then it also does not cross with \(F_i\) on \(S\).
		\end{itemize}
		Suppose we have already defined \(F_i = (A_i, B_i)\).
		If the family \(\mathcal{F} \cup \Set{F_i}\) is laminar on \(S\), then the process terminates and we have arrived at a contradiction with the maximality of $\mathcal{F}$; note here that $F_i$ splits $\{u,v\}$ while no other cut of $\mathcal{F}$ does, so the cuts of $\mathcal{F} \cup \Set{F_i}$ have pairwise different intersections with~$S$.
		So, suppose that \((A_i, B_i)\) crosses some \((A', B') \in \mathcal{F}\) on $S$. Without loss of generality assume that
		\(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		Apply~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing} to \((A_i , B_i)\),
		\((A', B')\) and the set \(S\).
		In outcome~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uninteresting} we get either:
		\(A_i \cap S \subseteq B' \cap S\), or \(B' \cap S \subseteq A_i \cap S\).
		Either way, we get a contradiction that the two cuts were crossing on \(S\).
		Thus we must get outcome~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing:out_uncrossing}.
		If \(u \in A'\) then let us set \(F_{i + 1} =(A_{i+1,B_{i+1}})\coloneqq (A_i \cap A', B_i \cup B')\),
		and otherwise let us set \(F_{i + 1} =(A_{i+1,B_{i+1}})\coloneqq (A_i \cup A', B_i \cap B')\).
		By~\cref{lemma:potato_uncrossing}, \(F_{i + 1}\) is a cut of order k.
		By construction and by the fact that $(A',B')\in \mathcal{F}$ does not split $\{u,v\}$, we have \(u \in A_{i+1}\) and \(v \in B_{i+1}\). 
		Thus first two invariants hold. We also note that, clearly, \(C_{i + 1}\)
		does not cross \((A', B')\).
		We must argue the third invariant.
		
		Let \((A'', B'')\in \mathcal{F}\) be a cut that does not cross \(F_i\) on \(S\).
		Without loss of generality, let \(A''\) be the side that does not contain \(u\) and \(v\).
		Assume first that \(A'\) is the side of \((A', B')\) not containing \(u\) and \(v\).
		Then, by construction, \(A_{i + 1} = A_{i} \cup A'\).
		If \(A_{i}\cap S\) contains \(A''\cap S \) then so does \(A_{i + 1}\cap S\), and $(A'',B'')$ and $F_{i+1}$ do not cross on $S$.
		So \(A_i\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\) are disjoint. 
		Now, there are three cases to consider: 
		\begin{itemize}
			\item If \(A'\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\) are disjoint as well, then so are \(A_{i + 1}\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\), and again $(A'',B'')$ and $F_{i+1}$ do not cross on $S$.
			\item If \(A'\cap S \subseteq A''\cap S\), then \(A_i\cap S\) and \(A'\cap S\) are disjoint (due to \(A_i\cap S\) and \(A''\cap S\) being disjoint), so $F_i$ and $(A',B')$ were not crossing on $S$ to begin with; a contradiction.
			\item Finally, if \(A''\cap S \subseteq A'\cap S\), then \(A''\cap S \subseteq A_{i + 1}\cap S\) and again, $(A'',B'')$ and $F_{i+1}$ do not cross on~$S$.
		\end{itemize}    
		The case in which \(B'\) is the side of \((A', B')\) not containing \(u\) and \(v\) follows by a symmetric argument.
		
		Since in each step the cut \(F_{i + 1}\) crosses on $S$ with at least one cut less than \(F_{i}\) does, the process must terminate in a finite number of steps giving
		the desired contradiction.
<!-- end claimproof-1 -->
<!-- claim-2: body_md -->
\label{potlemma:near-partition}
		For every node \(t \in V(T)\), the set \(\beta(t)\) is either empty or satisfies
		\(\beta(t) \in \mathcal{X}\).
		Moreover, for every element \(X \in \mathcal{X}\) there is an unique node
		\(t_X \in V(T)\) such that \(\beta(t_X) = X\).
<!-- end claim-2 -->
<!-- claimproof-2: body_md -->
First, we verify that \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\) is a near-partition of \(S\).
		Consider any \(u \in S\) and suppose \(u \in \beta(t)\)
		and \(u \in \beta(t')\) for some distinct $t,t'\in V(T)$.
		It follows that \(u \in \lambda(t)\) and \(u \in \lambda(t')\),
		so either \(t\) is a descendant of \(t'\)
		or \(t'\) is a descendant of \(t\).
		Without loss of generality, assume the former.
		Let \(t''\) be the child of \(t'\) that is an ancestor of $t$.
		It follows that \(u \in \lambda(t'')\subseteq \lambda(t')\), and
		thus, by construction, \(u \not \in \beta(t)\); a contradiction.
		So \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\)	is a near-partition of \(S\).
		
		Note that for any node \(t \in V(T)\) we have
		\[\bigcup_{t'\colon \text{ a descendant of } t\text{ in }T} \beta(t') = \lambda(t)\text{.}\]
		Hence, if \(e=(t,c) \in E(T)\) (so \(t\) is the parent of \(c\)) and
		\((A,B) = \eta((t,c)) \in \mathcal{F}|_S\) with \(s \not \in A\), then we have
		\(A = \lambda(z)\) and consequently
		\(A = \beta({(T)}^{\downarrow}_{e})\), \(B = \beta({(T)}^{\uparrow}_{e})\).
		
		Observe now, that if $u \sim_\mathcal{F} v$, then there must exist $t\in V(T)$ with $u,v\in \beta(t)$.
		Indeed, otherwise there would be an edge $e\in E(T)$ such that $u$ and $v$ belong to the opposite sides of the cut $(\beta({(T)}^{\downarrow}_{e}),\beta({(T)}^{\uparrow}_{e}))=\eta'(e)$, implying that $\{u,v\}$ would be split by $\eta'(e)\in \mathcal{F}|_S$;
		a contradiction to $u\sim_\mathcal{F} v$.
		Consequently, every element of $\mathcal{X}$ is entirely contained in a single element of the near-partition \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\).
		
		It now remains to show that if $u,v\in S$ are such that \(u \nsim_\mathcal{F} v\), then $u,v$ belong to different parts of the near-partition \(\{\beta(t)\colon t\in V(T)\}\).
		Since $u\nsim_\mathcal{F} v$, there is a cut \((A,B) \in \mathcal{F}|_S\) such that $u$ and $v$ belong to the opposite sides of $(A,B)$.
		Since $\eta$ is a bijection, there is an edge \(e \in E(T)\) such that \(\eta'(e) = (A,B)\).
		It follows that the nodes whose bags contain $u$ and $v$ must belong to different components of \(T - e\), so these nodes in particular must be different.
		This ends the proof.
<!-- end claimproof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:partial_potatoes_uncrossed}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph, and \(S \subseteq 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) a \(k\)-flow-connected set.
  Let \((T, \beta, \eta')\) be a partial tree-partition of \(S\),
  such that all cuts \(\eta'(e)\), for \(e \in E(T)\), have order \(k\).
	Then, there exists a partial tree-partition \((T, \beta, \eta)\) of \(S\) in \(D\),
  such that the family of cuts \(\eta(E(T))\) is a laminar family
  of cuts.
	Furthermore, the porosity of \(\eta(e)\), for \(e \in E(T)\), is at most \(k^2\cdot 4^{k+1} + 2k\).
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
If \(S = \emptyset\) there is nothing to be done.
  Otherwise, let \(\mathcal{F}' \coloneq \eta'(E(T))\) and let \(s \in S\) be any
  vertex such that \(T\) is the underlying tree of \(\mathsf{LaminarTree}(\mathcal{F}'|_S,s)\).
	The first step is to clean the family \(\mathcal{F}'\) so that cuts having the same ``direction'' are also laminar in \(D\).
	\begin{claim}
		\label{potlemma:claim:same_orientation_laminar}
		There exists a family \(\mathcal{F}\) of cuts of $D$ of order \(k\) and a bijection \(\nu: \mathcal{F}' \to \mathcal{F}\), such that
		\begin{itemize}
			                                                                                                     
			                                                                   
			\item for every \((A,B) \in \mathcal{F}'\), the cut \((A_{\mathsf{new}}, B_{\mathsf{new}}) = \nu((A,B))\in \mathcal{F}\)
			fulfils
			\begin{equation*}
				A \cap S = A_{\mathsf{new}} \cap S\qquad\text{and}\qquad B \cap S = B_{\mathsf{new}} \cap S, \text{ and}
			\end{equation*}
			                                                                                                          
			\item for all \((A, B),(A', B') \in \mathcal{F}\),                                    
			where \(\nu^{-1}((A,B))\) is a descendant of \(\nu^{-1}((A',B'))\) in \(T\),
			\begin{itemize}
				\item if \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\), then \(A \subseteq A'\); and
				\item if \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\), then \(A \subseteq A'\).
			\end{itemize}
		\end{itemize}
		                                                                                                      
		                                                                                                                    
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		Consider any \((A,B) \in \mathcal{F}'\).
		Assume first that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		Let \(\mathcal{A}\) be the set of all ancestors \((A',B')\) of \((A,B)\) in \(T = T'\) such that
		\(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\) as well. ($\mathcal{A}$ includes $(A,B)$ as well.)
		We set
		\begin{equation*}
			A_{\mathsf{new}} \coloneqq \bigcap_{(A',B') \in \mathcal{A}} A'\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad
			B_{\mathsf{new}} \coloneqq V(D) - A_{\mathsf{new}} \qquad\textrm{and}\qquad \nu((A,B))) \coloneqq (A_{\mathsf{new}},B_{\mathsf{new}}).
		\end{equation*}
		In case that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\), we use a symmetric definition, but take $\mathcal{A}$ to be the set of all ancestors $(A',B')$ of $(A,B)$ satisfying \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		
		We next show that \((A_{\mathsf{new}}, B_{\mathsf{new}})\) is a cut of order \(k\).
		To that end it suffices to work with a single ancestor \((A',B')\) of \((A,B)\), and to assume by symmetry that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi}=\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\).
		By submodularity,
		\begin{equation*}
			\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cup A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}
                     
	\fi} + \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cap A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}
                     
	\fi} \leqslant \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} + \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A' \right)}{( A' )}{( A' )}{( A' )}}
                     
	\fi} = 2k.
		\end{equation*}
		Since \(\mathcal{F}'\) is laminar on \(S\), and \((A',B')\) is an ancestor of \((A,B)\),
		it follows that \((A \cup A') \cap S = A' \cap S\) and \(A \cap A' \cap S = A \cap S\).
		As a laminar cut family does not contain trivial cuts, we have
		\(A' \cap S \neq S\), \(A' \cap S \neq \emptyset\) and
		\(A \cap S \neq S\), \(A \cap S \neq \emptyset\).
		Therefore, as \(S\) is \(k\)-flow-connected, neither \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cup A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}
                     
	\fi}\)
		nor \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cap A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}
                     
	\fi}\) can be smaller than \(k\).
		Thus, from the inequality above it follows that, actually, \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cup A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cup A' \right)}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}{( A \cup A' )}}
                     
	\fi}=\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A \cap A'\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \cap A' \right)}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}{( A \cap A' )}}
                     
	\fi}=k\).
		By applying this reasoning to each cut of $\mathcal{A}$ consecutively, we conclude that $\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A_\mathsf{new}\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A_\mathsf{new} \right)}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_\mathsf{new} \right)}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_\mathsf{new} \right)}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_\mathsf{new} \right)}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}{( A_\mathsf{new} )}}
                     
	\fi} = k$, as required.
		
		In particular the above argument also shows \(A \cap S = A_{\mathsf{new}} \cap S\),
		and \(B \cap S = B_{\mathsf{new}} \cap S\).
		Now let \(\nu((A, B)) = (A_{\mathsf{new}},B_{\mathsf{new}}),\nu((A', B')) = (A'_{\mathsf{new}},B'_{\mathsf{new}}) \in \mathcal{F}\), where \((A,B)\) is a descendant of \((A',B')\) in \(T\).
		If \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A_{\mathsf{new}}\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}
                     
	\fi} = \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'_{\mathsf{new}}\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}} )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\) or \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A_{\mathsf{new}}\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A_{\mathsf{new}} \right)}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}{( A_{\mathsf{new}} )}}
                     
	\fi} = \Abs{
	\ifx\relax A'_{\mathsf{new}}\}\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} \right)}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}{( A'_{\mathsf{new}}\} )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\),
		then the set \(\mathcal{A}\) of ancestors used for obtaining \(A_{\mathsf{new}}\) is a subset of the set \(\mathcal{A}'\) used for obtaining \(A'_{\mathsf{new}}\).
		Thus, \(A_{\mathsf{new}} \subseteq A'_{\mathsf{new}}\).
		                                                              
			                                             
		                                                                                                                                                              
		                                                                                                                                                
			                                                                                                                     
		     
		                                                              
		                                                       
		                                                 
		                                        
		                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
		                                                                                      
		                                                                   
		                                                         
		                               
		                                                                                       
		                                                                                      
	\end{claimproof}
	
	Note that \(\mathsf{LaminarTree}(\mathcal{F}|_S,s) = \mathsf{LaminarTree}(\mathcal{F}'|_S,s)\) for the family $\mathcal{F}$ yielded by \cref{potlemma:claim:same_orientation_laminar},
  as no intersection with \(S\) has changed.
	                                                                                                       
  So we continue working with the cut family \(\mathcal{F}\).
                                                                                  
                                                                           
	
	For a cut \((A,B) \in \mathcal{F}\)                           
	we define a set $Z(A)\subseteq A$ as follows:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item If \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\), then \(Z(A)\) is the set of at most \(k\) vertices in \(A\) that are the tails of edges starting in \(A\) and ending \(B\).
		\item If \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax A\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( A \right)}{( A )}{( A )}{( A )}}
                     
	\fi} = k\), then \(Z(A)\) is the set of at most \(k\) vertices in \(A\) that are the heads of edges starting in \(B\) and ending in \(A\).
	\end{itemize} 
	
	                                  
	Consider any \((A,B) \in \mathcal{F}\).
	For a non-empty set \(Z' \subseteq Z(A) - (S \cap A)\), let \(\mathcal{C}_{A}(Z')\) be the set of
	all $k$-key \(Z'\)-\((S \cap A)\)-cuts in \(D\).
	(In most cases, we investigate \(\mathcal{C}_{A}(Z')\) for a
	specific cut \((A,B)\), and refer to it as just \(\mathcal{C}(Z')\).)
	Also, let us define two sets, \(\mu(A)\) and \(\mu'(A)\), by the following formulas:
	\begin{align*}
		\mu'(A) &\coloneqq A - \bigcup\limits_{z \in Z(A) - (S \cap A)} \ \bigcup\limits_{(E,F) \in \mathcal{C}_A(\Set{z})} \ensuremath{\text{closure}_{D[E]}\Paren*{Z(A) \cap E}}\\
		\mu(A) &\coloneqq \ensuremath{\text{closure}_{D[\mu'(A)]}\Paren*{S \cap A}}\text{.}
	\end{align*}
	Note that by definition,
	\[S\cap A\subseteq \mu(A)\subseteq \mu'(A)\subseteq A.\]
	First, let us investigate the porosity of the cut \((\mu(A), V(D) - \mu(A))\).
	
	\begin{claim}\label{claim:potato_cycle_order}
		The porosity of the cut \((\mu(A), V(D) - \mu(A))\) is at most \(2 k^2 \cdot 4^k + k\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		By~\cref{coro:key_cuts}, \((\mu'(A), V(D) - \mu'(A))\)
		is a boolean combination of at most \( 2 k \cdot 4^k + 1\) different cuts,
		each of order at most \(k\), and thus also of porosity at most \(k\).
		So by~\cref{lemma:porosity-boolean}, the porosity of
		\((\mu'(A), V(D)- \mu'(A))\) is at most \(2 k^2 \cdot 4^k + k\).
		Finally, by \cref{lemma:porosity-onecl}, the claim follows.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	With \(\mu(A)\) at hand, we almost have a globally laminar family of cuts, as the next two claims observe.
	
                                                             
                                                            
                                                                
                                                                     
               
	\begin{claim}\label{cl:incomparable}
		If two cuts \((A,B), (A',B') \in \mathcal{F}\)                                             
		are incomparable in~\(T\), then \(\mu(A) \cap \mu(A') = \emptyset\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		\begin{figure}[h!]
			\centering
                                                           
			\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=1.2]
				  
				\node (A) at (-2.5,0) {};
				\draw ($(A)+(0:2.9)$) edge[edge,closed,curve through={($(A)+(30:3)$) ($(A)+(90:2)$) ($(A)+(150:3)$) ($(A)+(180:2.7)$) ($(A)+(210:3)$) ($(A)+(270:2)$)}] ($(A)+(-30:3)$);
				\node (A-label) at ($(A)+(90:2.5)$) {\(A\)};
				
				   
				\node (Ap) at (2.5,0) {};
				\draw ($(Ap)+(0:2.7)$) edge[edge,CornflowerBlue,closed,curve through={($(Ap)+(30:3)$) ($(Ap)+(90:2)$) ($(Ap)+(150:3)$) ($(Ap)+(180:2.9)$) ($(Ap)+(210:3)$) ($(Ap)+(270:2)$)}] ($(Ap)+(330:3)$);
				\node[CornflowerBlue] (Ap-label) at ($(Ap)+(90:2.5)$) {\(A'\)};
				
				\node[vertex,violet] (v) at ($(A)!0.5!(Ap)$) {};
				\node[violet] (v-label) at ($(v)+(240:0.4)$) {\(v\)};

				\node (mpA-b) at ($(A)+(160:1)$) {};
				\node (mpA-c) at ($(A)+(200:2)$) {};
