		\label{fig:potdecomp_combination}
    \caption{Illustration of the tree-partition of \(D\) obtained by combining the constructed trees \(T_{x}\), for $x\in V(T)$, into a single tree.
      Black tree on the top-left is the tree \(T\) of the initial decomposition, the blue trees on the top-left are the trees \(T_{x}\) associated with the corresponding nodes \(x \in V(T)\).
      The blue tree on the bottom-right depicts the
      final tree-partition of \(D\).
    }
	\end{figure}

	Now we want to build a single $(k+1)$-flow-connected\xspace tree-partition of \(D\) out of the set \(\set{ (T_x, \beta_x, \eta_x) \ \colon \ x \in V(T)}\).
  We do so by adding, for every edge \((x,y) \in E(T)\)
  a single edge connecting some node of \(T_{x}\) with
  some node of \(T_{y}\).

  Consider any edge \((x',x) \in E(T)\); so \(x'\) is the parent of \(x\) in $T$.
  Denote \(R \coloneqq \beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{x})\) for brevity.
  Also, let~$T'$ be the tree $T_{x'}$ with the orientation of the edges forgotten; thus $T'$ is an undirected tree.
  We now use~\(R\) to construct a new orientation of (a subset of) the edges of \(T'\) (possibly different from the orientation of $T_{x'}$).
  Consider any edge \(f=tt' \in E(T')\) and let \((A,B)\) be the cut associated with $f$ by $\eta_{x'}$, where $A\supseteq \beta_{x'}({T}^{t}_{f})$ and $B\supseteq \beta_{x'}({T}^{t'}_{f})$.
  There are three possibilities:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item \(R\) is a subset of \(A\): we then orient the edge \(f\) towards \(t\).
    \item \(R\) is a subset of \(B\): we then orient the edge \(f\) towards \(t'\).
    \item \(R\) is neither a subset of \(A\) nor of \(B\): we leave \(f\) unoriented.
  \end{itemize}
  In the last case we say that \(x\) is \emph{ambiguous} over the edge \(f\).
  Let \(
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) denote the set of all children~\(x\) of~\(x'\) that are ambiguous over the edge~\(f\). 
  
                                                        
  The laminarity of the family of cuts $\eta_{x'}(E(T_{x'}))$ implies that there is a connected subtree $T^{\star}_x$ of $T'$ so that the edges within $T^{\star}_x$ are left unoriented, and all the other edges of $T'$ are oriented towards $T^{\star}_x$.
  (It may happen that $T^{\star}_y$ consists of one node, which is then the unique sink in the orientation of $T'$ that is total.)
  
                 
                                                                              
                                                                       
                                                                 
                                        
                                       
                                                      

  We now connect the trees $T_{x'}$ and $T_{x}$ by making the root of \(T_{x}\) a child of an arbitrarily chosen node of \(T^{\star}_{x'}\).
  By performing this operation for all the edges of the tree \(T\),
  we combine all the trees \(\set{T_{x} \colon x \in V(T)}\) into a single (rooted) tree \(T_{\mathsf{fin}}\), with \(\bigcup_{x \in V(T)} V(T_{x})\) as the node set.
  We endow $T_\mathsf{fin}$ with a bag function $\beta_\mathsf{fin}$ constructed naturally from the partial tree-partitions:
  for a node $t$ of $T_\mathsf{fin}$, we set $\beta_\mathsf{fin}(t)\coloneqq \beta_{x}(t)$, where $x\in V(T)$ is such that $t\in V(T_{x})$.
  It follows straightforwardly from the construction that $\{\beta_\mathsf{fin}(t)\colon t\in V(T_\mathsf{fin})\}$ is a near-partition of the vertex set of $D$.
  It also follows immediately from~\cref{potlemma:claim:connectivity} that for each $t\in V(T)$, the set $\beta_\mathsf{fin}(t)$ is $(k+1)$-flow-connected.
  
  To finish the description of the final tree-partition of \(D\),
  we need to specify the edge-guard function \(\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}\) and bound the cardinalities of the edge-guards.
  We define it as follows.
  For a node $x$ of $T$, set
  \(W_x \coloneqq \gamma(e)\) where $e$ is the edge connecting the parent of $x$ with $x$; if $x$ is the root of $T$, then set \(W_x \coloneqq \emptyset\).
  Let \(f=(t',t)\) be an edge of \(T_{\mathsf{fin}}\).
  If \(t' \in V(T_{x}), t \in V(T_{y})\) for some $x\neq y$, then we set \(\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f) \coloneqq \gamma((x,y))\).
  Otherwise, we have that \(t',t\in V(T_{x})\) for some $x\in V(T)$.
  Let \((A,B) \coloneqq \eta_{x}(f)\) and let \(H\)
  be a minimum size set of edges that hits all the cycles crossing the cut $(A,B)$.
  We set
  \begin{equation*}
    \gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f) \coloneqq H\cup W_x \cup \bigcup_{y \in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi} W_y\text{.}
  \end{equation*}
  This completes the description of
  \((T_{\mathsf{fin}}, \beta_{\mathsf{fin}}, \gamma_{\mathsf{fin}})\) of \(D\).

  We now argue that \((T_{\mathsf{fin}}, \beta_{\mathsf{fin}}, \gamma_{\mathsf{fin}})\)
  is indeed a correct tree-partition of \(D\).
  Clearly, \(T_{\mathsf{fin}}\) is a tree, and we have already argued that $\{\beta_\mathsf{fin}(t)\colon t\in V(T_\mathsf{fin})\}$ is a near-partition of the vertex set of $D$; so this established \cref{item:tp:bags}.
  To argue \cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, we need to prove that for each edge $f=(t',t)\in E(T_\mathsf{fin})$, the set \(\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f)\)
  intersects every cycle visiting both sets
  \(\beta_{\mathsf{fin}}({(T_{\mathsf{fin}})}^{\downarrow}_{f})\) and \(\beta_{\mathsf{fin}}({(T_{\mathsf{fin}})}^{\uparrow}_{f})\).
  This clearly holds if \(t' \in V(T_{x})\) and \(t \in V(T_{y})\) for distinct \(x,y \in V(T)\)
  by induction, for $(T,\beta,\gamma)$ is a tree-partition of $D$.
  So we may assume that \(t,t' \in V(T_{x})\) for some node $x$ of $T$.
  Let \((A,B) \coloneqq \eta_{x}(f)\).
  Further, let \(X \coloneqq \beta_{\mathsf{fin}}({(T_{\mathsf{fin}})}^{\downarrow}_{f})\)
                                                                                           
  and \(Y \coloneqq V(D) - X=\beta_{\mathsf{fin}}({(T_{\mathsf{fin}})}^{\uparrow}_{f})\).
                                                                
  Observe that by construction, \(Y\) is the union of the following sets:
  \begin{itemize}
  	\item \(\beta({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\),
  	\item \(\beta(x) \cap B\),
  	\item sets \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\) for all children \(y\) of \(x\) such that \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y}) \subseteq B\), and
  	\item sets \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\) for $y$ ranging over some subset of \(
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\)
  	(i.e.~some subset of the children of \(x\) that are ambiguous over $f$).
  \end{itemize} 
  Similarly, \(X\) is the union of the following sets:
  \begin{itemize}
  	\item \(\beta(x) \cap A\),
  	\item sets \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\) for all children \(y\) of \(x\) such that  \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y}) \subseteq A\), and
  	\item sets \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\) for $y$ ranging over a subset of \(
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) complementary to that used for \(Y\).
  \end{itemize} 
  Let \(C\) be a cycle visiting both a vertex of \(X\) and a vertex \(Y\).
  If \(C\) contains a vertex of \(\beta({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\), then $C$ must be hit by $W_x$, because $C$ cannot be entirely
  contained within \(\beta({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\) due to this set being disjoint with $X$. So assume that \(C\) is disjoint with \(\beta({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\).
  If \(C\) contains a vertex of \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\)
  for some node \(y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\), then \(C\) cannot be entirely contained in \(\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\) due to this set being disjoint with $X$ or $Y$, and consequently $C$ is hit by $W_y$.
  Thus, the only possibility remaining is that \(C\) contains
  both a vertex of \(A\) and a vertex of \(B\), and is thus hit by the set \(H\).
  Since all the mentioned hitting set were included in the definition of \(\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f)\), we conclude that in any case, \(C\) is hit by \(\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f)\).
  
  This concludes the verification of~\cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, so $(T_\mathsf{fin},\beta_\mathsf{fin},\gamma_\mathsf{fin})$ is indeed a rooted tree-partition of $D$.
  As we have already argued, the set $\beta_\mathsf{fin}(t)$ is $(k+1)$-flow-connected for each $t\in V(T_\mathsf{fin})$, hence $(T_\mathsf{fin},\beta_\mathsf{fin},\gamma_\mathsf{fin})$ is $(k+1)$-flow-connected\xspace.
  It remains to bound the porosity of $(T_\mathsf{fin},\beta_\mathsf{fin},\gamma_\mathsf{fin})$.

                                                                                                    
                                 

  To this end, consider any edge $f=(t',t)\in E(T_\mathsf{fin})$; we have to bound the size of \(\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f)\).
  Again, if~\(t\) and~\(t'\) belong to trees $T_{x}$ and $T_{y}$ for $x\neq y$, then
  \(\Abs{\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f)} = \Abs{\gamma((x,y))} \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
  So, assume that \(t,t' \in V(T_{x})\) for some node $x$ of $T$,
  and let \((A,B) \coloneqq \eta_{x}(f)\).
  Clearly, \(\Abs{W_x} \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
  By~\cref{claim:potato_cycle_order}, \(\Abs{H} \leqslant k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k\).
  All that remains is to bound the size of \(
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\). 
  For this, we make use of the coherence\xspace of $(T,\beta,\gamma)$ --- a property that we established in the second paragraph of the proof using~\cref{lem:rooting_potdecomps}.
  
  \begin{claim}\label{cl:bound-ambiguous}
  	For every node $x\in V(T)$ and edge $f\in V(T_{x})$, we have $\Abs{
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi}\leqslant k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k$.
  \end{claim}
  \begin{claimproof}
  Consider any child $y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi$ and let \(R_y \coloneqq \beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\).
  By the coherence\xspace of $(T,\beta,\gamma)$, each set $R_y$
  induces a strongly connected subgraph of $D$.
  By the ambiguity of \(y\) over the edge \(f\), it follows that $(A,B)$ splits $R_y$.
  Moreover, the sets \(\{R_y\colon y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\}\) are pairwise vertex-disjoint. Hence, for each \(y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) we can find a cycle contained in \(R_y\)
  that visits both \(A\) and \(B\).
  These cycles are pairwise vertex-disjoint and thus also
  pairwise edge-disjoint, showing that the porosity of \((A,B)\)
  is at least \(|
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi|\).
  However, by~\cref{claim:potato_cycle_order}, the porosity of $(A,B)$ is at most $k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k$.
  Hence we get \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi}\leqslant k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k\).
\end{claimproof}
  
  
  
  
  Thus, by~\cref{cl:bound-ambiguous} we have
  \begin{align*}
    \Abs*{\bigcup_{y \in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi} W_y} \leqslant \ & \Abs{
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi} \cdot 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\\ 
    \leqslant \ & \Paren{k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k} \cdot 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\text{.}
  \end{align*}
  This yields the final bound:
  \begin{align*}
    \Abs*{\gamma_{\mathsf{fin}}(f)} \leqslant \ & \Paren{k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k}+
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi+ \Paren{k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k} \cdot 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\\
    = \ & \Paren{
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi + 1} \cdot \Paren{k^2 \cdot 4^{k+1} + 2 k + 1} - 1\text{.}
  \end{align*}
  
  Therefore, we can set \(
	\ifx\relax k + 1\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k + 1 \right)}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k + 1 \right)}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k + 1 \right)}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k + 1 \right)}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}{( k + 1 )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq \Paren{
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi + 1} \cdot \Paren{k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 4k + 1} - 1\).
  
We note that the above reasoning can yield the algorithmic counterpart of the lemma.
Indeed, 
The base case of the induction can be performed in time polynomial in the size of~$D$, so can be finding the set~$\mathcal{F}'$ of cuts and modifying it into~$\mathcal{F}$ (\cref{potlemma:claim:same_orientation_laminar}).
Defining the sets $\mu(A)$ and $\mu'(A)$ for every cut $(A,B) \in \mathcal{F}$ can be done in fixed-parameter time, as finding important cuts can be done in fixed-parameter time \cite{PAbook,importantSep2008}.
Having this, for each $x \in V(T)$ we can construct $(T_{x}, \beta_{x},\eta_{x})$, as well as $T_\mathsf{fin}$ and $\beta_\mathsf{fin}$ in a straightforward~way. 

The place that benefits from more advanced algorithmic insight is the construction of $\gamma_\mathsf{fin}$ for an edge of $T_\mathsf{fin}$ that connects two vertices from $T_{x}$ for some $x \in V(T)$. 
Observe that we require computing a minimum set $H$ of edges that hits all the cycles crossing a cut $(A,B)$, but trying to adapt the proof steps directly only yields a set of size bounded by a function of $k$, without guaranteeing its minimality.
We note, however, that $H$ can be still computed fixed-parameter time, using the algorithm of Chitnis, Cygan, Hajiaghayi and Marx~\cite{DBLP:journals/talg/ChitnisCHM15} for the \textsc{Directed Subset Feedback Vertex Set}.
We define a line digraph $L(D)$ of $D$ to have the vertex set $E(D)$ and an edge from $(x,y)$ to $(z,w)$ if $y=z$. We also let $S \subseteq V(L(D))=E(D)$ to be the edges crossing $(A,B)$.
Then, by~\cite{DBLP:journals/talg/ChitnisCHM15}, we can find a minimum set $W$ of vertices of $L(D)$ that hits all the cycles that contain an element of $S$; clearly $W$ corresponds to a set of arcs of $D$ that define~$H$. 

Last, we note that the number of inductive steps in the proof of \cref{lemma:potato} is bounded by $k$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- claim-9: body_md -->
\label{cl:bound-ambiguous}
  	For every node $x\in V(T)$ and edge $f\in V(T_{x})$, we have $\Abs{
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi}\leqslant k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k$.
<!-- end claim-9 -->
<!-- claimproof-9: body_md -->
Consider any child $y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi$ and let \(R_y \coloneqq \beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y})\).
  By the coherence\xspace of $(T,\beta,\gamma)$, each set $R_y$
  induces a strongly connected subgraph of $D$.
  By the ambiguity of \(y\) over the edge \(f\), it follows that $(A,B)$ splits $R_y$.
  Moreover, the sets \(\{R_y\colon y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\}\) are pairwise vertex-disjoint. Hence, for each \(y\in 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) we can find a cycle contained in \(R_y\)
  that visits both \(A\) and \(B\).
  These cycles are pairwise vertex-disjoint and thus also
  pairwise edge-disjoint, showing that the porosity of \((A,B)\)
  is at least \(|
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi|\).
  However, by~\cref{claim:potato_cycle_order}, the porosity of $(A,B)$ is at most $k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k$.
  Hence we get \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\operatorname{Amb}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{Amb}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\operatorname{Amb}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi}\leqslant k^2 \cdot 4^{k + 1} + 2 k\).
<!-- end claimproof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:cyclewidth_to_dtcw}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph with maximum cycle-degree at most \(d\) and directed branchwidth at most~\(w\).
	Then \(D\) has directed tree-cutwidth\xspace at most \(\max \set{3 d \cdot w, 1}\).
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let \((\widehat{T},\widehat{\lambda})\) be an directed branch-decomposition of \(D\) of width at most \(w\).
	Thus there exists a vertex-guard \(\widehat{\gamma}(e)\) of size at most \(w\) for every \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	For a vertex \(v \in V(D)\), let \(h(v)\) be a set of at most \(d\) edges of \(D\)
	that hits all cycles containing \(v\).
	
	We define a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) of \(D\) using the following formulas, for all $x\in V(T)$ and $e\in E(T)$:
	\begin{align*}
		T &\coloneqq \widehat{T};\\
		\beta(x) &\coloneqq \begin{cases*}
			\Set{\widehat{\lambda}(x)} & \text{if \(x\) is a leaf,}\\
			\emptyset & \text{otherwise;}
		\end{cases*}\\
		\gamma(e) &\coloneqq \bigcup \set{h(v) \colon v \in \widehat{\gamma}(e)};\\
		\theta(x) &\coloneqq \bigcup_{e \sim x} \gamma(e).
	\end{align*}
	
	We now argue that this yields a valid directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace.
	\Cref{item:tp:bags} is obvious, as the mapping \(\widehat{\lambda}\) bijectively maps \(V(D)\) to the set of leaves of \(T\).
	To argue \cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, take any edge \(e \in E(T)\). The cuts induced by this edge in \((T,\beta)\)
	and in \((T,\widehat{\lambda})\) are the same. Thus, any cycle \(C\) crossing this cut contains a vertex \(v \in \widehat{\lambda}(e)\) by the definition of directed branchwidth.
	Thus it contains some edge \(f \in h(v)\), and thus also of \(\gamma(e)\); so this establishes \cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, and hence also \cref{item:dtcw:tp}.
	To argue~\cref{item:dtcw:onionguards}, consider any \(x \in V(T)\) and observe that every in-onion of $x$ and every out-onion of $x$ contains a cycle that crosses one of the cuts induced in $(T,\beta)$ by some edge $e$ of $T$ incident to $x$.
	So this cycle, and therefore also the onion, is hit by \(\gamma(e)\subseteq \theta(x)\).
	
	We finish by providing an upper bound on the width of the decomposition obtained.
	Note that for every edge $e$ of $T$ we have \(\Abs{\gamma(e)} \leqslant d \cdot w\), as it is the union of at most \(w\) edge sets (as \(\Abs{\widehat{\gamma}(e)} \leqslant w\)),	each of size at most \(d\).
	It follows that \(\Abs{\theta(x)} \leqslant 3d \cdot w\) for every node $x$, as the tree \(T=\widehat{T}\) is subcubic.
	Finally, by construction we also have \(\Abs{\beta(x)} \leqslant 1\), providing the desired bound on the width.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- theorem-14: body_md -->
\label{thm:bd_degree_case}
	                                                                                                                                                
	There exists a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\times \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that for every non-negative integer~\(d\), positive integer~\(k\), and digraph~\(D\) with maximum cycle-degree at most~\(d\), one of the following~holds:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item \(D\) contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as an immersion, or
		\item \(\ifx\relax D\relax
	\operatorname{dtcw}
	\else
	
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtcw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtcw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax d, k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{itemize}
	Moreover, if $d=d(k)$, there exists a fixed-parameter algorithm to find, for every digraph \(D\) with maximum cycle-degree at most $d$, and parameter $k$, either an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) in \(D\), or a directed tree-cut decomposition of $D$ of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax d, k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d, k \right)}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}{( d, k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
<!-- end theorem-14 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Let \(D\) be a digraph with maximum cycle-degree at most \(d\),	and assume that it does not contain \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as an immersion.
	It follows that it does not contain \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as a topological minor.
	By~\cref{thm:gridtheorem}, \(D\)~has directed tree-width at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\); let \(k' \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	By~\cref{thm:cyw_dtw} it follows that the directed branchwidth of \(D\) is at most \(
	\ifx\relax k'\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}
                     
	\fi\); let \(\ell \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax k'\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k' \right)}{( k' )}{( k' )}{( k' )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	Finally, by~\cref{lem:cyclewidth_to_dtcw} we infer that the directed tree-cutwidth of \(D\) is at most	\(\max\{3d \cdot \ell, 1\}\).
	
	Thus, we conclude that the theorem holds with \(
	\ifx\relax d,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq \max \set{3d \cdot 
	\ifx\relax 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi \right)}{( 
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:gridtheorem}}}
	\else

	\fi )}{(

	\fi )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{thm:cyw_dtw}}}\mathchoice{\left(

	\fi )}}
                     
	\fi, 1}\).
	
	Last, the construction of \cref{lem:cyclewidth_to_dtcw} and the algorithmic claim in~\cref{thm:cyw_dtw}                                  
	can be combined with \cref{thm:grid-algorithmic} in a straightforward way to yield the algorithmic statement.

<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:tripod_hitting}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph with no \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion and with at most \(z\) vertices of cycle-degree at least \(d\), for some \(k,d,z\in \mathds{N}\).
	If there is a hitting set of size at most \(x \in \mathds{N}\) for all out-tripods (or for all in-tripods) of \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi = \mathchoice{\left( D',S,T \right)}{( D',S,T )}{( D',S,T )}{( D',S,T )}\), then there is a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of \(D\) of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax d,x,z,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	
	Moreover, if $d=d(k)$, $x=x(k)$ and $z=z(k)$, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm with parameter $k$ to find a directed tree-cut decomposition of $D$ of width at most $
	\ifx\relax d,x,z,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}
                     
	\fi$.
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Let \(Z\) be the set of at most \(z\) vertices of cycle-degree at least \(d\).
  Without loss of generality, let us assume that there is a hitting set \(F'\subseteq E(D)\) of size \(x\) for all in-tripods of \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	The proof for out-tripods works analogously.
	
	We define \(
	\ifx\relax d,x,z,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}
                     
	\fi \coloneqq
	x + 
	\ifx\relax d,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}
                     
	\fi + z + 1\).

  Define the following set of edges \(F \subseteq E(D)\).
  For any edge \(e' \in F'\):
  \begin{itemize}
    \item if \(e' \in E(D)\), then add \(e'\) to \(F\);
    \item if \(e' = (
	\ifx\relax e_i\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( e_i \right)}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e_i \right)}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e_i \right)}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e_i \right)}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}{( e_i )}}
                     
	\fi,t_i)\) for some \(e_i \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi\), then add \(e_i\) to \(F\);
    \item if \(e' = (s_j, 
	\ifx\relax e_j\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( e_j \right)}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e_j \right)}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e_j \right)}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e_j \right)}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}{( e_j )}}
                     
	\fi)\) for some \(e_j \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi\), then add \(e_j\) to \(F\).
  \end{itemize}
  By construction we clearly have \(\Abs{F} \leqslant \Abs{F'} = x\).
	Let \[D^\star \coloneqq (D- F) -Z.\]
	We consider a topological ordering \(\preccurlyeq\) on the strongly connected components of \(D^\star\) (called further \emph{strong components} for brevity). Thus, for every edge $(u,v)$ of $D^\star$, if $R_u$ and $R_v$ are the strong components containing $u$ and $v$ respectively, then we have $R_u\preccurlyeq R_v$.
	
	                                                               
	                                                                                                                                                    
	                                                                                                                 
	                                                                                                                                         
	                                   
	                                                                                                                                                                                           
	                                                                                
	                                     
	                                                                                                                                         
                                                                                                            
                                                 
	We now build a partition \(\mathcal{B}\) of $V(D)- Z$ as follows.
  Let \(\mathcal{R}\) be the set of all strong components \(R\) of \(D^\star\)
  satisfying the following property: there exists a strong component \(R'\) of \(D^\star\)
  which is incident to an edge of \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\) and satisfies \(R' \preccurlyeq R\).
  That is, \(\mathcal{R}\) is the upwards closure of components incident to \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
  Let \(B_{\mathsf{rest}}\) be the union of all the remaining strong components of \(D^\star\).

                                                                           
                                                                            
                                                                             
                                                                            
    
  \begin{claim}
    \label{claim:atmost_1_edge}
    Let \(A\) be a strong component of \(D^\star\).
    If there is an edge not in \(F\) starting in \(Z\) and ending in some vertex of \(A\),
    then in \(D - F\), there is at most one edge of \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\)  that can be reached from $A$ by a directed path whose all internal vertices do not belong to $Z$.
  \end{claim}
  \begin{claimproof}
    Suppose towards a contradiction that the claim was false.
    Then, there are two edges \(f_1, f_2 \in E(D) - F\)
    with \(
	\ifx\relax f_i\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}
                     
	\fi \in Z, 
	\ifx\relax f_i\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}
                     
	\fi \in V(D) - Z\),
    and we can get from \(A\) both to \(
	\ifx\relax f_1\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}
                     
	\fi\) and to \(
	\ifx\relax f_2\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}
                     
	\fi\) by a directed path in \(D - F\) that avoids $Z$ except for the last vertex.
    Thus, the subpaths that reach \(
	\ifx\relax f_i\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}
                     
	\fi\) are actually contained within \(D^\star = (D - Z) - F\).
    Let \(e \in E(D) - F\) be such that
    \(
	\ifx\relax e\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}
                     
	\fi \in A\) and \(
	\ifx\relax e\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}
                     
	\fi \in Z\).
    Let \(s \in 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) be the vertex corresponding
    to the edge \(e\), and \(t_1, t_2 \in 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\)
    be the vertices corresponding to the edges \(f_1, f_2\).
    Clearly, in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi - F'\),
    \(s\) can reach both \(t_1\) and \(t_2\) (as \(A\) is a strong component).
    This gives us an in-tripod in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) that avoids \(F'\); this is a contradiction.
  \end{claimproof}
  This claim implies that we can induce a near-partition of \(\mathcal{R}\) via \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
  That is, for each edge \(f \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\),
  let \(\mathcal{B}_f\) be the set of all elements of \(\mathcal{R}\) that can reach \(
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) in \(D^\star\);
  additionally, let \(B_{\emptyset}\) be the set of all elements of \(\mathcal{R}\)
  that cannot reach (in \(D^\star\)) any \(
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\), for \(f \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
  By~\cref{claim:atmost_1_edge}, the family
  \(\set{\mathcal{B}_f \colon f \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F} \cup \set{\mathcal{B}_{\emptyset}}\)
  is a near-partition of \(\mathcal{R}\).
  This is the basis of our construction of \(\mathcal{B}\). In $\mathcal{B}$, we collect:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \(B_f \coloneqq \bigcup \mathcal{B}_f\), for \(f\in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\) provided that \(\mathcal{B}_f\) is non-empty;
    \item \(B_{\emptyset} \coloneqq \bigcup \mathcal{B}_{\emptyset}\), provided that \(\mathcal{B}_{\emptyset}\) is non-empty;
    \item \(B_{\mathsf{rest}}\), provided that \(B_{\mathsf{rest}}\) is non-empty.
  \end{enumerate}

	See~\cref{fig:fdeleted_structure}
	                          
	for an illustration of this construction.
