                                       
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  
                            
              
  


  For each set \(B \in \mathcal{B}\), define
  \[
    h(B) \coloneqq \begin{cases}
      \set{f},& \text{ if \(B = B_f\) for some \(f \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\),}\\
      \emptyset,& \text{ if \(B = B_{\mathsf{rest}}\) or \(B = B_{\emptyset}\).}
    \end{cases}
  \]

  \begin{claim}
    \label{claim:cycle_hitting}
    Let \(B \in \mathcal{B}\). Suppose \(C\) is a cycle in $D$
    visiting a vertex of \(B\) and of \(V(D) - B\).
    Then \(C\) contains an edge of \(F \cup h(B)\).
  \end{claim}
  \begin{claimproof}
    If \(C\) contains an edge of \(F\) we are immediately done.
    So, assume that \(C\) does not contain any edge of \(F\).
    As \(B\) is the union of a collection of strong components of \(D^\star = (D - F) - Z\),
    and \(C\) visits both a vertex of \(B\) and
    a vertex outside of \(B\), it follows that $C$ must contain a vertex
    of \(Z\).

    Let \(b \in B\) be a vertex visited by \(C\).
    Let \(e\) be an edge in \(C\) that is the last edge before
    \(C\) enters the vertex \(b\) that is an element of \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
    Clearly this edge enters some strong component \(A\) of \(D^\star\),
    and all the edges between $e$ and $b$ along $C$ are contained in \(D^\star\),
    showing that \(b\) is reachable from \(A\) in~$D^\star$.
    This means that the strong component of \(b\) in \(D^\star\)
    is an element of \(\mathcal{R}\). Thus, \(B \neq B_{\mathsf{rest}}\).
    
    Let \(f\) be the first edge of \(C\) occurring
    after \(C\) leaves the vertex \(b\) that is an element of
    \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
    Thus, the strong component of \(b\) in \(D^\star\)
    can reach the edge \(f\) by a path avoiding $F$ or $Z$, and by~\cref{claim:atmost_1_edge}
    it follows that \(B = B_f\), and so \(C\) is hit by \(\set{f} = h(B)\).
  \end{claimproof}

	Clearly, every \(B \in \mathcal{B}\) induces a subgraph \(D_B\coloneqq D[B]\) of \(D\) with maximum cycle-degree less than \(d\).
	So, by~\cref{thm:bd_degree_case} and as no \(D_B\) contains a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion, there is a
  directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T_B,\beta_B,\gamma_B,\theta_B \right)}{( T_B,\beta_B,\gamma_B,\theta_B )}{( T_B,\beta_B,\gamma_B,\theta_B )}{( T_B,\beta_B,\gamma_B,\theta_B )}\) for \(D_B\) of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax d,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:bd_degree_case}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,k \right)}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}{( d,k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	The next step is to combine these decompositions into a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) for \(D\).
	
	Let \(T\) be the tree obtained from the union over all \(T_B\), for \(B \in \mathcal{B}\), by adding an extra node \(s\) connected to exactly one (arbitrary chosen) node of each \(T_B\). We endow $T$ with a bag function $\beta$ defined naturally:
	\begin{align*}
		\beta(t) &\coloneqq \begin{cases}
			\beta_B(t), & \text{ if \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_B\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}
                     
	\fi\) for some \(B\in \mathcal{B}\),}\\
			Z, & \text{ if \(t = s\).}
		\end{cases} 
	\end{align*}
	
	We build the edge-guards as follows:
	\begin{align*}
		\gamma(e) & \coloneqq 
			\begin{cases}
				\gamma_B(e) \cup h(B) \cup F, &\text{ if \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T_B\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}
                     
	\fi\) for some \(B \in \mathcal{B}\),}\\
				F \cup h(B),&\text{ if \(e \sim s\) and \(e \sim a\) for some \(a \in 
	\ifx\relax T_B\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}
                     
	\fi\).}
			\end{cases}
	\end{align*}
	
	For the onion-guards we add the hitting set and the edges into \(Z\) to the old onion-guards:
	\begin{align*}
		\theta(t) &\coloneqq \begin{cases}
			\theta_B(t) \cup F \cup h(B), &\text{ if \(t \in T_B\) for some \(B \in \mathcal{B}\),}\\
			F,&\text{ if \(t = s\).}
		\end{cases}
	\end{align*}
	
	                                                                                                             
	                                                                                                                                    
	                                                      
	  
	                                                                                                                                                   
	                               
	                                                                                                                                                               
	
	Note that if $d=d(k)$, $z=z(k)$, and $x=x(k)$, the above can be modified in a straightforward way to obtain a fixed-parameter algorithm (with respect to $k$) to find \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\). What is left to verify is that \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) is indeed a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of $D$ of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax d,x,z,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	
	\paragraph{Edge-guards}
	                                                                                                                   
	                                                                 
	
	First, note that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax e\relax
		\gamma
	\else
		\mathchoice{\gamma\!\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\gamma\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\gamma\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\gamma\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}
                     
	\fi} \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax d,x,z,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z,k \right)}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}{( d,x,z,k )}}
                     
	\fi\) for all \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\). We are left with verifying the cycle-hitting property of the sets $
	\ifx\relax e\relax
		\gamma
	\else
		\mathchoice{\gamma\!\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\gamma\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\gamma\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\gamma\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}
                     
	\fi$.
	
	Consider the case that \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T_B\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_B \right)}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}{( T_B )}}
                     
	\fi\) for some \(B \in \mathcal{B}\).
  Let \(C\) be a cycle crossing the cut induced by \(e\).
  If \(C\) contains an edge of \(F\) then it is hit by \(\gamma(e)\),
  as it contains \(F\), so assume that is not the case.
  If \(C\) is contained in \(B\), then
  it is hit by \(\gamma_B(e) \subseteq \gamma(e)\),
  as \((T_B,\beta_B,\gamma_B,\theta_B)\) is a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace.
  As \(V(D) - B\) is contained on one side
  of the cut induced by \(e\), to cross this cut
  \(C\) must contain a vertex of \(B\) and a vertex outside of \(B\).
  Now, by~\cref{claim:cycle_hitting},
  \(C\) is hit by \(F \cup h(B) \subseteq \gamma(e)\)
  and we are done.

  Now consider the case of \(e\) being an edge connecting \(s\)
  and a node of some \(T_B\). Thus, the cut we consider is
  \((B, V(D) - B)\).
  This is exactly the setup of~\cref{claim:cycle_hitting},
  so \(C\) is hit by \(F \cup h(B) = \gamma(e)\).
	
	\paragraph{Onion-guards}
	Again, note that \(\Abs{
	\ifx\relax t\relax
		\theta
	\else
		\mathchoice{\theta\!\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\theta\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\theta\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\theta\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}
                     
	\fi} \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax d,x,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z \right)}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z \right)}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z \right)}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,x,z \right)}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}{( d,x,z )}}
                     
	\fi\) for all \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\). We are left with verifying the onion-hitting property of the sets $
	\ifx\relax t\relax
		\theta
	\else
		\mathchoice{\theta\!\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\theta\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\theta\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\theta\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}
                     
	\fi$.
	
  For the node \(s\), we argue that all the in-onions of $s$ are hit.
  We consider the following cases. Let \(e\) be an edge of \(T\)
  incident to \(s\), connecting it to some node of \(T_B\), \(B \in \mathcal{B}\).
  We thus we are considering the cut \((B, V(D) - B)\).
  Let \(\mu\) be an onion immersion model, with root in \(B\)
  and tip in \(V(D) - B\).
  We can reinterpret \(\mu\) as a strongly connected subdigraph
  that contains both a vertex of \(B\) and a vertex outside of \(B\).
  Thus, there is a cycle containing a vertex of \(B\) and a
  vertex outside of \(B\) contained in this subdigraph.
  By~\cref{claim:cycle_hitting},
  this cycle is hit by \(F \cup h(B)\).
  Thus, if \(B = B_{\mathsf{rest}}\) or \(B = B_{\emptyset}\) we are done,
  as in these cases \(F \cup h(B) = F = \theta(s)\).
  So, assume that \(B = B_f\) for some \(f \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
  Let us analyse what edges can there be between \(B_f\) and other members \(B' \in \mathcal{B}\)
  (outside of the set \(F\)).
  Observe that there cannot be any edges between \(B_f\) to \(B_{f'}\)
  for \(f' \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\) distinct from \(f\),
  as then some strong component of \(\mathcal{R}\) would reach both \(f\) and \(f'\)
  contradicting~\cref{claim:atmost_1_edge}.
  Likewise, there cannot be an edge from \(B_f\) to \(B_{\mathsf{rest}}\)
  as this would contradict the definition of \(B_{\mathsf{rest}}\).
  And finally there cannot be an edge from \(B_\emptyset\)
  to \(B_f\) as this would expose a component
  of \(B_\emptyset\) that can reach \(f\).
  Furthermore, there is a single edge connecting
  \(B_f\) to \(Z\) --- the edge \(f\).
  As the same applies to all other components
  \(B_{f'}\) for \(f' \in 
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\),
  see~\cref{fig:fdeleted_structure},
  this implies that \(
	\ifx\relax B_f \cup B_\emptyset\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\!\mathchoice{\left( B_f \cup B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\mathchoice{\left( B_f \cup B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\mathchoice{\left( B_f \cup B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\mathchoice{\left( B_f \cup B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}{( B_f \cup B_\emptyset )}}
                     
	\fi = \set{f}\).
  Thus, as \(\mu\) contains two edge-disjoint paths
  from the root to the tip,
  it follows that the tip of \(\mu\) must be an element of \(B_\emptyset\).
  But \(
	\ifx\relax B_\emptyset\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\!\mathchoice{\left( B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\mathchoice{\left( B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\mathchoice{\left( B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D - F}\mathchoice{\left( B_\emptyset \right)}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}{( B_\emptyset )}}
                     
	\fi = \emptyset\),
  which contradicts the existence of the path
  connecting the tip to the root within \(\mu\).
  This shows that there is no in-onion that avoids \(F\)
  at~\(s\).

  \begin{figure}[!ht]
    \centering
                                                        
    \begin{tikzpicture}
    	\node (centre) at (0,0) {};
    	\node[edge,LavenderMagenta,ellipse,minimum height=1.5cm,minimum width=2.5cm] (Z) at ($(centre)+(0,2)$) {\(Z\)};
    	
    	\node[edge,ellipse,minimum height=1.5cm,minimum width=2.5cm] (B-r) at ($(centre)+(160:3)$) {\(B_{\mathsf{rest}}\)};
    	\node[edge,ellipse,minimum height=1.5cm,minimum width=2.5cm] (B-0) at ($(centre)+(20:3)$) {\(B_{\emptyset}\)};
    	
    	\node[edge,CornflowerBlue,ellipse,minimum height=1.5cm,minimum width=2.5cm] (B-f) at ($(centre)+(0,0)$) {\(B_f\)};
    	\node[edge,CornflowerBlue,ellipse,minimum height=1.5cm,minimum width=2.5cm] (B-fp) at ($(B-f)+(0,-2)$) {\(B_{f'}\)};
    	
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,LavenderMagenta,bend right] ($(Z)+(170:0.8)$) to ($(B-r)+(60:0.4)$);
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,LavenderMagenta,bend right] ($(B-0)+(120:0.5)$) to ($(Z)+(10:0.7)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,out=175,in=5] ($(B-0)+(180:0.7)$) to ($(B-r)+(360:0.7)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,PastelOrange!50,line width=3pt,bend right] ($(B-r)+(315:0.6)$) to ($(B-f)+(180:0.7)$);
    	\draw[directededge,PastelOrange!50,line width=3pt,bend right] ($(B-f)+(0:0.8)$) to ($(B-0)+(225:0.5)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,PastelOrange!50,line width=3pt,bend right] ($(B-r)+(290:0.5)$) to ($(B-fp)+(180:0.7)$);
    	\draw[directededge,PastelOrange!50,line width=3pt,bend right] ($(B-fp)+(0:0.8)$) to ($(B-0)+(250:0.4)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,out=180,in=45] ($(B-0)+(200:0.7)$) to ($(B-f)+(45:0.5)$);
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,out=125,in=0] ($(B-f)+(135:0.5)$) to ($(B-r)+(-20:0.7)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,bend right] ($(B-f)+(250:0.4)$) to ($(B-fp)+(110:0.4)$);
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,bend right] ($(B-fp)+(70:0.4)$) to ($(B-f)+(300:0.4)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,out=225,in=225] ($(B-r)+(225:0.5)$) to ($(B-fp)+(225:0.5)$);
    	\draw[directededge,dashed,out=315,in=315] ($(B-fp)+(315:0.5)$) to ($(B-0)+(315:0.5)$);
    	
    	\draw[directededge,LavenderMagenta,out=20,in=315] ($(B-f)+(20:0.6)$) to ($(Z)+(315:0.5)$);
    \end{tikzpicture}
    \caption{Depiction of the structure of the digraph \(D - F\).
      Dashed arrows represent non-edges,
      while \textcolor{PastelOrange}{thick orange edges} represent arbitrary amount of edges (possibly zero) in the prescribed direction.
      The \textcolor{LavenderMagenta}{magenta} edge is the edge \textcolor{LavenderMagenta}{\(f\)}.
    }
    \label{fig:fdeleted_structure}
  \end{figure}

  For a node \(x \in V(T_B)\) for \(B \in \mathcal{B}\),
  we hit onions in the same orientation
  as in the original directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \((T_B, \beta_B, \gamma_B, \theta_B)\).
  Let \(e\) be an edge incident to \(x\).
  Let \(\mu\) be an onion immersion model.
  Note that we can reinterpret \(\mu\) as a strongly connected
  subdigraph of \(D\).
  If this subdigraph contains
  a vertex of \(B\) and of \(V(D) - B\)
  then it also contains a cycle with a vertex of \(B\) and a vertex outside of \(B\).
  By~\cref{claim:cycle_hitting},
  this cycle is hit by \(F \cup h(B) \subseteq \theta(x)\).
  So we are left with considering onion immersion models
  fully contained in \(B\) or fully contained in \(V(D)- B\).
  In the latter case, the model does not cross the cut
  induced by \(e\); in the former we conclude (as \(\mathcal{T}_B = (T_B, \beta_B, \gamma_B, \theta_B)\) is a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace)
  that if \(\mu\) has an appropriate orientation
  (the one hit by \(\mathcal{T}_B\) at \(x\)) then
  \(\mu\) is hit by \(\theta_B(x) \subseteq \theta(x)\);
  in either case we obtain the desired conclusion.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- claim-10: body_md -->
\label{claim:atmost_1_edge}
    Let \(A\) be a strong component of \(D^\star\).
    If there is an edge not in \(F\) starting in \(Z\) and ending in some vertex of \(A\),
    then in \(D - F\), there is at most one edge of \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\)  that can be reached from $A$ by a directed path whose all internal vertices do not belong to $Z$.
<!-- end claim-10 -->
<!-- claimproof-10: body_md -->
Suppose towards a contradiction that the claim was false.
    Then, there are two edges \(f_1, f_2 \in E(D) - F\)
    with \(
	\ifx\relax f_i\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}
                     
	\fi \in Z, 
	\ifx\relax f_i\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}
                     
	\fi \in V(D) - Z\),
    and we can get from \(A\) both to \(
	\ifx\relax f_1\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_1 \right)}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}{( f_1 )}}
                     
	\fi\) and to \(
	\ifx\relax f_2\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_2 \right)}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}{( f_2 )}}
                     
	\fi\) by a directed path in \(D - F\) that avoids $Z$ except for the last vertex.
    Thus, the subpaths that reach \(
	\ifx\relax f_i\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f_i \right)}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}{( f_i )}}
                     
	\fi\) are actually contained within \(D^\star = (D - Z) - F\).
    Let \(e \in E(D) - F\) be such that
    \(
	\ifx\relax e\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}
                     
	\fi \in A\) and \(
	\ifx\relax e\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e \right)}{( e )}{( e )}{( e )}}
                     
	\fi \in Z\).
    Let \(s \in 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) be the vertex corresponding
    to the edge \(e\), and \(t_1, t_2 \in 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\)
    be the vertices corresponding to the edges \(f_1, f_2\).
    Clearly, in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi - F'\),
    \(s\) can reach both \(t_1\) and \(t_2\) (as \(A\) is a strong component).
    This gives us an in-tripod in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) that avoids \(F'\); this is a contradiction.
<!-- end claimproof-10 -->
<!-- claim-11: body_md -->
\label{claim:cycle_hitting}
    Let \(B \in \mathcal{B}\). Suppose \(C\) is a cycle in $D$
    visiting a vertex of \(B\) and of \(V(D) - B\).
    Then \(C\) contains an edge of \(F \cup h(B)\).
<!-- end claim-11 -->
<!-- claimproof-11: body_md -->
If \(C\) contains an edge of \(F\) we are immediately done.
    So, assume that \(C\) does not contain any edge of \(F\).
    As \(B\) is the union of a collection of strong components of \(D^\star = (D - F) - Z\),
    and \(C\) visits both a vertex of \(B\) and
    a vertex outside of \(B\), it follows that $C$ must contain a vertex
    of \(Z\).

    Let \(b \in B\) be a vertex visited by \(C\).
    Let \(e\) be an edge in \(C\) that is the last edge before
    \(C\) enters the vertex \(b\) that is an element of \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
    Clearly this edge enters some strong component \(A\) of \(D^\star\),
    and all the edges between $e$ and $b$ along $C$ are contained in \(D^\star\),
    showing that \(b\) is reachable from \(A\) in~$D^\star$.
    This means that the strong component of \(b\) in \(D^\star\)
    is an element of \(\mathcal{R}\). Thus, \(B \neq B_{\mathsf{rest}}\).
    
    Let \(f\) be the first edge of \(C\) occurring
    after \(C\) leaves the vertex \(b\) that is an element of
    \(
	\ifx\relax Z\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\!\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{}\mathchoice{\left( Z \right)}{( Z )}{( Z )}{( Z )}}
                     
	\fi - F\).
    Thus, the strong component of \(b\) in \(D^\star\)
    can reach the edge \(f\) by a path avoiding $F$ or $Z$, and by~\cref{claim:atmost_1_edge}
    it follows that \(B = B_f\), and so \(C\) is hit by \(\set{f} = h(B)\).
<!-- end claimproof-11 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:otl}
	There exists a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\times \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}\) such that for all integers $q,k\in \mathds{N}$ and every digraph $D$ excluding the onion-star of order $k$ as an immersion, the following holds.
	Suppose $D$ contains a subset of vertices $Z$ that is $
	\ifx\relax q,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}
                     
	\fi$-flow-connected.
	Let $z,z'\in Z$ be distinct vertices, and suppose in $D$ there is an immersion model of an out-onion-star of order $
	\ifx\relax q,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}
                     
	\fi$ rooted at~$z$.
	Then there is also an immersion model of an out-onion-star of order $q$ rooted at $z'$.
	
	Moreover, if $q=q(k)$, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm with parameter $k$ to find such a model in $D$.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Define
	\[h_k(x)\coloneqq \mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(x,
	\ifx\relax k,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,2 \right)}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}{( k,2 )}}
                     
	\fi).\]
	We set 
	\begin{align*}
		q_1&\coloneqq h_k(h_k(h_k(h_k(2q))))\\
		q_2&\coloneqq 16\cdot q_1\\
		
	\ifx\relax q,k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( q,k \right)}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}{( q,k )}}
                     
	\fi&\coloneqq q_3\coloneqq h_k(q_2).
	\end{align*}
	Let ${\cal O}=(O_i)_{i\in [q_3]}$ be the family of $q_3$ edge-disjoint onion immersion models with root at $z$ that are assumed to exist. For each $i\in [q_3]$, we let $P^1_i, P^2_i$ be the two trails from $z$ to the tip of $O_i$ within $O_i$, and $P^3_i$ be the trail back from the tip of $O_1$ to $z$ within $O_i$. By shortening the trails if necessary, we may assume that $P^1_i,P^2_i,Q_i$ are paths. For $j\in [2]$, let ${\cal P}^j$ be the set of (pairwise edge-disjoint) trails obtained by taking, for each $i\in [q_3]$, the concatenation of $P^j_i$ and $Q_i$ and removing the last edge. We similarly define families ${\cal Q}^j$ for $j\in [2]$, just from the concatenation of $P^j_i$ and $Q_i$ we remove the first edge instead of the last. Note that each trail from the families ${\cal P}^j,{\cal Q}^j$, $j\in [2]$, visits every vertex at most twice. Moreover, the trails in ${\cal P}^j$ start at $z$ and never visit $z$ again, and the trails in ${\cal Q}^j$ end at $z$ and never visit $z$ before.
	
	Since $z,z'\in Z$ and $Z$ is $q_3$-flow-connected, we may find a family ${\cal K}$ of $q_3$ edge-disjoint paths from $z$ to $z'$ and a family ${\cal L}$ of $q_3$ edge-disjoint paths from $z'$ to $z$. By applying \cref{thm:BipRamsey} (in the bipartite intersection graph of $\cal K$ and $\cal L$) and \cref{lem:onion_harvesting} to ${\cal K}$ and ${\cal L}$, we may assume that all the paths in ${\cal K}\cup {\cal L}$ are pairwise edge-disjoint, at the cost of reducing the sizes of these families to $|{\cal K}|=|{\cal L}|=q_2$. (The other conclusion of the Bipartite Ramsey Theorem (\cref{thm:BipRamsey}) yields, through \cref{lem:onion_harvesting}, an immersion model of an onion-star of order $k$, which is a contradiction.) 
	
	Next, by losing at most half of the paths of ${\cal K}$ and at most half of the onions in ${\cal O}$, we may assume that the edges that the paths of ${\cal K}$ use to leave $z$ are all different from the edges that the paths of ${\cal P}^1$ use to leave $z$. By applying this argument also for the pairs of families $({\cal K},{\cal P}^2)$, $({\cal L},{\cal Q}^1)$, and $({\cal L},{\cal Q}^2)$, we may assume that no path of ${\cal K}$ leaves $z$ using the same edge as a path from ${\cal P}^1\cup {\cal P}^2$, and no path of ${\cal L}$ enters $z$ using the same edge as a path from ${\cal Q}^1\cup {\cal Q}^2$. This is at the cost of restricting ${\cal K}$ and ${\cal L}$ to size at least $q_2/16=q_1$, and ${\cal O}$ to size at least $q_3/16\geqslant q_1$. 
	
	Now, we may apply \cref{thm:BipRamsey} (in the intersection graph) and \cref{lem:onion_harvesting} to the families ${\cal K}$ and ${\cal Q}^1$ in order to assume that the paths in ${\cal K}\cup {\cal Q}^1$ are pairwise edge-disjoint. This is at a cost of losing the inverse of $h$ in the cardinalities of ${\cal K}$ and ${\cal O}$. By performing the same for pairs of families $({\cal K},{\cal Q}^2)$, $({\cal L},{\cal P}^1)$, and $({\cal L},{\cal P}^2)$, we draw the same conclusion about those pairs. This costs restricting ${\cal K}$, ${\cal L}$, and ${\cal O}$ to subfamilies of size $2q$ each.
	
	Now, the paths of ${\cal K}$ and of ${\cal L}$ are all pairwise edge-disjoint, and also edge-disjoint from the onion immersion models in ${\cal O}$. Since $|{\cal K}|=|{\cal L}|=|{\cal O}|=2q$, one can easily match the paths of ${\cal K}$ and ${\cal L}$ with the onions of ${\cal O}$ in order to construct an immersion model of an out-onion-star of order $q$ rooted at $z'$.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:SCL_flow}
	There are functions \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi : \mathds{N}^3 \to \mathds{N}\) and \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi : \mathds{N}^2 \to \mathds{N}\) such that for every digraph \(D\) with at most \(z\) vertices of cycle-degree at least \(d\), which moreover form a \(
	\ifx\relax k,d\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}
                     
	\fi\)-flow-connected set, it holds that
	\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(Z\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
		\item \(D\) contains a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion or
		\label{item:SCL_flow:immersion}
		\item \(\ifx\relax D\relax
	\operatorname{dtcw}
	\else
	
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtcw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtcw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi\).
		\label{item:SCL_flow:decomposition}
	\end{enumerate}
	Moreover, if $d=d(k)$ and $z=z(k)$, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm with parameter $k$ to find either a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion model in $D$ or a directed tree-cut decomposition of $D$ of width at most $
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi$.

We define

    m &\coloneq 
	\ifx\relax 2k^2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else

	\fi\\
    s &\coloneq 
	\ifx\relax m, 2k^2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}

Let \(Z\) be the set of all vertices of cycle-degree at least \(d\) in \(D\).
By assumption \(\Abs{Z} \leqslant z\) and the vertices of \(Z\) are pairwise \(
	\ifx\relax k,d\relax

We apply it twice, once for \(S\)-\(T\)-in-tripods and once for \(S\)-\(T\)-out-tripods.
