		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}
                     
	\fi,z,k )}{( d,
	\ifx\relax z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}
                     
	\fi,z,k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:tripod_hitting}}}\mathchoice{\left( d,
	\ifx\relax z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}
                     
	\fi,z,k \right)}{( d,
	\ifx\relax z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}
                     
	\fi,z,k )}{( d,
	\ifx\relax z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}
                     
	\fi,z,k )}{( d,
	\ifx\relax z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:tripod-EP}}}\mathchoice{\left( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell \right)}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}{( z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell )}}
                     
	\fi,z,k )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi\).
                                                                                                                         

\paragraph*{Case 2: \(z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\) edge-disjoint in-tripods and \(z^3 \cdot d \cdot \ell\) edge-disjoint out-tripods.}

If the family of in-tripods contains $d$ in-tripods with the same centre $c$, we can extract from it a family of $d$ edge-disjoint paths in $D$ that start and end in $Z$ and go through $c$. 
Since $Z$ is $d$-flow-connected, by \cref{cor:cycleDeg-hitting} we get a contradiction with $c$ having cycle-degree smaller than $d$.
Thus, there are \(z^3 \ell\) in-tripods, and, by symmetric argument, \(z^3 \ell\) out-tripods in $D$ with distinct centres.


                   

Finding a tripod in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) yields a similar object in \(D\) as follows.
A \emph{\(Z\)-out-tripod} is the union of three edge-disjoint paths \(P_1,P_2,Q\) such that \(P_1\) and \(P_2\) start in \(Z\) and are otherwise disjoint from \(Z\), \(Q\) ends in \(Z\) and is otherwise disjoint from \(Z\) and \(
	\ifx\relax P_1\relax
		\mathsf{end}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{end}\!\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}
                     
	\fi=
	\ifx\relax P_2\relax
		\mathsf{end}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{end}\!\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}
                     
	\fi=
	\ifx\relax Q\relax
		\mathsf{start}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{start}\!\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}
                     
	\fi\), called the \emph{centre}.
The vertices \(
	\ifx\relax P_1\relax
		\mathsf{start}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{start}\!\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P_1 \right)}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}{( P_1 )}}
                     
	\fi,
	\ifx\relax P_2\relax
		\mathsf{start}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{start}\!\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}{\mathsf{start}\mathchoice{\left( P_2 \right)}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}{( P_2 )}}
                     
	\fi\) we refer to as the \emph{sources} of the \(Z\)-onion, and \(
	\ifx\relax Q\relax
		\mathsf{end}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{end}\!\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}{\mathsf{end}\mathchoice{\left( Q \right)}{( Q )}{( Q )}{( Q )}}
                     
	\fi\), we refer to as its \emph{sink}.
\emph{\(Z\)-in-tripods} are defined accordingly.


\begin{claim}
	\label{claim:PH}
	Having a packing of \(S\)-\(T\)-out-tripods of order \(k\cdot z^3\) in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) yields a packing of \(Z\)-out-tripods of order \(k\) such that they all share the same two sources and the same sink.
	The same holds for in-tripods.
\end{claim}

\begin{claimproof}
	We prove the statement for out-tripods, the arguments for in-tripods are symmetric.
	In fact, this is obtained by a rather simple pigeon-hole-argument.
	
	Let \(\mathcal{P}\) be the packing of \(S\)-\(T\)-out-tripods of order \(k\cdot z^3\).
                                                                                                            
	Each of these sources and the sink of \(P\) correspond to a vertex in \(Z\), by construction of \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	So we consider the projection \(P_Z\) of \(P\) into \(D\), replacing the edges in \(P\cap 
	\ifx\relax S\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\!\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}
                     
	\fi\) by out-edges from \(Z\) in \(D\) and the edge in \(P\cap 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) by the corresponding in-edge into \(Z\) in \(D\).
	Note that \(P_Z\) is a \(Z\)-tripod and let \(\mathcal{P}_Z = \Set{P_Z\mid P\in \mathcal{P}}\) be the packing of \(Z\)-onions obtained.
	
	To simplify the argument assume that the \(Z\)-onions have a \emph{first} and a \emph{second} source, that is, making them distinguishable in an arbitrary way.
	
	As there is only \(z\) vertices in \(Z\), there is a vertex \(z_1 \in Z\) such that \(k\cdot z^2\) members \(\mathcal{P}_1\) of \(\mathcal{P}_Z\) have \(z_1\) as their first sources.
	Similarly, there is a vertex \(z_2 \in Z\) such that \(k\cdot z\) members \(\mathcal{P}_2\) of \(\mathcal{P}_1\) have \(z_2\) as their second source.
	
	Finally, there is a vertex \(z_3 \in Z\) such that \(k\) members \(\mathcal{P}_3\) of \(\mathcal{P}_2\) have \(z_3\) as their sink.
	
	Thus, the claim holds.
\end{claimproof}
 us focus on the out-tripod packing. We will use it to exhibit a large out-onion-star
with a centre at some vertex in \(Z\).
By~\cref{claim:PH}, we may assume that we have a packing of size \(\ell\) of
\(Z\)-out-tripods, where each out-tripod has once source in \(z_1 \in Z\) and
the second source in \(z_2 \in Z\) (\(z_1\) and \(z_2\) are not necessarily distinct),
and each tripod has \(z_3 \in Z\) as its sink (again, \(z_3\) may
coincide with one or both of \(z_1,z_2\)). Moreover
all these tripods have distinct centres by construction.
Call this \(Z\)-out-tripod packing \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{out}}\).

Let us consider the following auxiliary-digraph \(D_{\mathsf{aux}}\), obtained from \(D\)
by adding a new vertex \(x\), and \(\ell\) parallel edges from \(z_1\) to \(x\),
and \(\ell\) parallel edges from \(z_2\) to \(x\).
Consider removing a set \(F \subseteq E(D_{\mathsf{aux}})\) of size smaller than \(2\ell\).
Since the in-degree of \(x\) is equal to \(2\ell\), there is an in-edge
incident to \(x\) not in \(F\). Without loss of generality,
assume this is an edge from \(z_1\) to \(x\).
As \(Z\) is at least \((2\ell)\)-flow-connected in \(D \subseteq D_{\mathsf{aux}}\), in
\(D_\mathsf{aux} - F\) there is still a \(z_3\)-\(z_1\) path.
Thus, by Menger's Theorem (c.f.~\cref{thm:menger}),
in \(D_{\mathsf{aux}}\) there is a \(z_3\)-\(x\) flow of size \(2\ell\).
This flow must correspond to exactly \(\ell\) paths from \(z_3\) to \(z_1\),
and \(\ell\) paths from \(z_3\) to \(z_2\).
Let \(\mathcal{P}_1\) be the set of \(\ell\) paths from \(z_3\) to \(z_1\),
and \(\mathcal{P}_2\) be the set of \(\ell\) paths from \(z_3\) to \(z_2\).
By construction \(\mathcal{P}_1 \cup \mathcal{P}_2\) is a set of pairwise edge-disjoint
trails all starting at \(z_3\).
If \(z_3 = z_1\), then we consider \(\mathcal{P}_1\) to be a family
of \(\ell\) trivial trails (consisting of zero edges),
and similarly for \(z_3 = z_2\) and \(\mathcal{P}_2\).

\begin{claim}
  \label{claim:uncross}
  There exists a function \(h : \mathds{N} \rightarrow \mathds{N}\) such that the following holds.

  Let \(\mathcal{T}\) be a set of edge-disjoint \(Z\)-out-tripods in \(D\),
  all of which have
  the same vertex \(z \in Z\) as sink, and let \(\mathcal{P}\) be a set of pairwise
  edge-disjoint trails in \(D\), all starting at \(z\).

  Then, for any positive integer \(n\), if
  \(\size*{\mathcal{T}} \geqslant h(n)\),
  and \(\size*{\mathcal{P}} \geqslant h(n)\),
  then either
  \begin{itemize}
    \item \(D\) contains an immersion of an onion star of order \(2k^2\), or
    \item there exists a subsets \(\mathcal{T}' \subseteq \mathcal{T}, \mathcal{P}' \subseteq \mathcal{P}\),
      each of size \(n\),
      such that each tripod in \(\mathcal{T}'\) is edge-disjoint
      from every trail in \(\mathcal{P}'\).
  \end{itemize}
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
  Set 
  \begin{equation*}
    h(n) = \mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, \mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, n)).
  \end{equation*}
  Construct two sets of trails \(\mathcal{Q}_1 = \set{Q^1_T : T \in \mathcal{T}}, \mathcal{Q}_2 = \set{Q^2_T : T \in \mathcal{T}}\) indexed by \(\mathcal{T}\)
  as follows:
  for each \(Z\)-out-tripod \(T \in \mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{out}}\) with the centre \(c \in V(D) - Z\),
  let \(L_1\) be the \(z_1\)-\(c\) path contained in \(T\) where \(z_1 \in Z\) is one
  of the sources of~\(T\),
  let \(L_2\) be the \(z_2\)-\(c\) path contained in \(T\)
  where \(z_2\) is the second source of~\(T\),
  and let \(R\) be the \(c\)-\(z_3\) trail contained in \(T\);
  add \(Q^1_T\) defined as the concatenation of \(L_1\) and \(R\) to~\(\mathcal{Q}_1\),
  and~\(Q^2_T\) the concatenation of \(L_2\) and~\(R\) to~\(\mathcal{Q}_2\).
  Thus, each of \(\set{\mathcal{Q}_1, \mathcal{Q}_2}\) is a set of pairwise edge-disjoint
  trails in \(D\) all terminating at \(z\).

  Build an auxiliary bipartite graph on \(\mathcal{T} \cup \mathcal{P}\) as follows:
  \(T \in \mathcal{T}\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) are adjacent if and only if~\(Q^1_T\) and~\(P\) share an edge.
  By~\cref{thm:BipRamsey}, either there is a complete bipartite
  subgraph of size at least \(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi\)
  or an anti-complete bipartite subgraph of size at least \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, n)\).

  Assume we are in the former case.
  Thus, we have a subset \(\mathcal{T}_c \subseteq \mathcal{T}\),
  and \(\mathcal{P}_c \subseteq \mathcal{P}\) such that for each \(T \in \mathcal{T}_c\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}_c\),
  the trails~\(Q^1_T\) and~\(P\) share an edge, and each is of size \(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi\).
  This is exactly the setup of~\cref{lem:onion_harvesting},
  and in this case we conclude that \(D\) contains an onion star immersion
  of order~\(2k^2\).

  In the latter case, we have \(\mathcal{T}_0 \subseteq \mathcal{T}\) and \(\mathcal{P}_0 \subseteq \mathcal{P}\) such that for each \(T \in \mathcal{T}_0\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}_0\),
  the trails~\(Q^1_T\) and~\(P\) are edge-disjoint,
  and each is of size \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, n)\).
  Now, build another auxiliary bipartite graph on~\(\mathcal{T}_0 \cup \mathcal{P}_0\),
  but now instead focus on \(Q_2\):
  \(T \in \mathcal{T}_0\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}_0\) are adjacent if and only if
  \(Q^2_T\) and \(P\) share an edge.

  By~\cref{thm:BipRamsey}, either there is a complete bipartite
  subgraph of size at least \(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi\)
  or a anti-complete bipartite subgraph of size at least \(n\).

  The former case unfolds exactly as in the previous
  step, with~\cref{lem:onion_harvesting} yielding a
  onion star immersion of order \(2k^2\).
  In the latter case, we obtain the desired subsets,
  as each tripod \(T\) is edge-covered by the two paths \(Q_1\) and \(Q_2\).
\end{claimproof}

Apply~\cref{claim:uncross} to \(\mathcal{P}_1\) and \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{out}}\).
These two set are each of size \(\ell = h(h(s))\).
Thus, we either obtain an onion-star of order \(2k^2\) as an immersion,
and thus also \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\),
or a subset \(\mathcal{T}' \subseteq \mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{out}}\) and \(\mathcal{P}'_1 \subseteq \mathcal{P}_1\)
each of size \(h(s)\) that are pairwise edge-disjoint.
Again, apply~\cref{claim:uncross} to \(\mathcal{P}_2\)
and \(\mathcal{T}'\).
And either we conclude with a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\) as an immersion in \(D\),
or we obtain a subset \(\mathcal{T}''\) and \(\mathcal{P}'_2 \subseteq \mathcal{P}_2\),
each of size \(s\) and pairwise edge-disjoint.

We now use \(\mathcal{T}'', \mathcal{P}'_1 \mathcal{P}'_2\) to assemble an out-onion-star
with a centre \(z_3\) and of order \(s\).
We do it as follows:
for each \(T\in \mathcal{T}''\) let \(c \in V(D) - Z\)
be the centre of \(T\), further let \(L_i\) be the \(z_i\)-\(c\)
path contained in \(T\).
Extend \(L_i\) by prepending a unique path from \(\mathcal{P}'_i\),
thus obtaining a \(z_3\)-\(c\) trail.
This is an immersion model of an onion with root at \(z_3\)
and tip at \(c\).
As all tripods have distinct centres, and all objects
we used are pairwise edge-disjoint by construction,
this is a valid immersion model of an out-onion-star of order \(s\),
with centre at a vertex \(z \in Z\).

By a symmetric argument, applied to \(\mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{in}}\),
there exists also an immersion model of an in-onion start of
order~\(s\) with centre at \(z' \in Z\).

As the set \(Z\) is at least \(s\)-connected, and \(s = 
	\ifx\relax m, 2k^2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:otl}}}\mathchoice{\left( m, 2k^2 \right)}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}{( m, 2k^2 )}}
                     
	\fi\)
by~\cref{lem:otl},
either \(D\) contains an onion star of order \(2k^2\) as an immersion, or
there is a common \(z\) that is a centre of an
of both and in-onion-star and an out-onion-star immersion models in \(D\),
each of order \(m = 
	\ifx\relax 2k^2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2 \right)}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2 \right)}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2 \right)}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:two_onion_stars}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2 \right)}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}{( 2k^2 )}}
                     
	\fi\),
and so by~\cref{lem:two_onion_stars} there is an
onion star immersion of order \(2k^2\) in \(D\).
Thus, \(D\) contains \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\).
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- claim-12: body_md -->
\label{claim:PH}
	Having a packing of \(S\)-\(T\)-out-tripods of order \(k\cdot z^3\) in \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) yields a packing of \(Z\)-out-tripods of order \(k\) such that they all share the same two sources and the same sink.
	The same holds for in-tripods.
<!-- end claim-12 -->
<!-- claimproof-12: body_md -->
We prove the statement for out-tripods, the arguments for in-tripods are symmetric.
	In fact, this is obtained by a rather simple pigeon-hole-argument.
	
	Let \(\mathcal{P}\) be the packing of \(S\)-\(T\)-out-tripods of order \(k\cdot z^3\).
                                                                                                            
	Each of these sources and the sink of \(P\) correspond to a vertex in \(Z\), by construction of \(
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\mathcal{M}_d
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathcal{M}_d\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\mathcal{M}_d\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	So we consider the projection \(P_Z\) of \(P\) into \(D\), replacing the edges in \(P\cap 
	\ifx\relax S\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\!\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}{\partial^\mathsf{out}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( S \right)}{( S )}{( S )}{( S )}}
                     
	\fi\) by out-edges from \(Z\) in \(D\) and the edge in \(P\cap 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{\partial^\mathsf{in}_{D'}\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) by the corresponding in-edge into \(Z\) in \(D\).
	Note that \(P_Z\) is a \(Z\)-tripod and let \(\mathcal{P}_Z = \Set{P_Z\mid P\in \mathcal{P}}\) be the packing of \(Z\)-onions obtained.
	
	To simplify the argument assume that the \(Z\)-onions have a \emph{first} and a \emph{second} source, that is, making them distinguishable in an arbitrary way.
	
	As there is only \(z\) vertices in \(Z\), there is a vertex \(z_1 \in Z\) such that \(k\cdot z^2\) members \(\mathcal{P}_1\) of \(\mathcal{P}_Z\) have \(z_1\) as their first sources.
	Similarly, there is a vertex \(z_2 \in Z\) such that \(k\cdot z\) members \(\mathcal{P}_2\) of \(\mathcal{P}_1\) have \(z_2\) as their second source.
	
	Finally, there is a vertex \(z_3 \in Z\) such that \(k\) members \(\mathcal{P}_3\) of \(\mathcal{P}_2\) have \(z_3\) as their sink.
	
	Thus, the claim holds.
<!-- end claimproof-12 -->
<!-- claim-13: body_md -->
\label{claim:uncross}
  There exists a function \(h : \mathds{N} \rightarrow \mathds{N}\) such that the following holds.

  Let \(\mathcal{T}\) be a set of edge-disjoint \(Z\)-out-tripods in \(D\),
  all of which have
  the same vertex \(z \in Z\) as sink, and let \(\mathcal{P}\) be a set of pairwise
  edge-disjoint trails in \(D\), all starting at \(z\).

  Then, for any positive integer \(n\), if
  \(\size*{\mathcal{T}} \geqslant h(n)\),
  and \(\size*{\mathcal{P}} \geqslant h(n)\),
  then either
  \begin{itemize}
    \item \(D\) contains an immersion of an onion star of order \(2k^2\), or
    \item there exists a subsets \(\mathcal{T}' \subseteq \mathcal{T}, \mathcal{P}' \subseteq \mathcal{P}\),
      each of size \(n\),
      such that each tripod in \(\mathcal{T}'\) is edge-disjoint
      from every trail in \(\mathcal{P}'\).
  \end{itemize}
<!-- end claim-13 -->
<!-- claimproof-13: body_md -->
Set 
  \begin{equation*}
    h(n) = \mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, \mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, n)).
  \end{equation*}
  Construct two sets of trails \(\mathcal{Q}_1 = \set{Q^1_T : T \in \mathcal{T}}, \mathcal{Q}_2 = \set{Q^2_T : T \in \mathcal{T}}\) indexed by \(\mathcal{T}\)
  as follows:
  for each \(Z\)-out-tripod \(T \in \mathcal{T}_{\mathsf{out}}\) with the centre \(c \in V(D) - Z\),
  let \(L_1\) be the \(z_1\)-\(c\) path contained in \(T\) where \(z_1 \in Z\) is one
  of the sources of~\(T\),
  let \(L_2\) be the \(z_2\)-\(c\) path contained in \(T\)
  where \(z_2\) is the second source of~\(T\),
  and let \(R\) be the \(c\)-\(z_3\) trail contained in \(T\);
  add \(Q^1_T\) defined as the concatenation of \(L_1\) and \(R\) to~\(\mathcal{Q}_1\),
  and~\(Q^2_T\) the concatenation of \(L_2\) and~\(R\) to~\(\mathcal{Q}_2\).
  Thus, each of \(\set{\mathcal{Q}_1, \mathcal{Q}_2}\) is a set of pairwise edge-disjoint
  trails in \(D\) all terminating at \(z\).

  Build an auxiliary bipartite graph on \(\mathcal{T} \cup \mathcal{P}\) as follows:
  \(T \in \mathcal{T}\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}\) are adjacent if and only if~\(Q^1_T\) and~\(P\) share an edge.
  By~\cref{thm:BipRamsey}, either there is a complete bipartite
  subgraph of size at least \(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi\)
  or an anti-complete bipartite subgraph of size at least \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, n)\).

  Assume we are in the former case.
  Thus, we have a subset \(\mathcal{T}_c \subseteq \mathcal{T}\),
  and \(\mathcal{P}_c \subseteq \mathcal{P}\) such that for each \(T \in \mathcal{T}_c\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}_c\),
  the trails~\(Q^1_T\) and~\(P\) share an edge, and each is of size \(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi\).
  This is exactly the setup of~\cref{lem:onion_harvesting},
  and in this case we conclude that \(D\) contains an onion star immersion
  of order~\(2k^2\).

  In the latter case, we have \(\mathcal{T}_0 \subseteq \mathcal{T}\) and \(\mathcal{P}_0 \subseteq \mathcal{P}\) such that for each \(T \in \mathcal{T}_0\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}_0\),
  the trails~\(Q^1_T\) and~\(P\) are edge-disjoint,
  and each is of size \(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{bip}}(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi, n)\).
  Now, build another auxiliary bipartite graph on~\(\mathcal{T}_0 \cup \mathcal{P}_0\),
  but now instead focus on \(Q_2\):
  \(T \in \mathcal{T}_0\) and \(P \in \mathcal{P}_0\) are adjacent if and only if
  \(Q^2_T\) and \(P\) share an edge.

  By~\cref{thm:BipRamsey}, either there is a complete bipartite
  subgraph of size at least \(
	\ifx\relax 2k^2,2\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:onion_harvesting}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k^2,2 \right)}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}{( 2k^2,2 )}}
                     
	\fi\)
  or a anti-complete bipartite subgraph of size at least \(n\).

  The former case unfolds exactly as in the previous
  step, with~\cref{lem:onion_harvesting} yielding a
  onion star immersion of order \(2k^2\).
  In the latter case, we obtain the desired subsets,
  as each tripod \(T\) is edge-covered by the two paths \(Q_1\) and \(Q_2\).
<!-- end claimproof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:SCL}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi \colon \mathds{N}^3 \to \mathds{N}\) such that for every $k\in \mathds{N}$ and a digraph \(D\) with at most \(z\) vertices of cycle-degree at least \(d\), it holds that
	\begin{enumerate}[label=\textbf{(S\arabic*)},leftmargin=1.2cm]
		\item \(D\) contains a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion or
		\label{item:SCL:immersion}
		\item \(\ifx\relax D\relax
	\operatorname{dtcw}
	\else
	
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		\operatorname{dtcw}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\operatorname{dtcw}\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{\operatorname{dtcw}\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi
	\fi \leqslant 
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi\).
		\label{item:SCL:decomposition}
	\end{enumerate}
	Moreover, if $d=d(k)$ and $z=z(k)$, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm with parameter $k$ to find either a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion model in $D$ or a directed tree-cut decomposition of $D$ of width at most $
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi$.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Fix
	\begin{equation*}
		\ell\coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax k,d\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}
                     
	\fi.
	\end{equation*}
	Let \(Z\) be the set of vertices of cycle-degree at least \(d\) in \(D\); then \(|Z|\leqslant z\).
	
	We construct a set of edges \(W\) in \(D\) by the following iterative procedure.
	\begin{itemize}
		\item Start with \(W\coloneqq \emptyset\).
		\item Find a minimal set \(F\) such that there exist distinct $a,b \in Z$ and an $a$-$b$-path in $D-W$, but no $a$-$b$-path in $(D-W)-F$; if $|F| < \ell$, add $F$ to $W$ and proceed to the next iteration, otherwise $W$ is the final set.  
		                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
	\end{itemize}
	Observe that every ordered pair \((a,b)\in Z^2\) is considered at most once in this procedure, because once a set \(F\) intersecting every \(a\)-\(b\)-path is added to \(W\), the condition that there exists an \(a\)-\(b\)-path in \(D-W\) will never hold again.
	Thus, the procedure eventually constructs a set \(W\)~with \[|W|\leqslant z(z-1)(\ell-1)\] elements,
	that, by Menger's Theorem (\cref{thm:menger}), satisfies the following assertion:
	\begin{quote}
		For every pair of distinct vertices \(a,b\in Z\), either there is no \(a\)-\(b\)-path in \(D-W\), or there is an \(a\)-\(b\)-flow of order \(\ell\) in \(D-W\).
	\end{quote}
	
	\newcommand{{\cal C}}{{\cal C}}
	
	Let \({\cal C}\) be the family of strongly connected components of \(D-W\). Note that for each \(A\in {\cal C}\), the set \(Z^A\coloneqq V(A)\cap Z\) is \(\ell\)-flow-connected in \(A\). Also, \(|Z^A|\leqslant |Z|\leqslant z\) and all the vertices of \(A\) lying outside of \(Z^A\) have cycle-degrees smaller than \(d\). Therefore, we may apply \cref{lem:SCL_flow} to \(A\) and \(Z^A\), thus obtaining either a \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion model in \(A\) or a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A \right)}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}\) of \(A\) of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi\). If the first case occurs for any \(A\in {\cal C}\), then we have exposed an \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\)-immersion model in \(D\), which is conclusion \cref{item:SCL:immersion}. So from now we may assume that we have constructed a suitable decomposition \(\mathchoice{\left( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A \right)}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}\) for each \(A\in {\cal C}\), and we work towards conclusion~\cref{item:SCL:decomposition}.
	
	We combine the decompositions \(\Set{\mathchoice{\left( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A \right)}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}\colon A\in {\cal C}}\) into a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) of $D$ as follows:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item For each \(A\in {\cal C}\), select an arbitrary node \(r^A\) of \(T^A\). Construct \(T\) by taking the disjoint union of all the trees \(\Set{T^A\colon A\in {\cal C}}\), adding a fresh node $r$, and making it adjacent to all the nodes \(r^A\) for \(A\in {\cal C}\).
		\item Set \(\beta(r)=\emptyset\), \(\theta(r)=W\), and \(\gamma(rr^A)=W\) for every \(A\in {\cal C}\).
		\item For each \(A\in {\cal C}\), node \(x\) of \(T^A\), and edge \(e\) of \(T^A\), set $\theta(x)\coloneqq \theta^A(x)\cup W$ and $\gamma(e)\coloneqq \gamma^A(e)\cup W$.
	\end{itemize}
	Clearly, the width of \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) is bounded by \[
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi+|W|\leqslant 
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi+z(z-1)(\ell-1)= 
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi+z(z-1)(
	\ifx\relax k,d\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{lem:SCL_flow}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d \right)}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}{( k,d )}}
                     
	\fi-1).\]
	So, to reach conclusion \cref{item:SCL:decomposition}, set to the right hand side of the above to \(
	\ifx\relax k,d,z\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( k,d,z \right)}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}{( k,d,z )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	It suffices to argue that \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) is indeed a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of \(D\).
	
	It is clear from the construction that \(\{\beta(x)\colon x\in V(T)\}\) is a near-partition of the vertex set of \(D\).
	That \(W\) is an onion-guard for the node \(r\) and also an edge-guard for the edge \(rr^A\), for each \(A\in {\cal C}\), follows immediately from the observation that there is no directed cycle visiting two subgraphs of \(D-W\) induced by bags of different components of $T - r$.
	
	Next, consider an edge \(e\) of \(T^A\) for some \(A\in {\cal C}\).
	Observe that for every cycle \(C\) in \(D\) that crosses \(e\) (treated as an edge of \(T\)), either \(C\) also crosses the edge \(rr^A\) and consequently is intersected by \(W\), or \(C\) resides entirely within \(A\) and consequently is intersected by \(\gamma^A(e)\), due to this set being an edge-guard for \(e\) in \(\mathchoice{\left( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A \right)}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}{( T^A,\beta^A,\gamma^A,\theta^A )}\).
	In conclusion, \(\gamma(e)=\gamma^A(e)\cup W\) is a suitable edge-guard for $e$ in \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\).
	An analogous argument shows that \(\theta(x)=\theta^A(x)\cup W\) is a suitable onion-guard for any node \(x\) of \(T^A\).
	
	Last, the existence of the desired algorithm follows directly from the proof.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:onion_star}
	There is a function \(
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi \colon \mathds{N} \to \mathds{N}\) such that for every digraph \(D\) and $p\in \mathds{N}$, the following holds: If \(D\) contains a \(
	\ifx\relax p\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( p \right)}{( p )}{( p )}{( p )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( p \right)}{( p )}{( p )}{( p )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( p \right)}{( p )}{( p )}{( p )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( p \right)}{( p )}{( p )}{( p )}}
                     
	\fi\)-flow-connected set of size $2p$, then \(D\) also contains an immersion model of the onion-star of order $p$.
	
	Moreover, there is a fixed-parameter algorithm (with respect to parameter $p$) that given $D$ and a flow-connected set as above, finds an immersion model of the onion-star of order $p$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:quasi_onions}
	Let \(D\) be a digraph with a given set of edges \(F\) that forms a matching (i.e.~they do not share any endpoints) and a bipartition \((X,Y)\) of its vertices such that no edge of \(F\) crosses \((X,Y)\).
	Then every minimal \((F,(X,Y))\)-quasi-onion with root \(u \in X\) and tip \(v \in Y\) is an immersion model of an onion.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Let \(P_1\) and \(P_2\) be the two \(u\)-\(v\)-trails and \(P_3\) be the \(v\)-\(u\)-trail of a minimal \((F,(X,Y))\)-quasi-onion with root \(u \in X\) and tip \(v \in Y\).
	Note that the minimality asserts that $P_1,P_2$ and $P_3$ are paths. Also, note that since $P_1,P_2,P_3$ constitute a \((F,(X,Y))\)-quasi-onion, every edge shared by two of those trails must have both endpoints in $X$ (\emph{lie} in $X$) or both endpoints in $Y$ (\emph{lie} in $Y$).
	
	First we show that \(P_1\) is disjoint from \(P_2\).
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    
                                                                                 
                                                           
	Assume otherwise and consider the first edge \(f \in F\) along \(P_1\) that it shares with \(P_2\).
	If \(f\) lies in \(Y\), then we define \(v' \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) as the new tip and \(P'_1 \coloneqq P_1v'\), \(P'_2 \coloneqq P_2v'\) and \(P'_3 \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax v'P_2 \cdot P_3\relax
		\mathsf{red}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{red}\!\mathchoice{\left( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 \right)}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 \right)}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 \right)}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 \right)}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}{( v'P_2 \cdot P_3 )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	This way the overall number of edges drops by the number of edges in \(v'P_1\) which is at least 1, as it contains \(f\).                                            
	The paths \(P'_1,P'_2\) and \(P'_3\) therefore yield a smaller $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion with root \(u\) and tip \(v'\), a contradiction.
	
	If \(f\) lies in \(X\), then we define \(u' \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) as the new root.
	Then, we define \(P'_1 \coloneqq u'P_1\), \(P'_2 \coloneqq u'P_2\) and \(P'_3 \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax P_3 \cdot P_1u\relax
		\mathsf{red}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{red}\!\mathchoice{\left( P_3 \cdot P_1u \right)}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( P_3 \cdot P_1u \right)}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( P_3 \cdot P_1u \right)}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( P_3 \cdot P_1u \right)}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}{( P_3 \cdot P_1u )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	Again, we obtain a smaller $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion (by the number of edges in \(P_2u'\), in particular $f$) with root \(u'\) and tip \(v\), a contradiction.
	
	So, we may assume that the paths \(P_1\) and \(P_2\) are indeed edge-disjoint.
	We continue to consider the possibility of \(P_i\) intersecting \(P_3\) for \(i \in [2]\).
	Let \(j \coloneqq 3-i\).

	Consider the first intersection edge \(f \in F\) along \(P_i\) with \(P_3\).
	If \(f\) lies in \(Y\), then we define \(v' \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) as the new tip.
	Then, we choose \(P'_i \coloneqq P_iv'\), \(P'_j \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax P_j \cdot P_3v'\relax
		\mathsf{red}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{red}\!\mathchoice{\left( P_j \cdot P_3v' \right)}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( P_j \cdot P_3v' \right)}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( P_j \cdot P_3v' \right)}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( P_j \cdot P_3v' \right)}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}{( P_j \cdot P_3v' )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(P'_3 \coloneqq v'P_3\).
	This yields a smaller $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion, a contradiction.
	
	If \(f\) lies in \(X\), then we define \(u' \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax f\relax
		\mathsf{head}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{head}\!\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}{\mathsf{head}\mathchoice{\left( f \right)}{( f )}{( f )}{( f )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(P'_i \coloneqq u'P_i\), \(P'_j \coloneqq 
	\ifx\relax u'P_3\cdot P_j\relax
		\mathsf{red}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{red}\!\mathchoice{\left( u'P_3\cdot P_j \right)}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( u'P_3\cdot P_j \right)}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( u'P_3\cdot P_j \right)}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}}{\mathsf{red}\mathchoice{\left( u'P_3\cdot P_j \right)}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}{( u'P_3\cdot P_j )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(P'_3 \coloneqq P_3u'\).
	This again would yield a smaller $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion, a contradiction.
	
	So, indeed, the three paths \(P_1,P_2\) and \(P_3\) are all edge-disjoint and thus form an actual immersion model of an onion.

Throughout the proof we argue the combinatorial statement. At the end, we argue how the reasoning can be adapted to yield a fixed-parameter algorithm.
	
	If \(D\) contains an immersion model of \(\raisebox{2pt}{\scalerel*{\begin{tikzpicture}
				\path[use as bounding box] (-0,-0.55) rectangle (0.53,0.0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend left] (0.5,0);
				\draw (-0,0) to[bend right] (0.5,0);
				\draw (0,0) to (0,-0.5);
				\draw (0.5,0) to (0.5,-0.5);
				\draw (-0,-0.5) to[bend right] (0.5,-0.5);
		\end{tikzpicture}}{()}}_{k}\), then we are done, so we can assume it does not.
