	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi )}{( 
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi )}{( 
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lemma:potato}}}\mathchoice{\left( 
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi \right)}{( 
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi )}{( 
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi )}{( 
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	As for every $x \in V(T)$ the bag $\widehat{\beta}(x)$ is $
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{h}_{\ref{lem:onion_star}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi$-flow-connected, we have $|\widehat{\beta}(x)| \leqslant 2q$.
	
	We root $\widehat{T}$ in an arbitrary node.
	By~\cref{lem:rooting_potdecomps} we can assume that \(\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} \right)}{( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} )}{( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} )}{( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} )}\) is coherent\xspace.
	This allows us to build a torso \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) for every \(x \in 
	\ifx\relax \widehat{T}\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}
                     
	\fi\), as described in~\cref{sec:torso}.
	
	Note that since all auxiliary vertices of \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) have cycle-degree exactly one, \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) contains at most~\(2q\) vertices of cycle-degree at least two, i.e.~the original vertices of \(\widehat{\beta}(x)\).
	
	Therefore, we can apply~\cref{lem:SCL} with parameter $2k$ and, as by~\cref{lem:grid_imm_in_torsos} we cannot get~\cref{item:SCL:immersion}, it yields~\cref{item:SCL:decomposition}, that is, a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathcal{T}_x^0\coloneqq\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x,\beta^0_x,\gamma^0_x,\theta^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x,\beta^0_x,\gamma^0_x,\theta^0_x )}{( T^0_x,\beta^0_x,\gamma^0_x,\theta^0_x )}{( T^0_x,\beta^0_x,\gamma^0_x,\theta^0_x )}\) of \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax 2k,2,2q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	
	In order to be able to combine these decompositions into one decomposition of the entire graph, we first tidy it up a bit, ensuring that auxiliary vertices for the same subtree are placed into the same bag. To achieve this, we pay a multiplicative factor \(3\) in the width.
	
	\begin{claim}
		\label{claim:aux-vertices-together}
		We can construct a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathcal{T}_x\coloneqq \mathchoice{\left( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x \right)}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}\) from \(\mathcal{T}_x^0\) such that for every pair \(a_i\) and \(b_i\) of auxiliary vertices, both of them lie in the same bag. The width of $\mathcal{T}_x$ is at most \(3\cdot 
	\ifx\relax 2k,2,2q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
	If $x$ is a leaf of $\widehat{T}$, there is nothing to prove, so assume that $y_1,\ldots, y_\ell$ are the children of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$. 
		For every $i \in [\ell]$, let \(t_i \in 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) be such that \(a_i \in \beta^0_x(t_i)\) and let \(t'_i \in 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) be such that \(b_i \in \beta^0_x(t'_i)\).
		Let \(P_i\) be the unique \(t_i\)-\(t'_i\)-path in \(T^0_x\).
		
		Recall that we denote by $O_i$ the onion consisting of the three edges between $a_i$ and $b_i$.

	\begin{claim}
		\label{claim:edge_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}
		If \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\), then \(\gamma^0_x(e)\) contains at least one of the auxiliary edges \(
	\ifx\relax O_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		This follows from \(O_i\) containing a cycle that visits \(a_i\) and \(b_i\), which  crosses \(e\) and thus has to be hit by the edge-guard \(\gamma^0_x(e)\).
	\end{claimproof}
	
	\begin{claim}
		\label{claim:onion_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}
		If \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi-\Set{t_i,t'_i}\), then \(\theta^0_x(t)\) contains at least one of the auxiliary edges \(
	\ifx\relax O_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		This follows from \(O_i\) containing an onion with tip \(a_i\) and root \(b_i\), which crosses \(t\) and thus has to be hit by the onion-guard \(\theta^0_x(t)\).
	\end{claimproof}
		
		We define \(T_x \coloneqq T^0_x\).
		Then, for every \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) we define 
		\begin{equation*}
			\beta_x(t) \coloneqq \beta^0_x(t)-\Set{b_i\mid a_i \not\in \beta^0_x(t)} \cup \Set{b_i\mid a_i \in \beta^0_x(t).}
		\end{equation*}
		That is, we move each auxiliary vertex \(b_i\) to the bag containing \(a_i\).
		This increases the size of the bags at most by a factor of two.
		
		
		For every \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) we define
		\begin{equation*}
			\gamma_x(e) \coloneqq \gamma^0_x(e) \cup \Set{\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}\mid e\in
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi}.
		\end{equation*}
		The size of the edge-guard of $e$ grows by the number of paths \(P_i\) the edge $e$ lies on.
	By~\cref{claim:edge_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}, these are at most as many as the original edge-guard size.
	So, the edge-guards grow by at most a factor two.
		
		Similarly, for every \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) we define
		\begin{equation*}
			\theta_x(t) \coloneqq \theta^0_x(t) \cup \Set{\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}\mid t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi}.
	\end{equation*}
	Each onion-guard grows by one edge for every \(P_i\) its corresponding node $t$ in $T_x=T_x^0$ lies on.
	Thus, if we denote by $n_i$ and $n_e$, respectively, the number of paths $P_i$ that contain $t$ as an internal vertex, and as an endpoint, we have $|\theta_x(t)| \leqslant |\theta^0_x(t)| + n_i + n_e$. 
	If $t$ is an internal node of \(P_i\), by~\cref{claim:onion_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}, $\theta_x^0(t)$ already contains an edge from \(O_i\), thus $n_i \leqslant |\theta^0_x(t)|$.
	Note that $t$ can be only an endpoint of \(P_i\) if $\beta^0_x(t)$ contains \(a_i\) or \(b_i\). Therefore, $n_e \leqslant |\beta^0_x(t)|$.
	Thus, the new onion-guards are at most twice their original size plus the maximum bag-size, i.e.~three times the width of $\mathcal{T}^0_x$.
	
	It remains to show that both the new edge-guards and the new onion-guards are doing their job in hitting all crossing cycles or onions.
	Let \(e\in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) be an edge in the decomposition and \(C\) be a cycle of \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) crossing \(e\).
	If \(C\) is a cycle crossing \(e\) in \(\mathcal{T}^0_x\) as well, then it is hit by \(\gamma^0_x(e) \subseteq \gamma_x(e)\).
	If \(C\) does not cross \(e\) in \(\mathcal{T}^0_x\), then there is \(i \in [\ell]\) such that \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(C\) visits \(b_i\). Thus, $C$ contains the edge \(\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}\), which ensures that $C$ is hit by \(\gamma_x(e)\).
	
	For the onion-guards, let \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) and assume without loss of generality hat \(\theta^0_x(t)\) hits all in-onions of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}_x^0$.
	Let \(O\) be an in-onion of \(t\) in \(\mathcal{T}_x\) (thus $O$ is an onion immersion model in $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$).
	If \(O\) is also an in-onion of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}_x^0$, then it is hit by \(\theta^0_x(t) \subseteq \theta_x(t)\).
	If \(O\) only became an in-onion of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}_x$ due to moving the auxiliary vertices, then there is \(i \in [\ell]\) such that \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(b_i\) is the root or the tip of \(O\).
	In particular, $O$ must contain the edge $(a_i,b_i)$, and hence $O$ is hit by \(\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )} \in \theta_x(t)\).
\end{claimproof}

Based on the cleaned-up directed tree-cut-decompositions\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x \right)}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}\) of every torso \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) of the tree-partition \(\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} \right)}{( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} )}{( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} )}{( \widehat{T},\widehat{\beta},\widehat{\gamma} )}\), we now construct a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathchoice{\left( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta \right)}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}{( T,\beta,\gamma,\theta )}\) of the entire graph~\(D\).

\paragraph{Constructing the decomposition tree:}
We start with the union of the trees from all the torso decompositions: \(T'' \coloneqq \bigcup_{x \in 
	\ifx\relax \widehat{T}\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}
                     
	\fi} T_x\).
This yields a forest and we want to connect it into a single tree.
By \cref{claim:aux-vertices-together}, the pairs of auxiliary vertices always lie in the same bag, which we make use of for deciding how to connect the components of \(T''\).

For every vertex \(x \in 
	\ifx\relax \widehat{T}\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}
                     
	\fi\), we add exactly one edge between \(T_x\) and \(T_{y_i}\) for every child \(y_i\) of \(x\) in \(\widehat{T}\), so that the resulting graph \(T\) is a tree, as follows.
As \(y_i\) is a child of \(x\), there is a node \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) such that the bag \(\beta_x(t)\) contains both \(a_i\) and \(b_i\).
We add an edge between \(t\) and an arbitrary node of \(T_{y_i}\).
These edges correspond one-to-one to the edges of \(\widehat{T}\) and we refer to them as \emph{outer edges} of \(T\), while we call the remaining edges, which come from inside the torso decompositions, the \emph{inner edges} of \(T\).

                                                                                                                                               
                                       


\paragraph{Constructing the bags:}
As every node \(t\) of \(T\) is also a node of some \(T_x\) for some \(x \in \widehat{T}\), there is a bag \(\beta_x(t)\).
This bag potentially contains some auxiliary vertices of the torso \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\), thus we define \(\beta(t) \coloneqq \beta_x(t) \cap 
	\ifx\relax D\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( D \right)}{( D )}{( D )}{( D )}}
                     
	\fi\) in order to remove these.
Clearly the bags do not grow in size with doing this.

\paragraph{Constructing the edge-guards:}
We distinguish between the outer and the inner edges of \(T\).
If \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) is an inner edge of \(T\), then there is a node \(x \in 
	\ifx\relax \widehat{T}\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}
                     
	\fi\) such that \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\). Thus, there is an edge-guard \(\gamma_x(e)\) assigned to it.
Let \(x'\) and $y_1,\ldots,y_\ell$ be, respectively, the parent and the children of $x \in \widehat{T}$ (if they exist).  
We define                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         
\begin{align*}
	\gamma(e) \coloneqq{} &\big(\mathsf{origin}_{x}(\gamma_x(e) \cap 
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}\relax
		\mathsf{dom}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{dom}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}
                     
	\fi)\big) \cup{}\\
	&\widehat{\gamma}(x',x) \cup{}\\
	&\bigcup \Set{\widehat{\gamma}(x,y_i) \mid i\in [\ell]\text{ and }E(O_i)\cap \gamma_x(e)\neq \emptyset }.
\end{align*}
In other words, $\gamma(e)$ consists of the origins of every edge from $\gamma_x(e)$ that is not an auxiliary edge, 
the edge-guard of $(x',x) \in E(\widehat{T})$, 
and edge-guards of $(x,y_i) \in E(\widehat{T})$ for every $i \in [\ell]$ for which $\gamma_x(e)$ contains an edge of the onion $O_i$.

Next, consider an outer edge \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) between a node of \(T_x\) and a node of \(T_{x'}\), with $x'$ being the parent of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$.
As $e$ corresponds to an edge in \(\widehat{T}\), we make its edge-guard inherit the edge-guard from the tree-partition decomposition\xspace, i.e., set \(\gamma(e) \coloneqq \widehat{\gamma}(x',x)\).
See~\cref{fig:edge_guards} for an illustration of the edge-guards as well as the construction of the tree for the directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace.

	\fi \right)}{(

	\fi )}{(

\begin{claim}
	For every \(e = tt' \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\), the edge set \(\gamma(e)\) is indeed an edge-guard for \(e\), that is, it hits all cycles visiting both vertices from bags of \({T}^{t}_{e}\) and of \({T}^{t'}_{e}\).
\end{claim}
\begin{claimproof}
	Let \(e = tt'\in E(T)\). If $e$ is an outer edge, for some $(x, x') \in E(\widehat{T})$ such that $t \in V(T_x)$, and $t' \in V(T_{x'})$, we have \(\beta({T}^{t}_{e}) = \widehat{\beta}({{\widehat{T}}}^{x}_{e})\) and \(\beta({T}^{t'}_{e}) = \widehat{\beta}({{\widehat{T}}}^{x'}_{e})\), so the statement follows because $\gamma(e)=\widehat\gamma(x,x')$.
	
	Suppose now $e$ is an inner edge. Let $x$ be such that $t,t' \in V(T_x)$, and \(C\) be a cycle visiting vertices from the bags of both \({T}^{t}_{e}\) and of \({T}^{t'}_{e}\).
	
	If \(C\) visits a vertex that lies in \(\widehat{\beta}({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\), then it has to contain an edge from \(\widehat{\gamma}\mathchoice{\left( x',x \right)}{( x',x )}{( x',x )}{( x',x )}\), where \(x'\) is the parent of \(x\), by~\cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, so it is hit by \(\widehat{\gamma}\mathchoice{\left( x',x \right)}{( x',x )}{( x',x )}{( x',x )} \subseteq \gamma(e)\).
	
	                                                                                              
	
	So we can assume that $C$ does not visit vertices from \(\widehat{\beta}({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\).
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               
	We consider a subgraph of $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$ induced by all edges of the form $\mathsf{origin}^{-1}_x(e)$, for every edge $e \in E(C)$ for which $\mathsf{origin}^{-1}_x(e)$ exists, and edges $(a_i,b_i)$ for every child $y_i$ of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$ such that $C$ intersects $\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y_i})$.
	This subgraph includes a cycle $C'$ that, if there exists $y_i$ such that $C$ intersects $\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y_i})$, contains the auxiliary edge $(a_i,b_i)$. If there is no such $y_i$, consider $C'=C$, as $C$ is a cycle in $D[\widehat\beta(x)]\subseteq \mathsf{tor}(D,x)$.
	
	As \(C'\) is a cycle in \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) that crosses \(e \in E(T_x)\), it is hit by \(\gamma_x(e)\).
	If $C'$ is hit by an edge \((a_i,b_i) \in \gamma_x(e)\), then we have \(C \cap \widehat{\gamma}(x,y_i) \neq \emptyset\) and thus, \(C\) is hit by \(\widehat{\gamma}(x,y_i)\subseteq \gamma(e)\).
	Otherwise \(C\) is hit by \(\mathsf{origin}_{x}(\gamma_x(e) \cap 
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}\relax
		\mathsf{dom}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{dom}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}
                     
	\fi) \subseteq \gamma(e)\).
\end{claimproof}

\paragraph{Constructing the onion-guards:}
For every node \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) there also is some \(T_x\) with \(x \in 
	\ifx\relax \widehat{T}\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \widehat{T} \right)}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}{( \widehat{T} )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(t \in T_x\) and therefore there exists an onion-guard \(\theta_x(t)\) in the directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of the torso \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\).
Also, let \(x'\) be the parent of \(x\) in \(\widehat{T}\) (unless \(x\) is the root).
We define the onion-guard of $t$ as follows:
\begin{align*}
	\theta(t) \coloneqq{} &\big(\mathsf{origin}_{x}(\theta_x(t) \cap 
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}\relax
		\mathsf{dom}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{dom}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}
                     
	\fi)\big) \cup{}\\
	&\widehat{\gamma}(x',x)  \cup{}\\
	&\bigcup\Set{\widehat{\gamma}(x,c_i) \mid i\in [\ell] \text{ and }\theta_x(t)\cap E(O_i)\neq \emptyset}.
\end{align*}

	All that is left to prove is that the new onion-guards do indeed hit the onions. Denote $\mathcal{T}=(T,\beta,\gamma,\theta)$.
	
	\begin{claim}
		For every \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) the set \(\theta(t)\) hits all in-onions or all out-onions of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}$.
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		Let \(x \in V(\widehat{T})\) be such that \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\).
		Note that each onion immersion model for which either root or tip is outside of the bags of the subtree of $\widehat{T}$ rooted at \(x\), and the other one is inside, is hit by \(\widehat{\gamma}(x',x)\), where $x'$ is the parent of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$.
		So we only need to consider those onions that completely live in the subtree of $\widehat{T}$ rooted at \(x\).
		For these we make use of the Quasi-onion Lemma~(\cref{lem:quasi_onions}).
		
		Assume in the decomposition $\mathcal{T}_x$ of $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$, the onion-guard \(\theta_x(t)\) hits all out-onions of \(t\).
		We aim to show that then \(\theta(t)\) also hits all out-onions of \(t\) in $\mathcal{T}$.
		Let \(O\) be an out-onion of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}$ (so $O$ is an onion immersion model in $D$) and let \(P_1,P_2,P_3\) be the three trails forming it.
		Let \(F \coloneqq \Set{(a_i,b_i) \mid y_i \text{ is a child of } x}\).
		As the mapping $\mathsf{origin}_{x}$ is a bijection, passing $P_1,P_2,P_3$ through $\mathsf{origin}_{x}$ and augmenting them with the edges of $F$, if necessary, naturally yields an \((F,(X,Y))\)-quasi-onion \(O'\) in \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\), for any cut $(X,Y)$ such that the root is in $X$ and the tip is in $Y$.
		
		If the root of \(O\) lies within \(\beta(t)\), then consider the bipartition \((X,Y) \coloneqq (\beta(t), 
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{tor}(D,x)\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}
                     
	\fi-\beta(t))\).
		Otherwise, let \(t'\) be the neighbour of \(t\) such that the bags of the subtree \((T_x)^{tt'}_{t'}\) contain the root of \(O\), then consider the bipartition \((X,Y) \coloneqq (\beta_x((T_x)^{tt'}_{t'}),
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{tor}(D,x)\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}
                     
	\fi-\beta_x((T_x)^{tt'}_{t'}))\). Note that no edge of $F$ crosses the cut $(X,Y)$, and hence $O'$ is an $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion.
		
		Now let \(O''\) be the minimal $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion contained in the union of the paths of \(O'\) that has its root in $X$ and tip in $Y$.
		By~\cref{lem:quasi_onions}, \(O''\) is an onion and in particular, by our choice of \((X,Y)\), an out-onion of \(t\) in $\mathcal{T}_x$. Therefore, $O''$ is hit by \(\theta_x(t)\).
		
		If the intersection of \(O''\) and \(\theta_x(t)\) contains an edge $e$ that is not an auxiliary edge in $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$, then $\mathsf{origin}_{x}(e)$ also lies in \(\theta(t)\) and hits \(O\).
		Otherwise, \(O\) is hit by the set \(\bigcup\Set{\widehat{\gamma}(x,c_i) \mid i\in [\ell] \text{ and }\theta_x(t)\cap E(O_i)\neq \emptyset}\), which was included in $\theta(t)$.
	\end{claimproof}
	
	We conclude that \(\mathcal{T}=(T,\beta,\gamma,\theta)\) is indeed a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace of \(D\) of width at most \(
	\ifx\relax k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}}}\mathchoice{\left( k \right)}{( k )}{( k )}{( k )}}
                     
	\fi\). This finishes the proof of the combinatorial bound.
	
	
	Finally, it is straightforward to verify that all the steps of the construction can be performed in fixed-parameter time, parameterized by $k$. This, together with the algorithmic statements asserted in \cref{lemma:potato,lem:SCL,lem:onion_star}, proves the algorithmic statement of \cref{thm:grid_or_small_dtcw}.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- claim-14: body_md -->
\label{claim:aux-vertices-together}
		We can construct a directed tree-cut-decomposition\xspace \(\mathcal{T}_x\coloneqq \mathchoice{\left( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x \right)}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}{( T_x,\beta_x,\gamma_x,\theta_x )}\) from \(\mathcal{T}_x^0\) such that for every pair \(a_i\) and \(b_i\) of auxiliary vertices, both of them lie in the same bag. The width of $\mathcal{T}_x$ is at most \(3\cdot 
	\ifx\relax 2k,2,2q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{lem:SCL}}}\mathchoice{\left( 2k,2,2q \right)}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}{( 2k,2,2q )}}
                     
	\fi\).
<!-- end claim-14 -->
<!-- claimproof-14: body_md -->
If $x$ is a leaf of $\widehat{T}$, there is nothing to prove, so assume that $y_1,\ldots, y_\ell$ are the children of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$. 
		For every $i \in [\ell]$, let \(t_i \in 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) be such that \(a_i \in \beta^0_x(t_i)\) and let \(t'_i \in 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) be such that \(b_i \in \beta^0_x(t'_i)\).
		Let \(P_i\) be the unique \(t_i\)-\(t'_i\)-path in \(T^0_x\).
		
		Recall that we denote by $O_i$ the onion consisting of the three edges between $a_i$ and $b_i$.

	\begin{claim}
		\label{claim:edge_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}
		If \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\), then \(\gamma^0_x(e)\) contains at least one of the auxiliary edges \(
	\ifx\relax O_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		This follows from \(O_i\) containing a cycle that visits \(a_i\) and \(b_i\), which  crosses \(e\) and thus has to be hit by the edge-guard \(\gamma^0_x(e)\).
	\end{claimproof}
	
	\begin{claim}
		\label{claim:onion_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}
		If \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi-\Set{t_i,t'_i}\), then \(\theta^0_x(t)\) contains at least one of the auxiliary edges \(
	\ifx\relax O_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}
                     
	\fi\).
	\end{claim}
	\begin{claimproof}
		This follows from \(O_i\) containing an onion with tip \(a_i\) and root \(b_i\), which crosses \(t\) and thus has to be hit by the onion-guard \(\theta^0_x(t)\).
	\end{claimproof}
		
		We define \(T_x \coloneqq T^0_x\).
		Then, for every \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) we define 
		\begin{equation*}
			\beta_x(t) \coloneqq \beta^0_x(t)-\Set{b_i\mid a_i \not\in \beta^0_x(t)} \cup \Set{b_i\mid a_i \in \beta^0_x(t).}
		\end{equation*}
		That is, we move each auxiliary vertex \(b_i\) to the bag containing \(a_i\).
		This increases the size of the bags at most by a factor of two.
		
		
		For every \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) we define
		\begin{equation*}
			\gamma_x(e) \coloneqq \gamma^0_x(e) \cup \Set{\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}\mid e\in
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi}.
		\end{equation*}
		The size of the edge-guard of $e$ grows by the number of paths \(P_i\) the edge $e$ lies on.
	By~\cref{claim:edge_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}, these are at most as many as the original edge-guard size.
	So, the edge-guards grow by at most a factor two.
		
		Similarly, for every \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) we define
		\begin{equation*}
			\theta_x(t) \coloneqq \theta^0_x(t) \cup \Set{\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}\mid t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi}.
	\end{equation*}
	Each onion-guard grows by one edge for every \(P_i\) its corresponding node $t$ in $T_x=T_x^0$ lies on.
	Thus, if we denote by $n_i$ and $n_e$, respectively, the number of paths $P_i$ that contain $t$ as an internal vertex, and as an endpoint, we have $|\theta_x(t)| \leqslant |\theta^0_x(t)| + n_i + n_e$. 
	If $t$ is an internal node of \(P_i\), by~\cref{claim:onion_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}, $\theta_x^0(t)$ already contains an edge from \(O_i\), thus $n_i \leqslant |\theta^0_x(t)|$.
	Note that $t$ can be only an endpoint of \(P_i\) if $\beta^0_x(t)$ contains \(a_i\) or \(b_i\). Therefore, $n_e \leqslant |\beta^0_x(t)|$.
	Thus, the new onion-guards are at most twice their original size plus the maximum bag-size, i.e.~three times the width of $\mathcal{T}^0_x$.
	
	It remains to show that both the new edge-guards and the new onion-guards are doing their job in hitting all crossing cycles or onions.
	Let \(e\in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) be an edge in the decomposition and \(C\) be a cycle of \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) crossing \(e\).
	If \(C\) is a cycle crossing \(e\) in \(\mathcal{T}^0_x\) as well, then it is hit by \(\gamma^0_x(e) \subseteq \gamma_x(e)\).
	If \(C\) does not cross \(e\) in \(\mathcal{T}^0_x\), then there is \(i \in [\ell]\) such that \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(C\) visits \(b_i\). Thus, $C$ contains the edge \(\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}\), which ensures that $C$ is hit by \(\gamma_x(e)\).
	
	For the onion-guards, let \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T^0_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T^0_x \right)}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}{( T^0_x )}}
                     
	\fi = 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\) and assume without loss of generality hat \(\theta^0_x(t)\) hits all in-onions of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}_x^0$.
	Let \(O\) be an in-onion of \(t\) in \(\mathcal{T}_x\) (thus $O$ is an onion immersion model in $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$).
	If \(O\) is also an in-onion of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}_x^0$, then it is hit by \(\theta^0_x(t) \subseteq \theta_x(t)\).
	If \(O\) only became an in-onion of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}_x$ due to moving the auxiliary vertices, then there is \(i \in [\ell]\) such that \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\) and \(b_i\) is the root or the tip of \(O\).
	In particular, $O$ must contain the edge $(a_i,b_i)$, and hence $O$ is hit by \(\mathchoice{\left( a_i,b_i \right)}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )}{( a_i,b_i )} \in \theta_x(t)\).
<!-- end claimproof-14 -->

\label{claim:edge_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}
		If \(e \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi\), then \(\gamma^0_x(e)\) contains at least one of the auxiliary edges \(
	\ifx\relax O_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}
                     
	\fi\).

<!-- claim-16: body_md -->
\label{claim:onion_guards_splitting_contain_aux_edge}
		If \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax P_i\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( P_i \right)}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}{( P_i )}}
                     
	\fi-\Set{t_i,t'_i}\), then \(\theta^0_x(t)\) contains at least one of the auxiliary edges \(
	\ifx\relax O_i\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( O_i \right)}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}{( O_i )}}
                     
	\fi\).
<!-- end claim-16 -->
<!-- claimproof-16: body_md -->
This follows from \(O_i\) containing an onion with tip \(a_i\) and root \(b_i\), which crosses \(t\) and thus has to be hit by the onion-guard \(\theta^0_x(t)\).
<!-- end claimproof-16 -->
<!-- claim-17: body_md -->
For every \(e = tt' \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		E
	\else
		\mathchoice{E\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{E\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\), the edge set \(\gamma(e)\) is indeed an edge-guard for \(e\), that is, it hits all cycles visiting both vertices from bags of \({T}^{t}_{e}\) and of \({T}^{t'}_{e}\).
<!-- end claim-17 -->
<!-- claimproof-17: body_md -->
Let \(e = tt'\in E(T)\). If $e$ is an outer edge, for some $(x, x') \in E(\widehat{T})$ such that $t \in V(T_x)$, and $t' \in V(T_{x'})$, we have \(\beta({T}^{t}_{e}) = \widehat{\beta}({{\widehat{T}}}^{x}_{e})\) and \(\beta({T}^{t'}_{e}) = \widehat{\beta}({{\widehat{T}}}^{x'}_{e})\), so the statement follows because $\gamma(e)=\widehat\gamma(x,x')$.
	
	Suppose now $e$ is an inner edge. Let $x$ be such that $t,t' \in V(T_x)$, and \(C\) be a cycle visiting vertices from the bags of both \({T}^{t}_{e}\) and of \({T}^{t'}_{e}\).
	
	If \(C\) visits a vertex that lies in \(\widehat{\beta}({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\), then it has to contain an edge from \(\widehat{\gamma}\mathchoice{\left( x',x \right)}{( x',x )}{( x',x )}{( x',x )}\), where \(x'\) is the parent of \(x\), by~\cref{item:tp:edgeguards}, so it is hit by \(\widehat{\gamma}\mathchoice{\left( x',x \right)}{( x',x )}{( x',x )}{( x',x )} \subseteq \gamma(e)\).
	
	                                                                                              
	
	So we can assume that $C$ does not visit vertices from \(\widehat{\beta}({T}^{\uparrow}_{x})\).
	                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               
	We consider a subgraph of $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$ induced by all edges of the form $\mathsf{origin}^{-1}_x(e)$, for every edge $e \in E(C)$ for which $\mathsf{origin}^{-1}_x(e)$ exists, and edges $(a_i,b_i)$ for every child $y_i$ of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$ such that $C$ intersects $\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y_i})$.
	This subgraph includes a cycle $C'$ that, if there exists $y_i$ such that $C$ intersects $\beta({T}^{\downarrow}_{y_i})$, contains the auxiliary edge $(a_i,b_i)$. If there is no such $y_i$, consider $C'=C$, as $C$ is a cycle in $D[\widehat\beta(x)]\subseteq \mathsf{tor}(D,x)$.
	
	As \(C'\) is a cycle in \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\) that crosses \(e \in E(T_x)\), it is hit by \(\gamma_x(e)\).
	If $C'$ is hit by an edge \((a_i,b_i) \in \gamma_x(e)\), then we have \(C \cap \widehat{\gamma}(x,y_i) \neq \emptyset\) and thus, \(C\) is hit by \(\widehat{\gamma}(x,y_i)\subseteq \gamma(e)\).
	Otherwise \(C\) is hit by \(\mathsf{origin}_{x}(\gamma_x(e) \cap 
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{origin}_{x}\relax
		\mathsf{dom}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{dom}\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}{\mathsf{dom}\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{origin}_{x} \right)}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}{( \mathsf{origin}_{x} )}}
                     
	\fi) \subseteq \gamma(e)\).
<!-- end claimproof-17 -->
<!-- claim-18: body_md -->
For every \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T \right)}{( T )}{( T )}{( T )}}
                     
	\fi\) the set \(\theta(t)\) hits all in-onions or all out-onions of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}$.
<!-- end claim-18 -->
<!-- claimproof-18: body_md -->
Let \(x \in V(\widehat{T})\) be such that \(t \in 
	\ifx\relax T_x\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( T_x \right)}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}{( T_x )}}
                     
	\fi\).
		Note that each onion immersion model for which either root or tip is outside of the bags of the subtree of $\widehat{T}$ rooted at \(x\), and the other one is inside, is hit by \(\widehat{\gamma}(x',x)\), where $x'$ is the parent of $x$ in $\widehat{T}$.
		So we only need to consider those onions that completely live in the subtree of $\widehat{T}$ rooted at \(x\).
		For these we make use of the Quasi-onion Lemma~(\cref{lem:quasi_onions}).
		
		Assume in the decomposition $\mathcal{T}_x$ of $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$, the onion-guard \(\theta_x(t)\) hits all out-onions of \(t\).
		We aim to show that then \(\theta(t)\) also hits all out-onions of \(t\) in $\mathcal{T}$.
		Let \(O\) be an out-onion of $t$ in $\mathcal{T}$ (so $O$ is an onion immersion model in $D$) and let \(P_1,P_2,P_3\) be the three trails forming it.
		Let \(F \coloneqq \Set{(a_i,b_i) \mid y_i \text{ is a child of } x}\).
		As the mapping $\mathsf{origin}_{x}$ is a bijection, passing $P_1,P_2,P_3$ through $\mathsf{origin}_{x}$ and augmenting them with the edges of $F$, if necessary, naturally yields an \((F,(X,Y))\)-quasi-onion \(O'\) in \(\mathsf{tor}(D,x)\), for any cut $(X,Y)$ such that the root is in $X$ and the tip is in $Y$.
		
		If the root of \(O\) lies within \(\beta(t)\), then consider the bipartition \((X,Y) \coloneqq (\beta(t), 
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{tor}(D,x)\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}
                     
	\fi-\beta(t))\).
		Otherwise, let \(t'\) be the neighbour of \(t\) such that the bags of the subtree \((T_x)^{tt'}_{t'}\) contain the root of \(O\), then consider the bipartition \((X,Y) \coloneqq (\beta_x((T_x)^{tt'}_{t'}),
	\ifx\relax \mathsf{tor}(D,x)\relax
		V
	\else
		\mathchoice{V\!\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}{V\mathchoice{\left( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) \right)}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}{( \mathsf{tor}(D,x) )}}
                     
	\fi-\beta_x((T_x)^{tt'}_{t'}))\). Note that no edge of $F$ crosses the cut $(X,Y)$, and hence $O'$ is an $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion.
		
		Now let \(O''\) be the minimal $(F,(X,Y))$-quasi-onion contained in the union of the paths of \(O'\) that has its root in $X$ and tip in $Y$.
		By~\cref{lem:quasi_onions}, \(O''\) is an onion and in particular, by our choice of \((X,Y)\), an out-onion of \(t\) in $\mathcal{T}_x$. Therefore, $O''$ is hit by \(\theta_x(t)\).
		
		If the intersection of \(O''\) and \(\theta_x(t)\) contains an edge $e$ that is not an auxiliary edge in $\mathsf{tor}(D,x)$, then $\mathsf{origin}_{x}(e)$ also lies in \(\theta(t)\) and hits \(O\).
		Otherwise, \(O\) is hit by the set \(\bigcup\Set{\widehat{\gamma}(x,c_i) \mid i\in [\ell] \text{ and }\theta_x(t)\cap E(O_i)\neq \emptyset}\), which was included in $\theta(t)$.
<!-- end claimproof-18 -->
<!-- conjecture-1: body_md -->
\label{con:cycdeg}
	There exists a function $
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi\colon \mathds{N}\to \mathds{N}$ such that for every $t\in \mathds{N}$, every simple digraph of minimum cycle-degree at least $
	\ifx\relax t\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{f}_{\ref{con:cycdeg}}}\mathchoice{\left( t \right)}{( t )}{( t )}{( t )}}
                     
	\fi$ contains $\vec{K}_t$ as an immersion.
<!-- end conjecture-1 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:single-out}
Let $c,k\geqslant 1$ be integers. There exists a function $f_c\colon\mathds{N} \to \mathds{N}$ with the following property: If in a digraph $D$ there exists a well-crossing pair $(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})$ with congestion $c$ rooted at $x$ with $|\mathcal{P}|= f_c(k)$ and $|\mathcal{Q}|= f_c(k)$, then in $D$ there exist an onion-immersion $\pi$ with root $x$, and a well-crossing pair $(\mathcal{P}^*,\mathcal{Q}^*)$ with congestion $c$ rooted at $x$ with $|\mathcal{P}^*|= k$, $|\mathcal{Q}^*|= k$ such that:
\begin{itemize}
\item $A(\mathcal{P}^*) \subseteq A(\mathcal{P})$, and $A(\mathcal{Q}^*) \subseteq A(\mathcal{Q})$;
\item all paths in $\mathcal{P}^*\cup\mathcal{Q}^*$ are arc-disjoint with $\pi$;
\item no arc in $A(\mathcal{P}^*)\cap A(\mathcal{Q}^*)$ has the sink of $\pi$ for tail.
\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- theorem-15: body_md -->
\label{thm:kst}
There exists a function $
	\ifx\relax \relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\!\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left(  \right)}{(  )}{(  )}{(  )}}
                     
	\fi: \mathds{N}\to (0,\infty)$ such that if a graph $G$ has at least $
	\ifx\relax q\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( q \right)}{( q )}{( q )}{( q )}}
                     
	\fi \cdot |V(G)|^{2-1/q}$ edges for some $q \in \mathbb{N}$, then it contains $K_{q,q}$ as a subgraph.
<!-- end theorem-15 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Define functions $g_c$ and $f_c$ by:
\begin{align*}g_c(k)&=\max\left\{8,\left\lceil\frac12\left(16\cdot 
	\ifx\relax ck +k\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( ck +k \right)}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( ck +k \right)}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( ck +k \right)}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( ck +k \right)}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}{( ck +k )}}
                     
	\fi\right)^{ck+k}\right\rceil\right\}, \\ f_c(k)&=\max\left\{3k(c+1),3\cdot\left\lceil\frac14(36 \cdot 
	\ifx\relax g_c(k)\relax
		\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\!\mathchoice{\left( g_c(k) \right)}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( g_c(k) \right)}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( g_c(k) \right)}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}}{\textcolor{green!50!black}{\mathsf{g}_{\ref{thm:kst}}}\mathchoice{\left( g_c(k) \right)}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}{( g_c(k) )}}
                     
	\fi)^{g_c(k)}\right\rceil\right\}.
\end{align*}
Fix $Q \in \mathcal{Q}$, and let $e$ be the $<_Q$-smallest arc of $Q$ such that the number of paths in $\mathcal{P}$ that have a common arc with the suffix of $Q$ that starts in $\mathsf{next}_Q(e)$ equals $|\mathcal{P}|/3$. 
                                                                             
Let $\bar{Q}$ be the suffix of $Q$ that starts in $e$.

\textbf{Case 1.} Suppose that $\mathcal{P}(\bar{Q})=\{P \in \mathcal{P} \mid A(P) \cap A(\bar{Q})\}$ contains at least two $\bar{Q}$-dangerous paths. 
Let $d$ be the $<_Q$-greatest dangerous crossing in $Q$ and let $P_2$ be the path such that $d$ is a $(P_2,Q)$-crossing. 
Then $d$ belongs to $\bar{Q}$.
Let $e'$ be the $<_Q$-greatest dangerous crossing in $A(Q)- A(P_2)$ and let $P_1\in\mathcal{P}(\bar{Q})-\{P_2\}$ be the path such that $e'$ is a $(P_1,Q)$-crossing (with possibly $e=e'$).
Let $d'$ be the $<_{P_2}$-smallest $(P_2,Q)$-crossing belonging to the suffix of $Q$ that starts with $\mathsf{next}_Q(e')$.
Clearly since $d$ is dangerous and belongs to $\bar{Q}$, $d'$ must be dangerous (with possibly $d=d'$). 

Let $y=
	\ifx\relax d'\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( d' \right)}{( d' )}{( d' )}{( d' )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( d' \right)}{( d' )}{( d' )}{( d' )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( d' \right)}{( d' )}{( d' )}{( d' )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( d' \right)}{( d' )}{( d' )}{( d' )}}
                     
	\fi$.
We consider the $(x,y)$-trails: one that goes along $P_1$ to $e'$ and then continues along $Q$ to $y$, and the other that goes along $P_2$, and the $(y,x)$-trail that goes along $Q$.
All of these are arc-disjoint.
Now the minimal $(x,y)$-trails/$(y,x)$-trail contained in the above (as $y$ may occur more than one on each of them) form an onion immersion model with root $x$ and tip $y$.

Let $\mathcal{Q}^\circ \subseteq \mathcal{Q}$ be the set of trails which are arc-disjoint both with the prefix of $P_1$ that ends with $e'$ and the prefix of $P_2$ that ends with $d'$ (so in particular $Q\notin \mathcal{Q}^\circ$).
Since $e'$ and $d'$ are both dangerous, $|\mathcal{Q}^\circ|> |\mathcal{Q}|-|\mathcal{Q}|/3-|\mathcal{Q}|/3=|\mathcal{Q}|/3\geqslant (c+1)k$.
Let us pick an arbitrary collection $\mathcal{P}^*\subseteq\mathcal{P}- \{P_1,P_2\}$ of $k$ trails that are arc-disjoint with the suffix of $Q$ that starts with $e'$.
Such a collection exists, as the number of trails in $\mathcal{P}$ that intersect the suffix of $Q$ that starts with $\mathsf{next}_Q(e)$ equals $|\mathcal{P}|/3$, yielding $\smash{\frac23}|\mathcal{P}|\geqslant 2(c+1)k$ candidate trails for $\mathcal{P}^*$.
Since each trail in such a collection has at most $c$ arcs whose tail is $y$ (as each vertex occurs at most $c$ times on each trail), there are at most $c|\mathcal{P}^*|=ck$ arcs in $A(\mathcal{Q}^\circ)\cap A(\mathcal{P}^*)$ whose tail is $y$. Let $\mathcal{Q}^*\subseteq \mathcal{Q}^\circ$ comprise any $n$ trails disjoint with these arcs.
Clearly, since $\mathcal{P}^* \subseteq \mathcal{P}$ and $\mathcal{Q}^* \subseteq \mathcal{Q}$, $(\mathcal{P}^*,\mathcal{Q}^*)$ is a well-crossing pair. Hence $\pi$, $\mathcal{P}^*$ and $\mathcal{Q}^*$ satisfy all the desired properties.

\textbf{Case 2.} Suppose that there is at most one $\bar{Q}$-dangerous path in $\mathcal{P}(\bar{Q})$. 
Let $\mathcal{S}$ be the set of those trails in $\mathcal{P}(\bar{Q})$ whose all crossings with $\bar{Q}$ are safe, and let $\bar{\mathcal{S}}$ be the set of trails from $\mathcal{S}$ trimmed so that they start at $x$, have no arcs in common with $\bar{Q}$ and are maximally long.
Since $|\mathcal{P}(\bar{Q})| = |\mathcal{P}|/3+1$ and there is at most one dangerous trail in $\mathcal{P}(\bar{Q})$, we derive $|\bar{\mathcal{S}}|\geqslant|\mathcal{P}|/3$. 

\begin{claim}[\cite{bozyk2022digraphs}]\label{cla:same-paths}
There exist subfamilies $\bar{\mathcal{S}}'\subseteq \bar{\mathcal{S}}$, $\mathcal{Q}'\subseteq \mathcal{Q}- \{Q\}$ such that $|\bar{\mathcal{S}}'|=g_c(k)$, $|\mathcal{Q}'|=g_c(k)$ and $(\bar{\mathcal{S}}',\mathcal{Q}')$ is well-crossing with congestion $c$ rooted at $x$.
\end{claim}

Consider the pair $(\bar{\mathcal{S}}',\mathcal{Q}')$ given by \cref{cla:same-paths} and
let $\mathcal{S}'$ be the set of paths in $\mathcal{S}$ from which $\bar{\mathcal{S}}'$ originates (i.e., the paths from $\bar{\mathcal{S}}'$ before trimming).
              
\begin{claim}[\cite{bozyk2022digraphs}]\label{cla:paths}
There exist 
\begin{itemize}
\item distinct $P_1,P_2 \in \mathcal{S}'$, 
\item a subfamily $\mathcal{P}^\circ$ of $\bar{\mathcal{S}}'$, and 
\item a subfamily $\mathcal{Q}^\circ$ of the set $\bar{\mathcal{Q}}'$ of trails from $\mathcal{Q}'$ trimmed so that they end at $x$, have no arcs in common with $P_1$ or $P_2$ and are longest possible, 
\end{itemize}
such that $|\mathcal{P}^\circ| = |\mathcal{Q}^\circ| = (c+1)k$ and $(\mathcal{P}^\circ,\mathcal{Q}^\circ)$ is well-crossing with congestion $c$ rooted at~$x$.\footnotemark                                                 
                                                                                                                                                                                                         
\end{claim}
                            
                                           
\footnotetext{The proof of \cref{cla:paths} in \cite{bozyk2022digraphs} uses the definition of $g_c$ for $c=1$, but the correctness of calculations for the more general case is straightforward to verify.}
                       
             

Consider $P_1$, $P_2$ and $(\mathcal{P}^\circ,\mathcal{Q}^\circ)$ as in the claim above and denote
$e_i=\min_{<_{P_i}}\{A(\bar{Q})\cap A(P_i)\}$ for $i=1,2$. 
Assume without loss of generality that $e_1<_Q e_2$, i.e., that the $<_{P_1}$-smallest $(P_1,\bar{Q})$-crossing is further from $x$ on $\bar{Q}$ than the $<_{P_2}$-smallest $(P_2,\bar{Q})$-crossing. Let $y=
	\ifx\relax e_2\relax
		\mathsf{tail}
	\else
		\mathchoice{\mathsf{tail}\!\mathchoice{\left( e_2 \right)}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e_2 \right)}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e_2 \right)}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}}{\mathsf{tail}\mathchoice{\left( e_2 \right)}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}{( e_2 )}}
                     
	\fi$.
By the choice of $e_1$ and $e_2$, we have two $(x,y)$-trails: the one that goes along $P_1$ to $e_1$ and then continues along $Q$ to $y$, and the one that is a prefix of $P_2$, and one $(y,x)$-trail that is a suffix of $Q$.
All of these are pairwise arc-disjoint.
Now the minimal $(x,y)$-trails/$(y,x)$-trail contained in the above form an onion-immersion with root $x$ and tip $y$. 

The last step (to ensure that $y$ will not be the tail of a crossing in the found well-crossing pair) is performed similarly as in the first case: we pick an arbitrary collection $\mathcal{P}^\ast$ of exactly $k$ trails among the $(c+1)n$ paths in $\mathcal{P}^\circ$, and select a subfamily $\mathcal{Q}^\ast$ consisting of $k$ trails which do not contain crossings of tail $y$ among the $(c+1)k$ trails in $\mathcal{Q}^\circ$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- claim-19: body_md -->
\label{cla:same-paths}
There exist subfamilies $\bar{\mathcal{S}}'\subseteq \bar{\mathcal{S}}$, $\mathcal{Q}'\subseteq \mathcal{Q}- \{Q\}$ such that $|\bar{\mathcal{S}}'|=g_c(k)$, $|\mathcal{Q}'|=g_c(k)$ and $(\bar{\mathcal{S}}',\mathcal{Q}')$ is well-crossing with congestion $c$ rooted at $x$.
<!-- end claim-19 -->
<!-- claim-20: body_md -->
\label{cla:paths}
There exist 
\begin{itemize}
\item distinct $P_1,P_2 \in \mathcal{S}'$, 
\item a subfamily $\mathcal{P}^\circ$ of $\bar{\mathcal{S}}'$, and 
\item a subfamily $\mathcal{Q}^\circ$ of the set $\bar{\mathcal{Q}}'$ of trails from $\mathcal{Q}'$ trimmed so that they end at $x$, have no arcs in common with $P_1$ or $P_2$ and are longest possible, 
\end{itemize}
such that $|\mathcal{P}^\circ| = |\mathcal{Q}^\circ| = (c+1)k$ and $(\mathcal{P}^\circ,\mathcal{Q}^\circ)$ is well-crossing with congestion $c$ rooted at~$x$.\footnotemark
<!-- end claim-20 -->
